Getting this backwards would round the wrong ends of every capsule in this view specifically. `dayConnectionInfoByTask` (a `Map<taskId, Map<dateString, DayConnectionInfo>>`) had to be placed *after* `days` is declared in the component body (not alongside its sibling `completionCountsByTask`/`streakChainsByTask` maps, which don't depend on `days`) since its own `useMemo` dependency array reads `days` directly — an earlier draft that placed it up with the other two maps hit a genuine "used before initialization" (temporal-dead-zone) error at that exact line, not just a lint warning.

Both screens' badges are positioned fully inside their cell's own bounds (no negative offsets) — `TaskCalendarScreen`'s cells have breathing room from their own `padding: 4`, but `DashboardCalendarView`'s `gridCell`s are a fixed, tightly-packed 32px with no gap between adjacent cells, so a badge overflowing past its own cell edge would visibly spill into a neighboring day's (or, vertically, a different task's) cell.

**Verification**: 4 new unit tests in `__tests__/utils/reports.test.ts` covering a closed multi-day streak (badge + correct start/end rounding), an isolated 1-day completion (both caps rounded, no badge), a still-live streak reaching into today (badge stays on the last real completion, not today), and the `days_per_week` fixture already used by `getDayStreakState`'s own tests (met/failed/current-week boundaries, plus the "completed day stranded in a failed period" 1-day-island case) — all passing on the first attempt against hand-derived expected values. Additionally cross-checked via the same throwaway-`tsx`-script-against-real-demo-data method used throughout this feature's development (see the tri-state day-classification entries above): ran `buildDayConnectionInfo` for all 9 demo tasks across a 90-day window, asserting `isRunStart`/`isRunEnd` never fire on a disconnected day and `showStreakBadge` never fires with `badgeValue < 2` — zero crashes, zero invariant violations, and the printed per-task connectivity string (`#`=completed, `-`=soft-skip, `.`=disconnected, `B`=badge) visually confirmed badges always land immediately after the last `#` before a break, across all three frequency types.

**Follow-up (2026-08-10): the calendar badges disagreed with the Streaks screen for `days_per_week`/`days_per_month` tasks — found and fixed via two changes, per explicit user direction after they asked how streak counts are calculated everywhere and whether the calendar matched the Streaks screen.** The root cause: `calculateQuotaStats` (streaks.ts, backs `task.stats.currentStreak`/`bestStreak` — TaskCard/TaskHeader/Dashboard's hero stats) and `getQuotaStreakChains` (reports.ts, backs `getRecentStreaks` — the Streaks screen) both use an "IncludingClose" rule: a quota period that fails its own quota but wasn't empty still gets its own completed days credited to the streak that's ending. But `getDayStreakState`'s quota branch — which the badge's *original* implementation counted days within — was a **third, independently-implemented** notion of quota connectivity with no such forgiveness (deliberately, per this section's own "Final design" history above: an empty day in a failed period is a hard miss, full stop, no exceptions). So a `days_per_week` task with two met weeks followed by a week that failed quota but had one completion showed a shorter (or absent) badge number than what the Streaks screen reported for the same underlying streak.

Two changes, addressing two different levels of the problem:

1. **Extracted the genuinely-shared primitive** that *should* have been common all along: a new `getQuotaPeriodInfo(date, unit, quota, completionCounts, requiredTimes): { start, end, count, met, firstCompleted, lastCompleted }` in `streaks.ts`, replacing three independent re-implementations of "walk this period's days, count how many qualify, compare to quota" — `calculateQuotaStats`'s own per-period loop, `getQuotaStreakChains`'s identical loop (reports.ts), and `getDayStreakState`'s single-period check (also reports.ts). Purely a duplication removal, zero behavior change (confirmed: the entire existing `streaks.test.ts`/`reports.test.ts` suite — the pre-existing `getDayStreakState`/`getTaskStreakChains`/`isConnectedDay` tests included — passed unmodified against the refactored code). This does *not* by itself resolve the badge mismatch: all three callers now agree on the atomic per-period *fact* (count, met), but the aggregation each does with that fact — IncludingClose forgiveness vs. severity-first no-forgiveness — is a deliberate, pre-existing difference in what question each is answering, not a bug to unify away.
2. **The badge's value now comes directly from the matching `StreakChain`'s own `.length`**, read straight out of the same `chains` array `getRecentStreaks`/the Streaks screen already consume, instead of being tallied locally by counting completed days within `buildDayConnectionInfo`'s own connected-run detection. Concretely: `buildDayConnectionInfo` no longer does a forward pass tracking "last completed index in the current run" at all — it iterates `chains` directly and places one badge at each chain's own `endDate`, valued at `chain.length` (skipping `length <= 1`, mirroring `getRecentStreaks`' own filter). This guarantees the badge is *always* the same number the Streaks screen would show for that chain — at the cost of an accepted visual quirk: in the specific "failed-but-not-empty period" scenario, the badge can now land on (or name a length past) a day this same file's own per-day connectivity still renders as a small, disconnected island, since the chain's IncludingClose span can reach further than what `getDayStreakState` marks "connected". This is a deliberate tradeoff (numeric correctness over local visual self-consistency), not an oversight.

Rewrote the `days_per_week` describe-block test to match: the day that used to get the badge (the visual run's own last completion) no longer does, since it isn't the chain's actual `endDate`; the previously-unbadged isolated island now correctly gets the chain's real length. Added a second, more general regression test (`every badge's value exactly equals its chain's own length, for every chain a task has`) across `daily`/`days_per_month` fixtures with multiple, gapped chains, asserting badge count and value against `getTaskStreakChains`'s own output directly — a structural guard against this exact class of drift recurring. Re-verified against the real demo dataset via the same throwaway-script method: summed the per-task badge counts across all 9 tasks (22 total) and confirmed it exactly equals `getRecentStreaks`' own total streak count (22) for the same task set, plus a per-badge cross-check that every badge's `(date, value)` pair matches some chain's own `(endDate, length)` exactly — zero crashes, zero mismatches.

**Follow-up (2026-08-10): the capsule now bridges all the way to a chain's real endDate — where the badge sits — instead of stopping at a hard-miss wall, per explicit user direction ("I do want the connectivity rendering to reach the last completed day, as that is conceptually where the streak ends").** The prior fix made the badge *number* correct, but left a real visual gap: `getDayStreakState`'s per-day quota classification is deliberately strict (an empty day inside a failed-but-not-empty period is still a hard miss, no exceptions — that's the approved, tested design), so `buildDayConnectionInfo`'s run detection (built on `isConnectedDay`, which defers to that same strict classification) stopped the visual capsule right at the failing period's edge — stranding the chain's own credited tail (e.g. Jul 27 in the running example) as its own disconnected 1-day island, even though its badge correctly read "3".

Added `isChainConnectedDay` (reports.ts, module-private) — a strict superset of `isConnectedDay` used *only* by `buildDayConnectionInfo`, never by `TaskCard.tsx`'s own Home-screen connector stubs (confirmed via a full-repo grep that `isConnectedDay` has exactly one other call site, `TaskCard.tsx`'s `hasLeftConnector`/`hasRightConnector`, which must keep its own simpler, unrelated treatment untouched — this feature is still scoped to Task Details → Calendar and Dashboard → Timeline only). It adds one more "connected" case on top of `isConnectedDay`'s own result: chain-span membership (`chains.some(chain => day >= chain.startDate && day <= chain.endDate)`) — since `getQuotaStreakChains`' "IncludingClose" rule already means a chain's span can reach through a failed period's own empty days to its last real completion, that span is now the authoritative answer for how far the *capsule* (not the per-day mark) should visually reach. `buildDayConnectionInfo`'s `isRunStart`/`isRunEnd` now check neighbors via `isChainConnectedDay` instead of `isConnectedDay`.

**A second, distinct bug surfaced immediately by the same demo-data method used throughout this feature**, not caught by hand-written test fixtures: two *entirely separate* chains landing on chronologically adjacent calendar days with zero gap between them (a failed period's own last credited completion falling the day immediately before the next period's own first completion, if that next period goes on to meet its quota) were merging into one visual run — both days individually read "connected" (each is a real completion), so plain day-to-day adjacency saw no reason to break. Fixed with `chainOf`/`isBridgedNeighbor` (reports.ts): two connected neighbors only bridge into the same run when they aren't known to belong to two *different* chains (a day with no explicit chain of its own — e.g. a quota period's pending tail before its own first completion — never forces a break on its own, since it has no competing identity to conflict with). This is what lets each closed streak's own capsule still end exactly at its own chain's real boundary even when it happens to sit right next to another one.

Verified via a new pure-unit test (`__tests__/utils/reports.test.ts`) reproducing this exact adjacent-chains scenario (`getTaskStreakChains` confirmed to produce 2 distinct chains, lengths 5 and 3; the boundary day pair asserted `isRunEnd`/`isRunStart` correctly split rather than bridged) plus an updated version of the bridging test (now also asserting the interior hard-miss day, e.g. Jul 26, reads `isConnectedSelf: true` for capsule purposes while `getDayStreakState` — unaffected — still classifies it `'hardMiss'`, and that the bridge doesn't overreach past the chain's own actual endDate). Re-ran the same throwaway-script-against-demo-data check as before (all 9 tasks, cross-checking every badge's `(date, value)` against `chains`' own `(endDate, length)`, *and* that every badge sits at its run's own `isRunEnd`) — this caught 3 real instances of the adjacent-chains bug (Meal Prep, Deep Clean, Meditate) before the `chainOf`/`isBridgedNeighbor` fix, and one false-positive-looking case (Deep Clean's own still-live streak, badge correctly staying on its last real completion — Aug 7 — while the capsule legitimately keeps bridging into today, Aug 10, since `task.stats.currentStreak` exactly matches that chain's length) that turned out to be the same already-approved "badge stays on the last real completion of an ongoing streak" behavior the daily test already covers, not a new bug — the verification script's own assertion was refined to account for it. Final result: 0 crashes, 0 mismatches across all 9 real demo tasks.

**Follow-up (2026-08-10): `StreakCountBadge` restyled to match `TaskCard`'s own home-tile streak indicator, per explicit user direction ("make more like a small pill with the same styling as the home task tile streak indicator, but no flame effect").** It started as a plain filled circle (just the number, a white border for contrast). Rewrote it to mirror `TaskCard`'s `streakBubble`/`streakText` styles exactly: a `borderRadius: 999` pill, `fire` icon + number side by side (`flexDirection: 'row'`), white icon/text, the same subtle drop shadow (`shadowOpacity: 0.1`, `shadowRadius: 2`, `elevation: 2`) — no border. "No flame effect" means no *animation*: this badge stays a static label (no `useFireCelebration` pop/particle burst) — the `fire` icon itself is just the same static glyph the rest of the app already uses as generic streak iconography (e.g. `CardStats`'s own `streakBadgeStyle?.icon ?? 'fire'` fallback), not a re-introduction of the celebration effect.

One deliberate divergence from the home tile: `color` stays the **task's own color** (matching the connector capsule this badge sits on), not a streak-status color from `getStreakBadgeStyle` (red/orange/gray for up-to-date/expiring/expired) — this badge labels a specific historical run's length, which isn't the same thing as the task's *current* status, so status-coloring it would be misleading (e.g. a long-since-expired chain would render in "success red").

The single `size` prop (previously a raw pixel diameter for the circle) became `iconSize` (default 11), with padding/gap/font size all derived from it proportionally (matching `streakBubble`'s own ratios) rather than passed independently — keeps the two call sites down to one number each. `TaskCalendarScreen.tsx` (larger, padded day cells) uses `iconSize={11}`; `DashboardCalendarView.tsx` (a tightly-packed, gapless 32px `gridCell`) uses a smaller `iconSize={8}` to fit without spilling into a neighboring cell. Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` only — this is a pure visual/styling change with no logic touched, so no new test coverage was needed; worth a look on-device to confirm the pill fits cleanly in both grids, especially the tighter Dashboard Timeline cells.

**Follow-up (2026-08-10), two more tweaks per explicit user direction: tighter padding, and a fixed streak-red instead of the task's own color.** `paddingHorizontal`/`paddingVertical`/`gap`'s ratios against `iconSize` were all reduced (0.55/0.3/0.3 → 0.35/0.15/0.15) for a visibly smaller, snugger pill — icon and text sizes themselves are unchanged, only the space around them shrank. Separately, the badge's `color` prop was removed entirely: it now always renders in `#FF6B6B` (a local `STREAK_RED` constant), the same red `getStreakBadgeStyle` uses for an active/"up_to_date" streak on `TaskCard`'s own home-tile badge — reversing the earlier "use the task's own color, since this labels a historical run rather than current status" reasoning, per the user's explicit preference for the streak-red regardless. `#FF6B6B` is hardcoded directly (not pulled from a shared constant) matching how this exact literal is already independently repeated at its other call sites throughout the codebase (`TaskCard.tsx`, `streaks.ts`, `DashboardStatsView.tsx`, etc.) rather than centralized. Both call sites (`TaskCalendarScreen.tsx`/`DashboardCalendarView.tsx`) simplified accordingly — no `color` prop passed anymore.

**Follow-up (2026-08-10): the connector went from a full-cell semi-transparent wash toward a track, per explicit user direction.** Both screens' `connectorBand` style changed from an absolute-fill (`top/left/right/bottom: 0`) rectangle to a vertically-centered bar: `position: 'absolute', left: 0, right: 0, top: '50%'`, with a fixed `height`/`marginTop: -height/2` pair centering it, instead of stretching to the cell's full height. Opacity bumped 0.22 → 0.4 to compensate for the smaller painted area. All of the connectivity/rounding/bridging logic from the earlier work is completely unchanged — only the shape of what gets drawn (a full rectangle vs. a centered bar) changed; `connectorRoundLeft`/`connectorRoundRight`'s `borderRadius: 999` still produces a true rounded cap regardless of the bar's height.

**First-pass thickness was wrong, corrected same day per direct follow-up ("I mean the height of the track should be at least the height of the X icon, so it doesn't fully envelope the fill circles but it still quite thick").** The initial cut set `CONNECTOR_LINE_THICKNESS = 3`, matching `MissedDayMark`'s *stroke width* (2.5) — reading as a near-hairline, not the "thick" track the user actually wanted. Corrected to `CONNECTOR_LINE_THICKNESS = 16`, matching the `size` `MissedDayMark` is actually rendered at in both screens (its overall glyph box, not its stroke) — thick enough to read as a real track, while still shorter than the completed-day dot's own diameter in both grids (`dayContent` in `TaskCalendarScreen`, `gridDot` — `GRID_CELL_SIZE - 8 = 24` — in `DashboardCalendarView`), so a connected dot still visibly pokes out above/below the track rather than looking enveloped by it, per the explicit ask.

**Follow-up (2026-08-10): the track wasn't actually vertically centered on `TaskCalendarScreen`'s Month view, per direct user report.** Root cause: `connectorBand` positioned itself via `top: '50%'` + `marginTop: -CONNECTOR_LINE_THICKNESS / 2` against `day`, a `flex:1, aspectRatio:1, padding:4` parent — percentage-based `top` combined with a negative `marginTop` on an absolutely positioned view is a known soft spot in React Native's (Yoga) layout engine, particularly against a parent whose size comes from `aspectRatio` rather than an explicit height, and it wasn't resolving to the parent's true vertical center. Replaced with a more robust pattern: a full-sized absolutely-positioned wrapper (`connectorBandWrap`, `top/left/right/bottom: 0`) that centers its one child via plain flex (`justifyContent: 'center'`, the parent `View`'s default `flexDirection: 'column'` making that the vertical axis) instead of a percentage offset — `connectorBand` itself now only carries `height`/`opacity`, no positioning. Applied to both `TaskCalendarScreen.tsx` (where the bug was reported) and `DashboardCalendarView.tsx` (not reported broken, but sharing the exact same percentage-positioning pattern against its own padding-free but still aspectRatio-adjacent `gridCell`, so fixed proactively for consistency rather than leaving a latent duplicate of the same bug). Flex-based centering doesn't depend on the parent's size being a percentage-resolvable "definite" value the way `top: '50%'` does, so it's reliable regardless of how the parent's own height was derived.

**Follow-up (2026-08-10): `TaskCalendarScreen`'s own track thickened further, per explicit user direction — Dashboard's Timeline track deliberately left alone.** `CONNECTOR_LINE_THICKNESS` 16 → 22 in `TaskCalendarScreen.tsx` only; `DashboardCalendarView.tsx` keeps its own separate `CONNECTOR_LINE_THICKNESS = 16` unchanged (each screen has always had its own independent constant, not a shared one, so this was a one-line, single-file change with no risk of accidentally affecting the Timeline grid).

**Follow-up (2026-08-10): the Timeline's own track thinned to roughly half, per explicit user direction ("about 50% as thick").** `DashboardCalendarView.tsx`'s `CONNECTOR_LINE_THICKNESS` 16 → 8 — `TaskCalendarScreen.tsx`'s separate `CONNECTOR_LINE_THICKNESS = 22` constant is untouched, same "each screen owns its own value" reasoning as the entry above. Reads more like a thin connecting thread than a track at this size; a connected dot's diameter (`GRID_CELL_SIZE - 8 = 24`) still comfortably pokes out well above/below it.

**Follow-up (2026-08-10): the track no longer leaves a little rounded stump poking past a run's first/last day, per explicit user direction ("if there's a streak like this: O---O---O ... the first circle should be O-, the middle circle should be -O-, and the last circle should be -O").** Previously, a run boundary got a full-width track with a `borderRadius: 999` rounded cap flush against the cell's outer edge — visually a pill-shaped stub extending past the boundary day itself, not stopping at it. Replaced the rounding approach with a half-width one: on whichever side is a run's real start/end, the track now only occupies the *half* of the cell reaching toward the still-connected neighbor — `width: '50%'` plus `alignSelf: 'flex-start'` or `'flex-end'` (overriding `connectorBandWrap`'s default `alignItems: 'stretch'`, which is what gave the track its full width in the first place) — so it terminates exactly at the cell's own center: tucked behind a completed day's own dot, or ending cleanly in empty space for a connected-but-not-completed boundary day (e.g. a still-live streak's own "today" cell). A day connected on *both* sides keeps the full-width default (no override needed); an isolated single-day "run" (both `isRunStart` and `isRunEnd` true) now renders no track at all, guarded by a new `!(isRunStart && isRunEnd)` check at the render site — the two half-styles would otherwise both apply and fight over the same `alignSelf` property, rather than correctly canceling out to zero width.

The two new styles, `connectorHalfLeft`/`connectorHalfRight`, replace the now-deleted `connectorRoundLeft`/`connectorRoundRight` in both screens, reusing the exact same trigger conditions each screen already had (including `DashboardCalendarView`'s reversed left/right mapping for its newest-first rendering order) — only the *effect* of the style changed (a width/alignment override instead of corner rounding), not which boolean applies which side.

**Follow-up (2026-08-10), same day: the half-width track's own terminus is rounded again, per explicit user direction ("if an empty track ends on a day, it should be rounded").** Dropping the full-width rounded cap in the fix above also removed rounding from the newly-added center-facing edge entirely, leaving a flat cut there — invisible on a completed day (the dot covers it) but an abrupt hard edge on a connected-but-empty boundary day (e.g. a still-live streak's own "today" cell, or a quota period's pending tail), which has nothing to hide it. Added `borderRadius: 999` back, but only on the *center-facing* side of each half (`connectorHalfLeft` rounds its right edge, `connectorHalfRight` rounds its left edge) — the outer edge of each half, which keeps touching the next cell's own track to stay seamless across a run, stays flat. Applying it unconditionally (not gated on whether the day happens to be completed) was simplest and costs nothing: it's simply invisible wherever a dot already covers it.

**Follow-up (2026-08-10), same day: the rounded terminus' own curve wasn't actually centered on the day/dot, per explicit user direction ("the rounding should have its center around the day/circle center... the edge of the line overshoots based on the radius of the track end cap").** A corner-radius rounds a box's *corner*, not its edge as a whole — for `connectorHalfLeft`/`Right`'s half-width box (ending exactly at the cell's true center), the resulting arc's own geometric center sits inset by the radius *from* that center point, not *on* it. Visually this reads as the rounded end being tucked off to one side of the dot rather than centered under it, especially at this track's thickness (22px on `TaskCalendarScreen`, where the radius/inset is a meaningfully large 11px).

Rather than trying to reposition the half-width box itself to compensate (which would need the box to extend a fixed pixel amount past a percentage-based edge — not reliably expressible in a single RN style value without either CSS `calc()`, of uncertain support on this RN/Expo version, or per-cell pixel measurement via `onLayout`, extra complexity for a small polish item), added a **second, separate piece**: a small fixed-size circle (`connectorCap`, `width`/`height`/`borderRadius` all `CONNECTOR_LINE_THICKNESS`-based) centered in the cell **both ways** via the same reliable flex-centering technique already used for the track's own vertical centering (`connectorCapWrap`: `justifyContent: 'center', alignItems: 'center'` on a full-size absolute wrapper) — not a percentage position, which is exactly the class of bug the vertical-centering fix earlier today was about. Because this cap is centered by its own dedicated wrapper rather than being one edge of a larger box, its *center point* — not just an edge — lands exactly on the cell's/dot's own center, and its curve naturally straddles that point symmetrically in both directions (extending the radius' worth past center into the technically-unconnected half, by design — that's what "centered on the dot" requires, and it stays hidden behind the dot on a completed day, or reads as a small clean rounded knob on an empty boundary day).

Rendered only at a genuine run boundary (`isRunStart !== isRunEnd`, i.e., exactly one true — an isolated single-day run, both true, still gets no connector pieces at all) as a sibling of the straight track, both wrapped in one new `connectorGroup` (`opacity: 0.4` moved here from the individual `connectorBand`/`connectorCap` styles) so the two pieces' overlap composites as a single flat shape instead of double-darkening where a semi-transparent bar and a semi-transparent circle would otherwise stack. `connectorHalfLeft`/`connectorHalfRight` dropped their own `borderRadius` (now flat rectangles) — the cap alone provides the rounding, and it's real either way, since a corner-radius' curve on a straight-edged box could never have been rounded around a fixed point the box's own bounds weren't reaching, no matter the radius value chosen.

**Reverted the very same day, per direct user pushback ("The way you did it is causing overlapping semi-transparent shapes. Not good.")** — the separate-cap approach above did exactly what its own doc comment worried about: two semi-transparent shapes (bar + cap) visibly overlapping instead of compositing cleanly as one. Replaced with a single-shape fix instead, going back to rounding the track itself but correcting *where* its edge sits, per the user's own diagnosis ("the edge of the line overshoots based on the radius of the track end cap... should NOT be right at the center — it should be one track height/radius past the center").

The insight: a corner-radius' curve center sits inset *by the radius* from the box's own edge — so for the arc's center to land exactly on the cell's true center, the box's edge itself needs to sit one radius *past* that center, not stop there. Reworked `connectorBandWrap` from a single stretched child into a **row of flex children** (`flexDirection: 'row', alignItems: 'center'` — replacing the previous column+`justifyContent: 'center'`): on a boundary side, an invisible spacer sibling (`flexBasis: 0, flexGrow: 1`) sits alongside the track (`flexBasis: CONNECTOR_LINE_THICKNESS, flexGrow: 1`) — since `flexBasis: CONNECTOR_LINE_THICKNESS` equals exactly *2×* the corner radius `borderRadius: 999` clamps to (radius clamps to half the track's own height), splitting the remaining row space evenly between two equal-`flexGrow` siblings whose basis differs by exactly `2×radius` works out mathematically to: the track gets `50% + radius` of the row, the spacer gets `50% - radius` — so rounding the track's far corner (inset by `radius` from *its own* edge, which now sits a radius past center) lands the arc's center precisely at the cell's true center. Deliberately **not** a percentage-width + negative-margin combination (`width: '50%', marginRight: -radius` would express the same intent) — that's the same *class* of technique that caused the earlier vertical-centering bug, so despite the horizontal axis potentially behaving differently, it wasn't worth the risk of a third centering bug in the same feature. Plain `flexBasis`/`flexGrow` splitting is a core, long-standing flexbox mechanism with no equivalent history of trouble in this codebase.

`connectorGroup`/`connectorCapWrap`/`connectorCap` were deleted entirely (no longer needed — single shape again); `opacity: 0.4` moved back onto `connectorBand` itself, the same one shape it was on before the cap experiment. Re-verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (115/115 passing) — this is a pure layout/visual change with no logic touched, so no new test coverage was needed, but worth a careful on-device look given this is the third layout iteration on this same connector in one day.

**Simplified considerably the very same day, per a genuine reframing from the user: "we don't care about the rounding in this case at all... it should ONLY apply to streak start/end days that are COMPLETED, so it covers the track. The track can just cleanly break in the middle. We only need rounding on the streak start/end days that are pass days... and in those cases they should just use the full track length."** This cuts the problem in half instead of trying to solve one unified shape for both cases: the whole "arc must be centered exactly on the dot" complexity (the flex-overshoot fix immediately above) only matters when there's *no dot* to hide the track's terminus behind in the first place — a completed boundary day's dot already covers the cut regardless of whether it's rounded, flat, or anything else, so there was never anything to solve there.

Reverted the flex-based overshoot machinery (`connectorBandWrap` back to a single stretched child, `justifyContent: 'center'`, no `flexDirection: 'row'`/spacer/`flexBasis` math) and split the boundary-day styling on `isCompleted` (already in scope at each render site from the existing dot/mark logic, no new data needed):
- **Completed boundary day**: `connectorHalfLeft`/`connectorHalfRight` — back to the simple `width: '50%'` + `alignSelf` pair from several iterations ago, stopping flat at the cell's center, no rounding at all (nothing to gain from it — invisible behind the dot either way).
- **Pass (connected, not completed) boundary day**: `connectorRoundLeft`/`connectorRoundRight` — back to the *original*, very first version of this feature: the track keeps its full length (both edges reaching the cell's true bounds) with `borderRadius: 999` on the boundary side, since there's no dot here to create a "stump" — a rounded cap flush with the cell's actual edge is exactly the clean look this case needs, no centering math required at all.

Net effect: what looked like an increasingly finicky geometry problem (three straight iterations trying to get one shape's rounding right for both cases at once) turned out to not be one problem — `isCompleted` decides which of two already-solved, simple styles applies, and neither needs anything more sophisticated than what existed several steps back in this same conversation.

**Follow-up (2026-08-10): today's own connector now respects `'expiring'` status, not just whether a streak merely exists — plus a matching gray clock icon on the calendar, per explicit user direction.** The user noticed the track showed a pass-through on today even when today was *required* to keep the streak going, and proposed a cleaner mental model: the track is really answering "what happens if I do nothing today" — present means safe (a free pass), absent means the streak dies without action.

The gap: today's connectivity checked `task.stats.currentStreak > 0`, but that's true for **both** of the app's "streak still alive" statuses — `'up_to_date'` *and* `'expiring'`. Those mean opposite things for "is today required": `'up_to_date'` means today's either not due or already covered with slack (nothing rides on it); `'expiring'` means today (or, for a quota task, every remaining day in the period) is the actual make-or-break moment. The old check couldn't tell these apart, so an `'expiring'` today got the same reassuring connector as a genuinely safe one.

Fixed in `reports.ts`: `isConnectedDay`'s today-branch changed from `(task.stats?.currentStreak ?? 0) > 0` to `task.stats?.streakStatus === 'up_to_date'` — an expiring today no longer gets a "free pass" connector. This is a **shared, single-source fix**: `isChainConnectedDay` (the capsule/badge-facing superset) previously duplicated all of `isConnectedDay`'s own future/completed/today/`getDayStreakState`-fallback logic a second time rather than calling it — refactored to build directly on `isConnectedDay` (`if (isConnectedDay(...)) return true`, then its own extra chain-span-membership check on top) so both functions share the exact same "today" rule with nothing left to keep in sync by hand — the kind of duplication-removal opportunity the shared `getQuotaPeriodInfo` primitive addressed earlier for a different piece of this same engine. Confirmed behaviorally identical for every case *except* the intended one: the full existing test suite (due-day chain membership, quota pending-tail, hard-miss/soft-miss classification) passed unmodified against the refactor.

Since this genuinely changes what "connected" means for today (not just internal plumbing), it required real test updates, not just new coverage: the existing `isConnectedDay` "today" test and one `buildDayConnectionInfo` scenario were both built around a fixture that's now `'expiring'` rather than `'up_to_date'` — rewrote both to assert the new behavior directly (an expiring today is no longer connected; the badge correctly lands on the prior day instead) and added two new scenarios to keep the *positive* case covered: a `specific_days_of_week` fixture where today genuinely is `'up_to_date'` (non-due, nothing riding on it) still correctly shows a connector reaching into it, and the badge still correctly stays on the actual last completion rather than moving to today. Re-checked against the real demo dataset too: none of the 9 tasks happen to be `'expiring'` as of today's actual date (most have gone `'expired'` simply because a day already passed with no new completion added to the static demo file, unrelated to this change), so the invariant checks (an expiring task must never show today connected; an up-to-date task with a live streak must show today connected) didn't find a naturally-occurring `'expiring'` case to exercise, but ran clean with zero crashes and zero violations for what the dataset does have — the fake-timer unit tests are what actually pin the `'expiring'` scenario directly, and those passed.

**The matching visual, first pass**: on `TaskCalendarScreen`'s Month view (the only calendar surface that renders day *numbers* — `DashboardCalendarView`'s Timeline grid and `TaskCard`'s own flipped face don't, so neither applies here), a new `isExpiringToday` check (`isToday && !isCompleted && task.stats?.streakStatus === 'expiring'`) replaced the day number with a gray (`colors.textTertiary`) `clock-outline` icon. `stateLabel` (the day cell's spoken accessibility state) gained a matching case ("Action needed today", or "`N` of `M` completed, action needed today" for a partial day) ahead of the existing `isPartial`/`isMissed`/`isSkipped` cases.

**Follow-up (2026-08-10), split into two different treatments per screen, per explicit user direction: "On the Task Detail Calendar I want the expiration to be a badge (using expiration coloring) instead of replacing the number. On the Dashboard Timeline I want a gray expiration icon to replace the day number on that day."**

- **`TaskCalendarScreen.tsx`**: reverted the day-number replacement entirely — the number is always visible again, exactly as before this whole feature. In its place, a small corner badge (`expiringBadge`, 16×16, `borderRadius: 8`, matching `StreakCountBadge`'s shadow/shape language) shows the `clock-outline` icon in white on `#FFA726` — the exact orange `getStreakBadgeStyle` already uses for an `'expiring'` task elsewhere (the front-face badge, `CardStats`), rather than the quieter gray the first pass used. Positioned via the same `streakBadgePosition` (top-right) the streak-count badge already uses — the two are provably mutually exclusive (the streak badge only ever lands on a *completed* day; this one only ever on an *incomplete* today), so they never compete for the same corner.
- **`DashboardCalendarView.tsx`**: this grid has no day numbers to badge in the first place (only the shared date-column header above all tasks, not a per-task-per-day number) — so it keeps the *original* "icon replaces content" treatment instead, gray and inside the dot itself, the same place `MissedDayMark` would otherwise render. A new `isExpiringToday` (`dateString === todayStr && !isCompleted && task.stats?.streakStatus === 'expiring'`) takes priority inside `gridDot`, over both the partial-progress pie and the hard-miss X (mutually exclusive with both, the same way those two already are with each other) — `gridDot` itself goes transparent (`gridDotEmpty`, the same treatment `isMissed`/`isSkipped` already get) so the gray `clock-outline` (`colors.textTertiary`, `size={16}`, in the existing `missedMark` wrapper) shows cleanly with nothing behind it.

Both changes are pure UI/styling — no `reports.ts` logic touched again here — so the existing 116 tests passed unmodified.

**Follow-up (2026-08-10): the Timeline's clock icon sized up twice, per two rounds of explicit user direction — first to `20` (it looked lighter/smaller than `MissedDayMark`'s X despite sharing the same numeric `size`), then further to `22` ("close to the size of the completion circle").** The two marks aren't rendered the same way — `MissedDayMark` hand-draws its stroke lines directly to nearly fill its own `size × size` box, while `clock-outline` is an icon-font glyph, which carries real internal padding within its own character box that a hand-drawn mark doesn't — so matching the numeric size parameter alone doesn't match the visual weight/footprint between the two techniques. Final size (`22`) is deliberately just under `gridDot`'s own diameter (`GRID_CELL_SIZE - 8 = 24`) rather than exactly equal to it, leaving a hair of breathing room against the circular dot's own bounds instead of touching edge-to-edge.

**`TaskCalendarScreen`'s Month-view track now rounds off at week/month wraps too, not just true streak boundaries (2026-08-10), per explicit user direction ("rounded at the ends everywhere they stop, ex at the start/end of month or week").** The existing rounding (`connectorRoundLeft`/`Right` for a pass day, the half-width `connectorHalfLeft`/`Right` cut hidden behind a completed day's dot) was driven purely by `isRunStart`/`isRunEnd` — whether the *streak itself* starts/ends there, checked against the true chronological neighbor regardless of whether that neighbor is actually rendered nearby (per `buildDayConnectionInfo`'s own doc comment, deliberately so a streak continuing into an unrendered adjacent month doesn't wrongly cap). But a horizontal track can only ever visually bridge two cells sitting side by side in the *same* row — a streak that data-wise continues past a week's Saturday into the next row's Sunday (a different row, not adjacent on screen) or past the month's own first/last day has nowhere to visually go from a given cell, even when `isRunStart`/`isRunEnd` correctly say the streak doesn't end there. Previously this rendered as a plain full-width, flat-cut track dangling at the row/month edge with nothing to visually connect to.

Fixed by computing two new per-cell flags, `visualRunStart`/`visualRunEnd` — true whenever *either* the underlying connection data says the run stops there, *or* there's simply no rendered `'day'`-type cell adjacent within the same row (`week[colIndex - 1]`/`week[colIndex + 1]`, `week.map` changed to also destructure `colIndex` for this) — and using these in place of the raw `connection.isRunStart`/`isRunEnd` for the rounding/half-width style conditions. This one change covers both cases the user named without needing separate week-wrap vs. month-wrap logic: a week-row edge (Saturday/Sunday) has no same-row neighbor by construction regardless of month position, and a month's first/last day either sits at a row edge too or has an `'empty'`-type leading/trailing blank cell beside it (already distinguished from a real `'day'` cell) — both collapse to the same "no `'day'`-type neighbor in this row" check. The render-time visibility guard (`connection?.isConnectedSelf && !(connection.isRunStart && connection.isRunEnd)`, whether to show *any* track at all) is deliberately left keyed on the raw data flags, unchanged — a truly isolated single connected day (not part of a longer run at all) still correctly shows no track; only the *shape* of an already-showing track changed.

One edge case handled explicitly: a *completed* day with no visual neighbor on *either* side (`visualRunStart && visualRunEnd` both true — e.g. a month starting on Saturday, so day 1 sits alone in a mostly-blank first row) previously would have hit both `connectorHalfRight` and `connectorHalfLeft` simultaneously, which conflict on `alignSelf` and would silently resolve to whichever came last in the style array rather than anything intentional. Added an `isolatedBothSides` guard suppressing *both* halves in that specific case (nothing to poke a stub toward on either side, so nothing renders beyond the dot itself) — the equivalent *pass*-day case needs no such guard, since `connectorRoundLeft`/`Right` are independent, non-conflicting corner-radius properties that simply combine into a small fully-rounded pill when both apply, which is exactly the intended look for an isolated pass day.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (127/127, unaffected — pure rendering-condition logic, no changes to any tested `utils/` module); worth an on-device look specifically at a streak that crosses a week-row boundary mid-run and a task whose active streak spans a visible month boundary, to confirm the rounding reads as a clean cap rather than looking like the streak actually broke there.

## Dashboard Timeline: weekday labels, week/month separators, a bigger month/year label (2026-08-10)

Per explicit user direction, four related changes to `DashboardCalendarView.tsx`'s main Timeline grid:

**1. Weekday letters + a top-and-bottom axis.** Each column's header (`dateAxis`, previously a month-abbreviation row + a day-number row) is now a weekday letter (`format(day, 'EEEEE')` — S/M/T/W/T/F/S) stacked above the day-of-month number, in a single `gridAxisCell` (`GRID_AXIS_HEIGHT = GRID_WEEKDAY_HEIGHT + GRID_DAY_HEADER_HEIGHT`, replacing the old `GRID_MONTH_HEADER_HEIGHT`/`GRID_DATE_HEADER_HEIGHT` pair). The same `dateAxis` element now renders **twice** per column — once above the task rows, once below — so a task row far down a long task list is never more than a glance away from knowing which day its own column is, without needing to scroll back up to the top header. `gridLabelColumn` (the task-icon column outside the FlatList) grew a matching bottom spacer (`GRID_AXIS_HEIGHT`) alongside its existing top one, keeping its rows aligned with the grid's own task rows now that the grid itself is taller (axis + content + axis instead of just axis + content).

**2. Week and month vertical separators.** A new `gridSeparator` — a single absolutely-positioned `View` (`position: 'absolute', top: 0, bottom: 0, left: 0`) — spans the **whole column's own rendered height** (header through footer, in either Grid or Bars mode, since it's positioned against a plain-relative `gridColumn` parent that stretches to whatever height its content ends up needing — nothing to keep in sync by hand). Rendered on `isWeekStart` (`day.getDay() === 0`, i.e. Sunday) or `isMonthStart` (`day.getDate() === 1`), landing on the shared boundary with the older column to its right (this grid renders newest-first left-to-right, so a week/month's earliest day sits to the right of the previous week/month's latest day). Both share the same base color (`colors.border`) and differ only in width/opacity (`gridSeparatorWeek`: 1px, 0.5 opacity; `gridSeparatorMonth`: 1.5px, 0.9 opacity) so both stay visually subtle while the month separator reads as clearly the more prominent of the two, per the explicit ask. Month wins outright when a month also happens to start on a Sunday (one line, not two stacked ones) — implemented as a single ternary, not two independent conditions.

**3. A much larger month/year label.** The old per-column month abbreviation (`"MMM yy"`, capped at 8px — `"MMM yyyy"` didn't fit a 32px day column at all) is gone entirely. In its place, the Timeline section's own header — previously a static "Timeline" caption — now shows `format(visibleMonth, 'MMMM yyyy')` at `fontSize: 22`. Critically, `visibleMonth` wasn't new state added for this: it's the exact same value the mini month-grid section below already tracks reactively via `onViewableItemsChanged` (`sectionTitle`'s own `format(visibleMonth, 'MMMM yyyy')`, still there, unchanged) — so this label already updates live as the user scrolls the Timeline, for free. This is the actual answer to "how do you make the layout work so it can be bigger": a per-column label is hard-capped by that one column's own width (32px), but a label that lives *once*, above the whole horizontally-scrolling grid, isn't constrained by any single column at all.

**4. Performance evaluation, per explicit request.** Assessed as negligible:
- The weekday letter is one more cheap `format()` call per rendered column, alongside the day-number format call that already existed — not per task, so it doesn't scale with how many tasks are selected.
- The bottom axis duplicates a couple of already-cheap `Text` renders per column; `isWeekStart`/`isMonthStart` are trivial `Date` method calls (`.getDay()`, `.getDate()`).
- The separator itself is sparse by construction — it only renders on ~1-of-7 (week) or ~1-of-30 (month) columns, never on the rest.
- The big month label adds **zero** new computation or new re-render source: `visibleMonth` was already state read within this same component (driving the mini-month-grid section), so reading it in one more place doesn't create a new subscription or trigger — the component was already re-rendering on its change.
- None of the above touch the actual expensive per-`(task, day)` work (`getDayStreakState`, `buildDayConnectionInfo` lookups, completion-count lookups) — those are completely unchanged, and FlatList's own virtualization means all of this only ever runs for the small window of currently-mounted (visible + buffer) columns regardless, the same as before this change.
- The one structural change worth naming (not a regression, just worth knowing): each column's rendered height grew (axis + content + axis, not just axis + content). This doesn't affect the *horizontal* FlatList's own virtualization/windowing, which keys off width/x-position, not height — it just makes the outer vertical `ScrollView`'s total content taller, a one-time layout cost with no per-scroll-frame implication.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` only (116/116 unaffected, since none of the changed logic lives in a tested `utils/` module) — this is a layout/visual feature, worth a careful on-device look given the amount of structural change (axis duplicated top/bottom, label-column height now must match the grid's own new total height, separators need to align to the correct day).

**Follow-up (2026-08-10), three corrections from the same user, all same day.**

1. **"Two axis" meant two stacked *labels* (weekday letter + date), not a top-*and*-bottom duplicate.** Reverted the bottom copy of `dateAxis` entirely (both the Grid and Bars branches, plus the matching bottom spacer added to `gridLabelColumn`) — there's only ever one axis now, at the top, exactly as this app's other calendars already do it. `dateAxis` itself (the weekday-letter-over-day-number stack) is unchanged.
2. **Separators needed real visual differentiation, not just an opacity/width nudge.** Week separators switched from a solid filled background to a **dotted border** (`borderLeftWidth`/`borderStyle: 'dotted'`, since RN only supports dash/dot patterns on borders, not on solid fills) — genuinely subtle, a faint rhythm rather than a line. Month separators stayed solid but got heavier (`width: 2`, `colors.textSecondary` instead of `colors.border`, `opacity: 0.9`) for real contrast against the dotted week lines, per "the dividers more visible... the month separator should be stronger."
3. **The separator was on the wrong edge of the cell, a real bug (not just a style tweak).** Reported directly: "the week divider is between Monday/Saturday, shouldn't it be between Saturday/Sunday?" Root cause: this grid's index *increases* going right (older), so a Sunday's own **left** edge borders the more-recent Monday still inside the *same* week — not the boundary at all — while its **right** edge borders the next index over, the older Saturday, which *is* the real week boundary. The exact same off-by-one-edge logic affected the month separator too (a 1st-of-month's left edge borders the 2nd, not the prior month's last day). Fixed with a one-line change to the shared `gridSeparator` base style (`left: 0` → `right: 0`) — since `isWeekStart`/`isMonthStart`'s own trigger conditions (`getDay() === 0`, `getDate() === 1`) were already correct, only the edge they render on was wrong.

All three verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (116/116, unaffected — pure layout/positioning changes, no tested logic touched).

**Follow-up (2026-08-10): the smaller `sectionTitle` ("MMMM yyyy") above the per-task mini month grids removed as redundant, per direct user observation**, now that the same text renders large and prominent above the Timeline grid itself (`timelineMonthLabel`). The wrapping `<>...</>` fragment (previously needed since this section had two top-level children, the title and the grid) was also removed along with it, since the grid `View` is now the section's only element.

**Follow-up (2026-08-10), two quick corrections, same day.** First tried toning both separators down (`borderLeftWidth`/width, color, opacity all reduced) per "make both the dividers more subtle" — then reverted that back to the original heavier styling per direct follow-up ("use the prior less subtle styling"), so `gridSeparatorWeek`/`gridSeparatorMonth` ended up unchanged from where they were right after the initial "make the dividers more visible" pass (week: `borderLeftWidth: 1.5`, `colors.textTertiary`, `opacity: 0.6`; month: `width: 2`, `colors.textSecondary`, `opacity: 0.9`).

What *did* stick: per "how about make the dividers just up in the header area," `gridSeparator`'s own span changed from stretching the whole column (`top: 0, bottom: 0`, following whatever height the column's content needed) to a fixed `height: GRID_AXIS_HEIGHT` anchored at `top: 0` — confined to the axis row only, no longer reaching down through the task rows below it.

**A Bars-mode streak-connector feature (sloped SVG lines flowing between a task's own bar segments across days, since a task's segment can sit at a different height day-to-day depending on which other tasks also have a segment that day) was built, verified clean (`tsc`/`eslint`/`jest`), and then reverted the same day per direct user feedback ("that looks fuckin terrible, get rid of it") before ever reaching a device.** Reverted via `git checkout` back to the last commit (both `DashboardCalendarView.tsx` and this file), since the whole feature was still uncommitted. Worth remembering if a similar "connect two independently-varying values across virtualized/independent rendering units" need comes up again: the underlying geometry (shared top/bottom layout data feeding both the rendered shape and the connector, precomputed per day since a connector needs its neighbors' positions which an isolated `renderItem` can't see) was sound and cleanly verified, but evidently didn't read well visually in practice — lean toward a much lighter-touch first attempt (or checking in with a rough description before implementing) for this class of "flowing connector between independently-shifting positions" feature rather than building the full version straight away.

**Bars mode restyled to look like a real stacked bar chart bar (2026-08-10), per explicit user direction ("more like a bar chart bar... I mean a stacked bar chart").** Each day's segments previously read as a stack of separate rounded pills — every segment its own narrower-than-track, fully-rounded (`BAR_SEGMENT_WIDTH: '75%'`, `BAR_SEGMENT_RADIUS: 7` on all four corners), gap-separated (`BAR_SEGMENT_GAP: 2`) block. Changed to the conventional stacked-bar-chart look instead: every segment is now full track width (`barSegment`'s `width: '100%'`, replacing the centered 75%) with **no gap** between segments, so adjacent tasks' colors touch directly and read as one continuous multi-color bar. Rounding is no longer per-segment — a new `barSegmentTop` style (`borderTopLeftRadius`/`borderTopRightRadius: BAR_SEGMENT_RADIUS` (4, down from 7, now just a small cap rather than a pill's full curve), `overflow: 'hidden'`) applies only to whichever segment renders *first* (`index === 0` in the already-filtered, already-ordered `barSegments` array — the topmost segment, per the stack's existing top-to-bottom task order), leaving the rest of the stack — including the bottom, which rests on the track's own baseline — perfectly square, matching how a typical stacked bar chart only softens the very top of the bar.

Removing the gap also removed a whole category of complexity that existed purely to compensate for it: `barSegmentUnitHeight` (previously shrinking each segment's height slightly below `BAR_UNIT_HEIGHT` so `tasks.length - 1` worst-case gaps wouldn't overshoot the track's fixed height — see the "Overshoot bug" entry earlier in this doc) is deleted outright; every segment now just uses `fraction * BAR_UNIT_HEIGHT` directly, since with zero gap there's nothing left to overshoot. `barStack` itself is now a purely structural wrapper with no styles of its own (previously `alignItems: 'center'`, needed to center the narrower 75%-width segments — moot now that segments fill the full width). Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (127/127, unaffected — pure styling/layout change, no tested `utils/` logic touched).

**Tap-to-inspect a day added to Bars and Streamgraph (2026-08-10), per explicit user direction: tapping a column draws a grid line through it and shows a tooltip summarizing that day's data — Grid mode is untouched, this was scoped to just the two chart-style views.** New state, `selectedDate` (a `yyyy-MM-dd` string, matching every other per-day lookup key in this file), toggled by a shared `handleColumnTap(dateString)` (tapping the already-selected day deselects it), reset via a `useEffect` whenever `tasks` or `mainGridMode` changes — a stale line/tooltip pointing at a day that no longer applies to the current task filter or that Grid mode can't even render would be worse than just clearing it.

**The tooltip is a docked card, not a floating one that tracks the tapped bar's on-screen position.** Both charts scroll horizontally (a `FlatList` for Bars, a plain `ScrollView` for Streamgraph), so a true floating tooltip would need its x position recomputed on every scroll frame, handle clipping at the chart's edges, and decide when to flip sides — real complexity for comparatively little payoff. Instead it renders as a bordered `colors.surface` card (`dayTooltip`) sitting above the chart, right below the header row — always in the same place, never mispositioned or clipped regardless of scroll. Header: the tapped date (`EEEE, MMM d`) plus a close (X) button; body: one row per qualifying task (icon + name + a right-aligned status).

**Which tasks qualify, per explicit user direction ("don't show info for completely absent tasks"):** a task appears in the list only if `completionCount > 0` (it was actually completed, fully or partially, that day) *or* `dayConnectionInfoByTask`'s `isConnectedSelf` is true for that task/day (a soft-skip/pass-through day that's still part of a live streak thread) — computed in a new `selectedDayEntries` memo, reusing the exact same `completionCountsByTask`/`dayConnectionInfoByTask` maps already built for Grid/Bars rendering rather than a fresh lookup. A task with zero completion *and* no streak relevance to that specific day (a hard miss, a non-due day outside any chain, or simply never having anything to do with this task at all) is filtered out entirely — `.map(...).filter(entry => entry !== null)` with a type-guard, same pattern used elsewhere in this file. If the tapped day genuinely has nothing to show for any task, the card still renders (confirming the tap registered) with a plain "No activity that day" line rather than showing nothing at all.

**Per-row status, most-notable-state-wins:** a chain ending on this exact day (`connection.showStreakBadge`) shows a fire icon + `"${badgeValue}-day streak"` in the streak-red (`#FF6B6B`, the same literal already repeated at its other call sites throughout the codebase) ahead of anything else, since finishing/extending a streak is the most notable thing that can be true of a day. Otherwise: a completed multi-rep task shows its count (`"2/3"`), a completed single-rep task shows "Completed" (tinted in the task's own color, matching the per-task color-coding used everywhere else in the app), a partial multi-rep task also shows its count (in `colors.textSecondary`, since it's not yet done), and a connected-but-uncompleted day (the pass-through case) shows "Streak intact" — a deliberately generic label, since `isConnectedSelf` alone doesn't distinguish *why* (a non-due day, a still-open quota period, etc.) and inventing a per-reason label wasn't judged worth the added complexity for a first pass.

**Bars mode's tap target and grid line.** The whole day column (`gridColumn`, previously a plain `View`) is now a `Pressable` calling `handleColumnTap` with that column's own date string — direct and exact, no coordinate math needed, since `renderItem` already knows precisely which day it's rendering. The selection line (`columnSelectionLine`) is a plain absolutely-positioned 2px `View`, centered via `left: '50%'` + `marginLeft: -1` (safe here — unlike this file's earlier connector-track centering saga, there's no `aspectRatio`-derived parent or corner-radius math involved, just a simple fixed-width line), spanning `barColumnTrack`'s full height, rendered *last* in JSX (on top of the segments) so it stays visible regardless of what color happens to sit underneath it at that point.

**Streamgraph's tap target needed a different approach**, since the flowing ribbons are one continuous `<Path>` per task, not discrete per-day elements the way Bars' segments are — there's nothing per-day to attach an `onPress` to directly. Solved with a `streamTapOverlay`: a `position: 'absolute'` row of otherwise-invisible per-day `Pressable`s (`streamTapCell`, each exactly `GRID_CELL_SIZE` wide), layered on top of a `<View style={{ width: streamWidth, height: streamHeight }}>` that wraps both the `<Svg>` and this overlay — the explicit fixed size (rather than letting the wrapper auto-size to content) is what guarantees the absolutely-positioned overlay's `top/left/right/bottom: 0` lines up exactly with the SVG's own rendered bounds. The selection line itself is a second `<Line>` in the *same* `<Svg>` (reusing the exact coordinate space `streamPaths` already computes in — `x = selectedDayIndex * GRID_CELL_SIZE + GRID_CELL_SIZE / 2`, `y` spanning `0` to `streamHeight`), rendered after the task paths so it draws on top of them. `selectedDayIndex` (`days.findIndex(...)`, memoized) is `-1` when nothing's selected or the selected date somehow isn't in the currently-loaded `days` window, in which case the line simply doesn't render.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (127/127, unaffected — no changes to any tested `utils/` module); not yet confirmed on-device given this is a new interaction pattern for this screen (tap targets over horizontally-scrolling content, an SVG overlay for hit-testing in Streamgraph) — worth checking that taps register precisely (not off-by-one against the visually-tapped column, especially at the far edges of a scrolled view) and that the docked card's fixed position reads as clearly connected to the tapped column despite not visually pointing at it.

**Follow-up (2026-08-10), five refinements per explicit user direction, all same day.**

1. **Moved below the chart instead of above.** The card's `View` block relocated from right after `timelineHeaderRow` to right after the Grid/Bars/Streamgraph content block closes (and still above the per-task mini month grids section) — purely a JSX reordering, no logic changed.
2. **Slide in/out animation.** The plain `View` became a `Reanimated.View` with `entering={SlideInDown.duration(220)}` / `exiting={SlideOutDown.duration(180)}` — confirmed against Reanimated's own source (`Slide.ts`) before picking these over `SlideInUp`/`SlideOutUp`, since the naming is easy to get backwards: `SlideInDown`'s doc comment is "slide from bottom" and its `initialValues.originY` starts at `targetOriginY + windowHeight` (below its final position, animating *upward* into place) — exactly the "emerges from below, since it now sits below the chart" motion wanted here, despite "Down" being in the name. `SlideOutDown` mirrors it on the way out (slides back down and off). This is the first use of Reanimated's `entering`/`exiting` mount/unmount animation props anywhere in the codebase (existing usages like `HomeScreen`/`TaskDetailScreen` only use `entering`, e.g. `FadeIn.duration(150)`, for elements that don't need a reverse animation on unmount) — a plain conditional `{selectedDate && (...)}` render is all that's needed to trigger both; no manual mount/unmount state tracking required. Switching between two different already-selected days doesn't retrigger the animation (no remount happens, since the condition stays true) — deliberate, since replaying a slide for every tap between days would read as repetitive rather than a genuine show/hide.

   **Corrected the same day, per direct follow-up ("isn't quite what I meant -- I want it to slide in from the chart area, like its extending downward, and then slide back up").** `SlideInDown`/`SlideOutDown` translate an already-fully-sized element in from off-screen — reads as "arriving," not "extending out of the chart it's attached to." Replaced with a hand-written **height-based grow/shrink** instead: two module-level custom entering/exiting worklets, `dayTooltipEntering`/`dayTooltipExiting`, each returning a `LayoutAnimation` (`{ initialValues, animations }`) directly rather than using one of Reanimated's built-in preset builders — Reanimated's `entering`/`exiting` props accept a plain worklet function like this as a first-class alternative to `FadeIn`/`SlideInDown`/etc. (confirmed against `commonTypes.ts`'s `EntryAnimationsValues`/`ExitAnimationsValues`/`LayoutAnimation` exports before writing them, rather than guessing the shape). Entering: `height: 0 → withTiming(values.targetHeight, {duration: 220})`, `opacity: 0 → 1`; exiting mirrors it using `values.currentHeight → 0`. The key win over a manual `onLayout`-measured height: `targetHeight`/`currentHeight` are auto-measured by Reanimated's own layout animation system, so the card's actual natural height (which varies with however many tasks qualify that day) never needs measuring by hand. `dayTooltip` gained `overflow: 'hidden'` so its content clips cleanly while `height` is animating below its full value, rather than visibly overflowing the shrunken box — noted in a comment that this could clip an iOS shadow prop in principle, but Android's `elevation`-based shadow (this app's actual target platform) isn't affected the same way, so not a real concern here.

   **Follow-up, same day: "the reveal when showing looks good... you can make the close happen immediately with no transition."** `dayTooltipExiting` (the mirror-image shrink-to-zero worklet) was deleted outright, along with the `exiting` prop on the `Reanimated.View` — with no `exiting` animation supplied, Reanimated just removes the view immediately on unmount, which is exactly "no transition." `dayTooltipEntering` (the reveal) is completely unchanged; only the close side lost its animation. The now-unused `ExitAnimationsValues` type import was removed too.

   **Immediately followed by a related interaction change, same day: "I don't want to hide the panel when toggling a column that's already visible. Closing has to be done with the X button in the panel."** `handleColumnTap` previously toggled — tapping the already-selected column's date again set `selectedDate` back to `null`, closing the card. That's gone: `handleColumnTap` now unconditionally sets `selectedDate` to whatever was tapped (`setSelectedDate(dateString)`, no longer a `prev === dateString ? null : dateString` ternary) — tapping the same already-selected column again is a genuine no-op (React skips the re-render on an unchanged primitive state value, so it doesn't even re-run the entering animation), and the header's X button (`onPress={() => setSelectedDate(null)}`, unchanged from when it was first built) is now the *only* way to close the card.
3. **Replaced the text status ("Completed", "4/4", "Streak intact") with the same icon-based dot treatment Grid mode's `gridDot` already uses.** New `dayTooltipDot` style — a small (20px) circle, `colors.border` grey by default, going solid `task.color` when `isCompleted`, or staying grey with a `PartialDayPie` wedge (`fraction = completionCount / requiredTimes`) rendered inside for an under-quota multi-rep day — exactly mirroring `gridDot`'s own completed/partial pattern, just reused at a smaller size in a list row instead of a calendar cell. No numeric text anywhere now; the fill (solid vs. partial wedge) *is* the information.
4. **Pass-through days (zero completion, merely `isConnectedSelf`) removed entirely, not just "completely absent" ones.** `selectedDayEntries` now filters on `completionCount > 0` alone (previously `completionCount > 0 || isConnected`) — per explicit direction, this tooltip is specifically about what got *done* that day, not the broader streak-connectivity picture the calendars already show elsewhere. This incidentally simplified the entry shape too: `isPartial` and `isConnected` fields were dropped (no longer needed — `!isCompleted` alone is now equivalent to "partial," since a zero-completion entry can no longer reach this list at all), and the empty-state ("No activity that day") now also covers what used to read as "Streak intact" pass-through days, which is the intended behavior — a day where nothing was actually completed shows the empty state regardless of any streak still being technically alive.
5. **Streak-ending days get their final count called out via the existing `StreakCountBadge` component** (fire icon + number, the same one Grid/Calendar already use), rendered as its own element in the row — between the task name and the completion dot — whenever `connection.showStreakBadge` is true for that task/day, rather than folded into text as it briefly was. Reusing the actual component (not a hand-rolled fire-icon-plus-text pairing) keeps this visual language consistent with every other place in the app a streak's length gets called out.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (127/127, unaffected). Worth an on-device look specifically at the slide animation's feel (`220`/`180` were picked as a reasonable "quick" starting point, not yet tuned against the app's other animation timings) and the dot treatment at 20px, since `PartialDayPie`'s wedge angle can read less precisely at very small sizes than it does in a full 24px `gridDot`.

**Bars mode's left-hand task-icon column removed, replaced (along with Streamgraph) by a minimalistic legend below the chart (2026-08-10), per explicit user direction.** `gridLabelColumn` (the fixed left column of task icons, one per row, shared by Grid and Bars since both used the same `gridWrapper` layout) is now conditional on `mainGridMode !== 'bars'` — Grid mode keeps it unchanged, since each row there really is one fixed task for the whole column's height. Bars never had that same 1:1 alignment to begin with (a day's stacked segments only include whichever tasks actually have a completion that day, bottom-anchored in a shifting stack — see this file's own "Overshoot bug"/stacked-bar-chart history above), so the icon column never lined up with anything meaningful there; removing it was a correction, not just a stylistic choice.

In its place: a new `minimalLegend` — a plain wrapping row of `minimalLegendItem`s (icon + name, no swatch, no card chrome), rendered below whichever chart is currently showing, for **both** Bars and Streamgraph. This is a deliberately lighter version of the icon+swatch+name legend Streamgraph briefly had and then had removed a few turns earlier for being "redundant to the icons at the top and individual month grids below." Shown only when `!selectedDate` (the day-tooltip panel isn't open) — the panel already names every task it lists with icon *and* full name, so showing both at once would be genuinely redundant; the legend and the tooltip occupy the same visual slot below the chart, switching based on selection state rather than stacking.

**Follow-up, same day: "that doesn't look like a legend, it doesn't have names, doesn't look like a legend."** The first pass over-corrected — bare colored icons with no name at all (`minimalLegend` mapping straight to `<MaterialCommunityIcons>` elements, no wrapping item, no text) genuinely can't function as a legend, since a legend's whole job is mapping a visual marker to an identity, and an icon alone doesn't do that for anyone who hasn't already memorized which icon belongs to which task. The earlier "redundant" reasoning about Streamgraph's old legend was specifically about the extra *swatch* circle being one marker too many alongside the icon's own color — not about the name being unnecessary. Fixed by pairing each icon with its task's name again (`minimalLegendItem`: icon `size={14}` + `minimalLegendLabel`, `fontSize: 11`, `colors.textSecondary`, `numberOfLines={1}`, `maxWidth: 120` so a long name truncates rather than pushing the row wide) — still no swatch and no card chrome, so it stays visually lighter than Streamgraph's original three-marker version, but now actually reads as a legend rather than decoration.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (127/127, unaffected — pure conditional-rendering/styling change, no tested `utils/` logic touched).

**Follow-up, same day, three more spacing/layout requests all landing together: center it, add real separation from the mini month grid cards below, and pull it snug against the chart above.**
- **Centered**: `minimalLegend` gained `justifyContent: 'center'` — both the row itself and how each wrapped line packs when there are enough tasks to wrap.
- **Separation below**: a new `minimalLegendDivider` (a real hairline, `colors.border`, not just added whitespace) renders as a sibling right after the legend, inside the same conditional (wrapped in a `<>...</>` fragment now that the block has two top-level children) — relies on `dayTooltip`'s neighbor, `gap: 8`-style spacing isn't in play here since this is a different container, so the divider's own implicit margin from `minimalLegend`'s `marginBottom: 16` above it and its own placement before the mini month grid section is what creates the gap on both sides.
- **Snug above**: this took more than a style tweak on the legend itself, since the *gap above* it was coming from the chart container, not the legend. Bars mode's chart lives in `gridWrapper` (`marginBottom: 24`, shared with Grid mode) — reducing that shared value directly would've also tightened Grid mode's own (currently-fine) gap before the mini month grids, so instead added a conditional override, `gridWrapperTight` (`marginBottom: 8`), applied only when `mainGridMode === 'bars'`. Streamgraph's outer wrapper previously had *no* style at all (a bare `<View>`, since nothing used to render directly below it) — gave it a new `streamgraphWrapper` style with the same `marginBottom: 8`, for consistency between the two modes' chart-to-legend gap rather than one being tighter than the other by accident.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (127/127, unaffected — pure layout/styling, no tested `utils/` logic touched).

**Follow-up, same day, the legend abandoned entirely: "Fuck it, just get rid of the legend. Add a horizontal line under the bar graph, though."** After several rounds of tuning (bare icons → icon+name → centered/spaced/snugged), the legend concept itself got dropped. `minimalLegend`/`minimalLegendItem`/`minimalLegendLabel`/`minimalLegendDivider` (styles and JSX) were deleted outright, along with the `gridWrapperTight`/`streamgraphWrapper` margin overrides that existed purely to pull the chart snug against it — Bars' chart wrapper reverted to the plain shared `gridWrapper` (same as Grid mode again), and Streamgraph's outer `<View>` reverted to bare/unstyled. Bars' own left-hand task-icon column (removed earlier in this same saga, since it never lined up with the shifting stacked segments anyway) stays removed; nothing replaces its identification role now beyond the day-tooltip panel's own per-task icon+name rows and the mini month grid cards below.

**First pass at "the horizontal line" was a plain `barsDivider` element rendered once below the whole chart wrapper — corrected immediately, same day, per direct follow-up: "the horizontal line should be touching the bottom of the bars, like a bottom border on the chart, like an x axis the chart is originating from."** A single element positioned below the *entire* chart section (with its own margin) reads as a generic section divider, not an axis the bars sit on. `barsDivider` was deleted and replaced with `borderBottomWidth: 1, borderBottomColor: colors.border` added directly to `barColumnTrack` itself — the per-day column every bar segment already renders inside. Since every day's `barColumnTrack` shares the exact same fixed height (`tasks.length * BAR_UNIT_HEIGHT`) and is bottom-anchored (`justifyContent: 'flex-end'`), each column's own bottom border lands at the identical Y position across the whole horizontally-scrolling chart, so the borders line up end-to-end and read as one continuous axis line the bars visibly rest on — not a single spanning element (which isn't achievable across `FlatList`'s independently-rendered/virtualized columns without the kind of cross-column geometry this same file's reverted connector-line feature ran into), just many small per-column borders that happen to align. The ~6px `marginHorizontal` gap between adjacent days' tracks leaves a barely-visible break in the line at each day boundary — a deliberately accepted minor imperfection rather than solving for a perfectly unbroken line, given how small the gap is and the complexity avoided by not chasing it.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (127/127, unaffected — pure styling, no tested `utils/` logic touched).

**Grid mode's connecting track removed entirely, replaced by a self-contained pill mark on pass-through days (2026-08-10), per explicit user direction ("get rid of the tracks completely... just render pass-through days with a little colored, semi-transparent pill, similar to the home screen task tile calendar face").** This is the main Timeline grid specifically (`DashboardCalendarView.tsx`'s `mainGridMode === 'grid'` per-`(task, day)` cell render) — not `TaskCalendarScreen.tsx`'s own Month-view track (a separate, independently-tuned feature with its own long history above; untouched here) and not Bars/Streamgraph, which never had this connector-track concept to begin with.

The whole `connectorBandWrap`/`connectorBand` block — the half-width/full-width/rounded band that used to render behind `gridDot`, tracking a task's streak continuity across adjacent day cells (its own multi-entry history fills much of this doc above: thickness tuning, vertical-centering fixes, the today-connectivity fix, etc.) — is deleted outright, JSX and styles both (`connectorBandWrap`, `connectorBand`, `connectorHalfLeft`/`Right`, `connectorRoundLeft`/`Right`, and the now-unused `CONNECTOR_LINE_THICKNESS` constant). `dayConnectionInfoByTask` itself (the `buildDayConnectionInfo` map powering `isRunStart`/`isRunEnd`/`isConnectedSelf`) is untouched and still computed — it's still needed for the streak-count badge (`connection?.showStreakBadge`), which is a separate feature from the track and stays exactly as it was; only the track-shaping conditionals (`isRunStart`/`isRunEnd` deciding half-width vs. rounded-full-width) are gone, since there's no track left to shape.

In place of the track's role marking a pass-through/soft-skip day (`isSkipped`, i.e. `getDayStreakState(...) === 'connected'`): a new `skipPill` — a small (`width: '75%', height: 8, borderRadius: 4`), semi-transparent (`opacity: 0.4`) capsule in the task's own color, rendered *inside* `gridDot`'s existing circular container (the same slot `PartialDayPie`/`MissedDayMark` already render into for their own states) rather than as a separate absolutely-positioned overlay — so the cell's structure/layout is unchanged, only what draws inside it for the `isSkipped` case is new. Deliberately self-contained per cell now, with no cross-cell connecting element at all — "similar in spirit to" (not a literal reuse of) the Home screen `TaskCard`'s own flipped-calendar-face pass-through mark, which is actually a dash (`SkippedDayMark`) rather than a pill; the instruction's own "similar to" wording was read as "match that general colored/semi-transparent/task-tinted visual language," not "import the exact same component," since a dash and a pill are visually distinct shapes and the user asked for a pill specifically.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (127/127, unaffected — pure rendering/styling change scoped to one file, no tested `utils/` logic touched); worth an on-device look to confirm pass-through days read clearly as "connected but not required" now that there's no connecting band between consecutive skip days to reinforce that reading visually — each one is judged on its own mark alone.

**Replaced the same day, per explicit user direction: "a gray circle (same as skipped day with no streak) and a very thiner full width semi transparent colored line (still only on the pass days, not on completed days)."** The `skipPill` capsule (rendered *inside* `gridDot`, only a few turns old) is gone, replaced by two separate pieces:
- **The dot itself** now matches a plain soft-miss day's own look exactly — `isSkipped` was added to the same conditional that already applies `gridDotSoftMiss` (`opacity: 0.3` over `gridDot`'s default grey fill) for `isSoftMissed`, and removed from the `gridDotEmpty` (transparent) group it previously shared with `isMissed`/`isExpiringToday`. A pass-through day and a genuinely-nothing-going-on soft-miss day are now visually identical at the dot level — the *only* thing that distinguishes them is the new line below.
- **A new `skipLine`** — full `gridCell` width (not just `gridDot`'s smaller diameter), `height: 2` (down sharply from the old track's `CONNECTOR_LINE_THICKNESS` progression, which had ranged 8–22 across this doc's whole connector history), `opacity: 0.4`, in the task's own color — rendered as a sibling *before* `gridDot` in JSX (so it paints behind the dot, reading as a thread the grey dot sits on rather than a line cutting across it). Vertically centered via `top: '50%'` + a negative `marginTop` — flagged as safe specifically because `gridCell` has an explicit fixed height (`GRID_CELL_SIZE`), not the `aspectRatio`-derived kind that caused this exact percentage-centering technique to misbehave elsewhere in this file's earlier connector-track history (`TaskCalendarScreen`'s own `day`/`dayContent`).

Only rendered for `isSkipped` (a real pass-through day, i.e. a live streak thread) — never for `isSoftMissed` ("no streak at stake, nothing to thread") and never for completed days, per explicit direction.
