Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (127/127, unaffected — pure rendering/styling change, no tested `utils/` logic touched).

**Follow-up, same day: "I want the line over top of the gray circle."** `skipLine`'s render moved from *before* `gridDot` in JSX to *after* it — a plain paint-order swap (still a sibling of `gridDot`, not nested inside it), so it now draws on top of the dot instead of behind it. Caught and fixed a real syntax bug introduced while moving it (a stray extra `</View>` left behind from the reshuffle, closing `gridCell` one element early and breaking the rest of the file's JSX) via `tsc`, before it ever reached lint or tests. Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (127/127, unaffected).

**A minimal fixed left-hand Y axis added to Bars and Streamgraph (2026-08-10), per explicit user direction ("just using minimalist axis and a few numbers").** Grid mode is untouched (it keeps its own task-icon label column). New module-level `YAxisColumn` (`{ ticks, max, height, styles }`) — since Bars and Streamgraph already share the exact same total vertical scale by design (`tasks.length * BAR_UNIT_HEIGHT`, "every selected task fully done that day" — already documented above as deliberate, so the two modes stay visually comparable), one small component covers both rather than duplicating the tick math and layout twice. Deliberately spare: a single thin `colors.border` vertical line (`yAxisTrack`'s `borderRightWidth: 1`) and a handful of plain number labels (`yAxisLabel`, `fontSize: 10`, `colors.textTertiary`) — no gridlines reaching across the chart, no axis title, nothing beyond the line and the numbers themselves.

Original first pass: ticks were `[tasks.length, Math.round(tasks.length / 2), 0]` (three fixed points), and the column had its own separate `Y_AXIS_WIDTH = 24`. Both were revised the same day, see the follow-up below. Each label's vertical position is computed directly from its own value against the shared `height`/`max` (originally `height - (tick / max) * height`, then nudged up by `Y_AXIS_LABEL_HALF_HEIGHT = 6` to center the label's own text on its tick point rather than aligning its top edge there) — plain arithmetic against a value already known at render time, no measurement needed.

Placement: rendered as a fixed sibling *outside* the horizontal scrolling content in both modes (`gridWrapper`'s existing row, alongside where Grid mode's own label column renders, for Bars; a new `{flexDirection:'row'}` wrapper added around Streamgraph's previously-bare outer `<View>`, with the axis column and the `ScrollView` as siblings), matching how Grid mode's task-icon column already stays fixed while its own content scrolls underneath/beside it — not inside the `FlatList`/`ScrollView`, which would have scrolled the axis away with the chart instead of keeping it pinned to the left edge.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (127/127, unaffected — pure additive rendering, no tested `utils/` logic touched).

**Three fixes, same day, all per explicit user direction, landing together.**

1. **Column width now matches `gridLabelColumn` exactly** ("so switching doesn't shift around the headers") — `Y_AXIS_WIDTH` (a separate `24`) was deleted; `yAxisColumn`'s `width` now reads `GRID_LABEL_WIDTH` (`36`, the same constant Grid mode's own task-icon column already uses) directly, so the fixed left column is always the same width across all three modes and nothing in the header/chart area shifts horizontally when toggling between them.
2. **Streamgraph's tick values were wrong, per direct correction ("On a stream graph the middle is 0, and the ends are the max, yes?") — yes, exactly right.** Bars is a flat-bottomed 0-to-max stack (0 at the bottom, max at the top), but Streamgraph's own silhouette baseline (`computeStreamgraphLayers`) centers every day's stack around 0 — its "0" is the vertical *middle* of the track, and a given magnitude can extend that far in *either* direction from center, not climb once from a fixed bottom edge. `YAxisColumn` gained a `mirrored?: boolean` prop (Streamgraph passes `mirrored`, Bars doesn't): in mirrored mode, every nonzero tick renders *twice* — once above center, once below, both showing the same magnitude — with a single unmirrored `0` at the true center, rather than reusing Bars' single climb-from-bottom mapping, which had been silently wrong for Streamgraph's actual coordinate space from the start.
3. **More ticks, scaled to how many tasks are actually selected ("based on the number of visible tasks").** The fixed three-tick set (`max`/`mid`/`0`) was replaced by a new `getYAxisTicks(max)`: one tick per whole task count (`0` through `max`) when that's `Y_AXIS_MAX_TICKS` (6) or fewer — which, given the app's own free-tier active-task cap is also 6 (see the "Free-tier task cap" section below), covers realistically every free-tier user without ever needing to thin — falling back to evenly-spaced whole-number steps (`Math.ceil(max / Y_AXIS_MAX_TICKS)` apart, always including `max` itself even if the step doesn't land on it exactly) for a task count large enough to otherwise crowd the column.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (127/127, unaffected — pure additive/rendering change, no tested `utils/` logic touched).

**Follow-up, same day: "stream graph mode needs margin below the graph before the month grids, consistent with the margin we already have on the grid and bar modes."** Streamgraph's outer wrapper (holding the new Y-axis column and the horizontal `ScrollView`) had only ever gotten a bare `{ flexDirection: 'row' }` inline style — no `marginBottom` — so it sat snug against the mini month grid cards below, unlike Grid/Bars' shared `gridWrapper` (`marginBottom: 24`). Gave it a real named style, `streamWrapper` (`flexDirection: 'row', marginBottom: 24`) — deliberately *not* reusing `gridWrapper` itself, since that style also carries `marginHorizontal: -16, paddingLeft: 16` (canceling the screen's own side padding for an edge-to-edge scroll) which Streamgraph was never asked to take on and wasn't part of this request — just matching the one property that was actually the point: the bottom gap before the next section.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (127/127, unaffected — pure styling, no tested `utils/` logic touched).

**The day-tooltip panel's header restyled the same day, per explicit user direction ("make the date title a bit more distinct and title-like, perhaps with a divider... make the close button bigger").** `dayTooltipDate` sized/weighted up from `14`/`700` to `17`/`800` — closer to how the app's other screen-level titles read (e.g. `DashboardScreen`'s own `headerTitle`) rather than just another line of body text. A new `dayTooltipHeaderDivider` hairline renders directly below the header row, separating the title from the task list — relies on `dayTooltip`'s own `gap: 8` for spacing on both sides rather than carrying its own margin. The close button's icon grew `16 → 20`, and gained its own `dayTooltipCloseButton` style (`padding: 6, margin: -6`) — padding alone would have pushed the header row's layout outward and shifted where the button's outer edge sits; pairing it with an equal negative margin grows the button's real visible/tappable footprint (not just `hitSlop`'s already-existing invisible extension) while keeping its outer edge anchored where the bare icon used to sit.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (127/127, unaffected — pure styling, no tested `utils/` logic touched).

**Grid/Bars toggle became icon-based, plus a third "Streamgraph" mode (2026-08-10), per explicit user direction.** The `mainGridModeToggle` pill's two `TouchableOpacity`s switched from visible text labels ("Grid"/"Bars") to `MaterialCommunityIcons` (`view-grid-outline`/`chart-bar`/plus the new `chart-areaspline-variant` for Streamgraph, size 18, tinted `colors.textSecondary` when inactive and white when active) — rendered via one `.map()` over a small `{ mode, icon, label }` array rather than three near-identical `TouchableOpacity` blocks, now that there are three. Since the buttons are icon-only, each gained an explicit `accessibilityLabel` ("Grid view"/"Bars view"/"Streamgraph view") — previously unnecessary since the visible text itself served that role for screen readers; `accessibilityRole="radio"` + `accessibilityState={{checked}}` (the app's established mutually-exclusive-toggle-group convention) are unchanged. `mainGridModeButtonText`/`mainGridModeButtonTextActive` styles were deleted as now-unused; `mainGridModeButton`'s padding changed from text-oriented (`paddingHorizontal: 14, paddingVertical: 6`) to a squarer icon-button shape (`10`/`8`).

**Streamgraph mode** shows the same per-day, per-task completion data as Grid/Bars, but as flowing stacked ribbons (a classic "streamgraph"/ThemeRiver look) rather than a grid of dots or flat-bottomed bars — each task's daily completion fraction becomes a layer thickness, stacked using a **silhouette baseline** (the whole stack centered around zero each day, rather than resting on a flat zero edge the way Bars mode's stack does) via a new pure, unit-tested module `app/utils/streamgraph.ts`:
- `computeStreamgraphLayers(values: number[][]): StreamLayerPoint[][]` — `values[t][d]` is task t's already-pixel-scaled thickness on day d (every task's array the same length); returns each task's own `{top, bottom}` span per day in a coordinate space centered on 0. Pure stacking math, no rendering concerns.
- `buildStreamLayerPath(topPoints, bottomPoints): string` — a closed SVG path per layer: a smooth top edge (left-to-right), a straight drop to the bottom edge at the rightmost day, a smooth bottom edge back (right-to-left), then closed. "Smooth" uses a lightweight quadratic-Bezier-through-midpoints technique (each original point becomes a control point, the curve itself passes through the *midpoint* of each consecutive pair) rather than full Catmull-Rom spline math — simple enough to hand-write and unit-test exactly, while still giving the flowing look a streamgraph needs instead of jagged straight-line segments between days.
- Covered by `__tests__/utils/streamgraph.test.ts` (11 tests): silhouette centering for one/multiple tasks, per-day baseline recomputation as totals change, an all-zero-day edge case (guarded against a stray `-0` baseline — numerically harmless but the more honest value to return/compare against is a plain `0`), and path-shape assertions (starts at the first top point, reaches the exact last top/first bottom point before dropping/closing, uses `Q` commands between interior points, degenerates safely for a single loaded day).

**Why this needed its own rendering path, not another `FlatList` `renderItem` branch like Grid/Bars:** a streamgraph is one continuous flowing shape across the *whole* visible width — it can't be decomposed into independently-rendered, virtualized day columns the way a grid of cells or a set of flat-bottomed bars can (each of *those* only ever needs to know about itself). So when `mainGridMode === 'streamgraph'`, the screen renders an entirely different branch: a plain horizontal `ScrollView` (not a `FlatList`) holding one `<Svg>` sized to the full loaded date range (`days.length * GRID_CELL_SIZE` wide), with one `<Path>` per task, plus a day-axis row (reusing `gridAxisCell`'s weekday-letter-over-day-number look, wrapped in `gridColumn` for its fixed per-day width so it lines up with the SVG's own per-day x-positions — a real alignment bug caught before committing: without that explicit width, the axis cells would shrink-to-content instead of matching each day's fixed 32px slot). It still shares the exact same `days`/`loadedDays` pagination state as Grid/Bars (switching modes doesn't reset how much history is loaded) via its own `onScroll`-driven "am I near the end" check (comparing `contentOffset.x + layoutMeasurement.width` against `contentSize.width`) in place of `FlatList`'s `onEndReached`, and drives the shared `visibleMonth` state (which the mini month-grid section below depends on) via the same scroll handler computing which day is centered in the viewport, mirroring what `onViewableItemsChanged` does for the `FlatList`-based modes.

**Follow-up (2026-08-10), two changes per explicit user direction, right after the first on-device look ("Look great. A few changes...").**
1. **Week/month divider lines added to the axis row.** The same `gridSeparator`/`gridSeparatorWeek`/`gridSeparatorMonth` styles and `isWeekStart`/`isMonthStart` trigger logic Grid/Bars' `renderItem` already uses (see the earlier Timeline-redesign entries above for the full reasoning — right-edge positioning to match this newest-first-left grid, month winning over week on a tie, confined to the axis row's own height) are now reused directly inside the streamgraph axis's own `days.map()`, rendered as a sibling of the weekday/date labels within each `gridColumn`-wrapped cell. No new styles needed — this was purely wiring up styling that already existed for the other two modes.
2. **The legend row was removed entirely**, per direct feedback that it was redundant with the task icons already shown in the Timeline header row above and the per-task mini month grids below. `streamLegend`/`streamLegendItem`/`streamLegendSwatch`/`streamLegendLabel` styles were deleted along with it, not just left unused.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (127/127, unaffected — no logic in a tested `utils/` module touched).

`streamPaths` (the computed `{task, path}` list) is skipped entirely (`useMemo` returns `[]` early) whenever `mainGridMode !== 'streamgraph'`, so switching to/using Grid or Bars never pays for this computation. Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (127/127, up from 116 with the new util's 11 tests) — this is a genuinely new rendering architecture for this screen (a second scrollable container, a hand-built SVG path generator) so it's worth a careful on-device look, particularly: the axis/SVG alignment across a pagination boundary, the legend's wrapping behavior with many selected tasks, and whether the silhouette baseline (versus the more visually-optimized "wiggle" baseline some streamgraph implementations use, deliberately not attempted here given the added complexity for a first pass) reads as acceptably smooth rather than jumpy when day-to-day totals vary a lot.

**`TaskCard`'s outer icon ring now splits into segments for >1x/day tasks (2026-08-08).** Previously the ring (the `Circle` stroke drawn around the icon, separate from the `dayProgressAnimatedProps` fill wedge and the press-and-hold `animatedProps` sweep wedge already documented above) was always one continuous circle regardless of `timesPerDay`. Per explicit user direction, while a task is incomplete it now renders as `timesPerDayCount` equal arc segments with a small gap between each (`RING_SEGMENT_GAP_DEGREES = 14`) instead, for any task requiring more than one rep a day — a static structural cue ("this many reps needed today") independent of the fill-based progress wedge, which still shows how many of those reps are actually done. `ringSegments` (a `useMemo` keyed only on `timesPerDayCount`, since the shape doesn't depend on any animated/shared value) computes each segment's arc path via a `getRingPoint(angleDegrees)` helper — the same `(angle - 90) * (π/180)` convention (0° = top, clockwise) the existing pie-wedge code already used, just building a plain stroke arc (`M start A r r 0 largeArcFlag 1 end`, no line back to center) rather than a filled wedge — with `strokeLinecap="round"` on each segment so the gap ends read as soft rounded caps rather than flat cuts. 1x/day tasks (`ringSegments` is `null`) keep the original single continuous `Circle` stroke unchanged, and the completed state (solid `task.color` fill, regardless of `timesPerDay`) is also unchanged — segments only apply to the incomplete, >1x/day case.

**Follow-up (2026-08-08): the incomplete-state ring/track is now faded/muted, so the press-and-hold sweep visibly "fills it in."** Per explicit user direction, both incomplete-state branches (the plain `Circle` for 1x/day, the `ringSegments` arcs for >1x/day) now render at `opacity={INCOMPLETE_RING_OPACITY}` (0.25) instead of full strength — deliberately more faded than the existing 0.35 used by the today's-progress wedge (`dayProgressAnimatedProps`), so the visual hierarchy reads faintest-to-boldest as: empty track (0.25) → today's already-logged reps (0.35) → the press-and-hold sweep itself, which was already full solid `task.color` with no opacity set and needed no change — it was always "filling" over whatever sat behind it, it just wasn't very noticeable when the track behind it was full-strength color too. The completed state (solid fill) is unaffected.

**Follow-up (2026-08-08): the press-and-hold confirmation itself now fills the ring/track, not a pie wedge in the center.** The previous behavior (`animatedProps`, since renamed `holdProgressAnimatedProps`) built a *filled pie-slice* path (`M center, L top, A ..., Z`) that grew a solid wedge outward from the icon's center as `progress.value` (the hold-gesture's own 0→1) increased — reading as a wedge filling the middle of the icon, not the ring itself. Per explicit user direction ("I want the ring to be filled up solid like a progress bar"), it now builds a *stroke arc* instead (`M top, A r r 0 largeArcFlag 1 x y` — no center point, no `Z` close), rendered with `stroke`/`strokeWidth`/`strokeLinecap="round"`/`fill="none"` at `CIRCLE_RADIUS` — the same radius and stroke styling as the muted track/`ringSegments` themselves, so holding now visibly fills up the ring/track like a circular progress bar sweeping clockwise from the top, matching the muted-track-gets-filled-with-solid-color effect the previous ring-opacity change was meant to set up. Also added an `angle <= 0` guard (return `{ d: '' }`) matching `dayProgressAnimatedProps`'s existing pattern, avoiding a degenerate zero-length arc right at the start of a press.

**Follow-up (2026-08-08): for >1x/day tasks, each already-logged rep's own segment now renders solid, even before the overall task is complete.** Previously every segment in `ringSegments` rendered at the same muted `INCOMPLETE_RING_OPACITY` regardless of `completionCount`, with only the separate `dayProgressAnimatedProps` pie wedge showing today's actual progress. Per explicit user direction, the segment `.map()` now renders each segment at `opacity={i < completionCount ? 1 : INCOMPLETE_RING_OPACITY}` — segment order matches rep order (index 0 first; reps themselves are interchangeable, not tied to which specific press logged which one), so the first `completionCount` segments go solid and the rest stay muted. This is purely a styling addition to the existing segment `.map()`, still governed by the same `completionCount`/`timesPerDayCount` values already in scope — `ringSegments` itself (the memoized geometry) is untouched, since the per-segment solid/muted state is derived at render time rather than baked into the memoized path data. `dayProgressAnimatedProps`'s wedge was left in place, not removed — the user asked specifically for the segment behavior, not to drop the existing wedge indicator.

**Follow-up (2026-08-08): the original inner pie-slice fill is back too, alongside the ring-arc fill, per explicit user direction ("bring back the inner solid circle/pie animation while holding").** The ring-arc-only version above wasn't a full replacement after all — both now render together during a press-and-hold, driven by the same `progress.value * 360` sweep: `holdProgressPieAnimatedProps` (the restored original filled-pie-from-center path, at `INNER_CIRCLE_RADIUS`, with the same `angle <= 0` guard added for consistency) renders first, then `holdProgressAnimatedProps` (the ring/track stroke arc) on top — so holding now fills the icon's interior *and* sweeps the ring simultaneously, rather than either alone.

**Fixed (2026-08-08): long-pressing an already-completed task card played the completion animation before discovering there was nothing to complete.** `TaskCard`'s `handleLongPress` used to unconditionally `setIsCompleting(true)` for the task face regardless of completed state, so an already-done task still ran the full pop/checkmark animation — only after `COMPLETION_HOLD_DURATION` elapsed did `onCompleted` fire, calling `onLongPressTask`, which (in `HomeScreen`) checked `isTaskCompleted` and routed to `add-task` (Edit) instead of actually completing. The animation played every time regardless of whether anything was actually being completed, which read as broken. Per explicit user direction ("I want no completion interaction... just open the stats/calendar view"), `handleLongPress` now checks `isTaskCompleted(task)` *before* deciding what to do: an already-completed task skips the animation entirely and calls a new `onLongPressCompletedTask` prop immediately, while an incomplete task behaves exactly as before. Kept as a separate prop from `onLongPressTask` rather than having one callback branch on completed state internally, so `HomeScreen`'s `handleLongPressTask` could simplify down to only the completion path (it's now never called for an already-completed task, so the `isTaskCompleted` check and Edit-task navigation that used to live there were deleted outright rather than left dead). `handleLongPressCompletedTask` opens `task-detail` with no `tab` param — the same "falls back to `taskDetailLastTab`" behavior `task-detail` already had for any tab-less navigation — satisfying "remember last selection" for free rather than needing new state. The accessibility action label (`longPressActionLabel`) was updated to match: "Open task details" instead of "Mark complete" when the task is already done.

`app/utils/importExport.ts` — export/import logic used by `SettingsScreen`:
- Export shape is `TasksExport` (`app/types/index.ts`): `{ schemaVersion, exportId, exportedAt, appVersion, taskCount, tasks }`. `schemaVersion` is currently always `1`; `migrateTasksExport` (a `switch` on `schemaVersion`) is the intended landing spot for future format migrations, but there's only the one case so far.
- `parseTasksImport` accepts both the wrapped `TasksExport` format and the old bare-`Task[]`-array format (pre-schema exports) for backward compatibility — legacy imports parse with `meta: null`.
- Import is either `'replace'` (drop-in swap) or `'merge'` via `mergeTaskLists`: tasks are matched by `id`; for a match, the task with the newer `updatedAt` wins for top-level fields, and `completions` are unioned by completion `id` (not concatenated) so re-importing doesn't duplicate completions. Tasks only present in one list are kept as-is. Which mode runs is picked by which Settings row the user tapped (see Toasts section below), not a runtime prompt.
- **Bug (2026-08-08): `SettingsScreen.handleImport`'s file picker couldn't select real export files browsed from Dropbox.** `DocumentPicker.getDocumentAsync` was called with `type: 'application/json'` — several cloud-storage document providers (Dropbox specifically, confirmed) don't reliably report that exact MIME type for synced files, so the picker showed the file but greyed it out as unselectable, even though it was a perfectly valid export. Fixed by loosening the picker to `type: '*/*'` — the actual content is already validated immediately after picking via `parseTasksImport` (which throws a clear "This doesn't look like a Streakaholic export file" toast for anything that isn't real export JSON), so the OS-level MIME filter was redundant safety that was actively breaking real imports rather than preventing bad ones.

`TaskCard.tsx` is the most complex component: a flip card with three faces (task/calendar/stats) cycled via long-press-context-dependent actions, plus its own completion animation (progress ring, checkmark swap, streak badge pop, confetti-style `ParticleSystem`), and a secondary static progress ring + "`n`/`timesPerDay`" label for multi-rep tasks. The completion pop's timing and structure (2026-08-07, five passes) went through: its original damping:8/stiffness:100 spring + 200ms fades + 500ms fixed hold (800ms total, carried over unchanged from the pre-Reanimated `Animated` version and never re-tuned — read as a slow, gentle bounce with a sluggish hold); a punchier spring; an explicit-duration three-phase version (150ms pop + 1000ms hold + 450ms return) built specifically to stop depending on a spring's completion callback for control flow (that callback measured 3-4s on Android instead of the few-hundred-ms its damping/stiffness would predict) — abandoned for feeling mechanical, with the 1000ms hold in particular reading as an awkward freeze; a single-action version (`scale.value = 1.2` instant jump, then one `withSpring` back down) that fixed the timing but, per explicit user direction, still didn't match the intended interaction: **the press-and-hold depress and the completion pop were two independent, uncoordinated animations** (`TaskCard`'s own `scaleAnim`, whole-card, reset only on finger-lift; `CardTask`'s `scale`, icon-only, teleporting to a peak with no relation to whatever the outer depress was doing) rather than one continuous "depress → elastic overshoot → settle" motion.

The current version unifies them: `CardTask` now takes an `isPressed` prop (mirroring `HomeScreen`'s `TaskCard`-ref pattern of passing interaction state down as a plain prop) and its own `scale` shared value handles the *entire* journey in one `useEffect` keyed on `[isPressed, isCompleting]` — while not completing, `scale` just tracks press state (`withTiming(isPressed ? DEPRESS_SCALE : 1, ...)`, `DEPRESS_SCALE` = 0.9); once `isCompleting` flips true (typically while still pressed, since the long-press fires before the finger lifts), a spring springs *from wherever it currently sits* (depressed) up through an elastic overshoot and settles at rest — a genuinely continuous motion, not two layered ones. The checkmark reversal + `onCompleted` (the real task completion + streak update) still fire from a plain fixed `setTimeout(COMPLETION_HOLD_DURATION)` (500ms), not the spring's own "finished" callback, for the same reason as before — that callback isn't trustworthy for real control flow on Android. Trying the fixed-timeout approach first (rather than tying it to the spring settling) was an explicit, acknowledged tradeoff to revisit later if 500ms doesn't feel right against the spring's actual visual settle time.

Two follow-up tweaks (still 2026-08-07): `TaskCard`'s own whole-card `scaleAnim` depress — initially skipped on the `'task'` side to avoid compounding with `CardTask`'s new depress — is back to applying unconditionally on both faces per explicit user direction (they wanted *both* the whole-card depress *and* the icon's own elastic pop, not just the icon's). And the return spring's overshoot went through two rounds of retuning: `damping`/`stiffness` alone couldn't make it dramatic *and* fast, because with `mass: 1` (Reanimated's default) settle time is governed almost entirely by `damping` alone, while overshoot amount is capped by how far the value actually travels — `DEPRESS_SCALE` 0.9 → 1.0 is only 0.1 of travel, far too little to ever overshoot dramatically regardless of damping. The fix was an explicit positive `velocity` (an artificial initial kick, independent of that small positional gap — like a rubber band getting snapped rather than a shallow dip falling back into place), which is what actually buys the dramatic overshoot; landed initially on `withSpring(1, { damping: 16, stiffness: 280, velocity: 6 })`, then pushed considerably further per explicit user direction ("MUCH FURTHER") to `{ damping: 26, stiffness: 450, velocity: 14 }`.

That still wasn't dramatic enough on-device — a spring's overshoot from a `velocity` kick is a physics *estimate*, not a guaranteed number, and it evidently wasn't translating to the intended effect regardless of how far the inputs were pushed. The velocity approach was abandoned entirely in favor of something deterministic: `POP_PEAK_SCALE` (a named constant) is now an explicit, fixed target the icon snaps to via `withTiming(POP_PEAK_SCALE, { duration, easing: Easing.out(Easing.cubic) })` — guaranteed to actually reach that value, not an estimate — immediately chained (via `withSequence`, no `withDelay`, no pause) into a separate spring for the settle back to rest.

One more round of feedback (peak "a little too big", settle "too slow/not bouncy enough") landed on: `POP_PEAK_SCALE` 1.6 → 1.4, snap-up `duration` 130ms → 100ms, and — the more important fix — the return spring's `{ damping: 14, stiffness: 150 }` (few, slow, barely-visible wobbles) → `{ damping: 20, stiffness: 800 }`. The key realization: for the settle spring specifically, `stiffness` sets how fast each oscillation cycle is and `damping` sets how quickly those oscillations decay away — independent knobs. High stiffness *with* high damping gives several fast, clearly-visible bounces that still die out quickly, which low-stiffness/low-damping (slow cycles, slow decay) can't produce no matter how those two are balanced against each other.

Yet another round (bounce "cuts off too abruptly", wants it to "keep bouncing" longer, decay "a bit faster" but *without* losing bounce count/size) exposed a second, independent issue: Reanimated's spring config exposes an `energyThreshold` (the combined kinetic+potential energy below which it snaps straight to the target) — its default was cutting the animation over before the decay had visibly finished, reading as an abrupt stop rather than a natural fade-out, regardless of damping/stiffness. Explicitly setting `energyThreshold: 0.0001` (much stricter than default) keeps it animating further into the decay tail before calling itself "at rest". Combined with the insight that real-time-to-settle depends on `damping` alone (not `stiffness`, given `mass: 1`) — so raising `stiffness` further fits *more* oscillation cycles into the *same* real-time envelope, "for free," rather than taking longer — landed on `stiffness` 800 → 1200, `damping` 20 → 22 (a modest bump for slightly faster overall decay, as requested, without sacrificing the bounce count/amplitude gained from the higher stiffness). `COMPLETION_HOLD_DURATION` was bumped 550ms → 750ms to give this now-longer, more complete decay room to actually finish before the checkmark reverts and the completion commits.

(Note: Reanimated's spring config in this version doesn't have the older `restDisplacementThreshold`/`restSpeedThreshold` pair some docs/examples reference — `tsc` caught that immediately as a real type error; `energyThreshold` is the single combined-threshold equivalent here.)

Once satisfied with the shape, a final small "a little over the top" pass (2026-08-07) dialed both dimensions down slightly without touching the overall structure: `POP_PEAK_SCALE` 1.4 → 1.2 (via 1.3), and the return spring's `stiffness` 1200 → 1000 / `damping` 22 → 23 (marginally fewer/smaller bounces, marginally faster decay) — `energyThreshold` and `COMPLETION_HOLD_DURATION` left as-is.

The streak badge's own celebration pop (triggered by the separate `useEffect` watching `streakBadgeStyle?.icon === 'fire'`, not the completion press itself) originally used a much softer, slower two-`withSpring` sequence (`damping: 8, stiffness: 100` both ways) left over from before the icon's own pop went through all of the above tuning — read as noticeably sluggish next to the icon's punchier settle. Fixed (2026-08-08) by giving the badge the *exact* same shape as the icon's pop: an explicit snap to `POP_PEAK_SCALE` via `withTiming(100ms, Easing.out(Easing.cubic))`, then the same `withSpring(1, { damping: 23, stiffness: 1000, energyThreshold: 0.0001 })` settle — same constants, same two-phase structure, just triggered off the streak-badge transition instead of the completion press.

**Grid layout fix (2026-08-07):** `HomeScreen`'s `TaskCard` grid was stretching a lone card in an incomplete last row (e.g. 1 leftover card in a 3-column grid) to span the full row width instead of staying the same fixed size as every other card. Root cause: `TaskCard`'s own root `View` had `container: { flex: 1 }` in addition to an explicit inline `{ width: size, height: size }` — the `flex: 1` shorthand implies `flexBasis: 0%`, which in a `flexDirection: 'row'` parent (`HomeScreen`'s `columnWrapperStyle`) takes precedence over `width` for main-axis sizing, so `flexGrow: 1` stretched a row's sole remaining child to fill the leftover space regardless of the explicit width. Fixed by dropping `flex: 1` from `TaskCard`'s `container` style entirely (width/height are always passed explicitly via the `size` prop, so nothing depended on it). Separately, `HomeScreen`'s `row` style (`columnWrapperStyle`) changed `justifyContent` from `'space-between'` to `'center'` — a full row already exactly fills the available width (`cardSize` is derived from it), so this has no visible effect there, but it centers a partial last row's leftover card(s) instead of leaving them left-aligned, per explicit user direction ("ideally odd tile row should be center aligned"). Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` only, per the user's standing instruction to skip browser-based visual verification — not yet confirmed on-device.

## Task Details header: interactive icon, prev/next cycling, bigger streak badge (2026-08-10)

Per explicit user direction ("1- improve the visual emphasis of the current streak status, 2- add prev/next buttons by the icon to cycle through tasks, 3- render the icon with the same icon and interaction as the task card itself"), `TaskHeader` (the shared banner atop `task-detail`'s Calendar/Stats/Streaks tabs) went from a static display to matching `HomeScreen`'s own `TaskCard` in both visuals and interaction.

**Extraction, to reuse rather than reimplement.** `TaskCard.tsx`'s `CardTask` sub-component carried two genuinely reusable, heavily-tuned pieces that were previously locked inside it:
- **`app/components/TaskProgressIcon.tsx`** (new) — the actual icon: the ring (single circle for 1x/day, `RING_SEGMENT_GAP_DEGREES`-gapped arc segments for >1x/day), the partial/solid fill, and the full press-and-hold-to-complete animation (depress, hold-sweep, checkmark pop, elastic settle). Exports the same tuned constants `CardTask` used to define locally (`DEPRESS_SCALE`, `POP_PEAK_SCALE`, `COMPLETION_HOLD_DURATION`, `RING_SEGMENT_GAP_DEGREES`, `INCOMPLETE_RING_OPACITY`) so both call sites stay governed by one set of values instead of two copies that could drift. Deliberately takes resolved `iconSize`/`iconFontSize` as props rather than deriving them from a card-size formula internally — that sizing logic (`showCardBackground`-dependent scaling) is a `CardTask`-specific layout concern, not part of "the icon widget" itself, so `TaskHeader` can pass its own fixed size (96px) without inheriting card-grid assumptions.
- **`app/hooks/useFireCelebration.ts`** (new) — the streak-badge "pop + particle burst" celebration, previously an inline `useEffect` in `CardTask` watching `streakBadgeStyle?.icon === 'fire'`. Returns `{ streakBadgeStyle, badgeAnimatedStyle, showParticles, celebrationKey, handleParticlesComplete }`; both `CardTask` and `TaskHeader` now call this instead of each maintaining their own `badgeScale`/`showParticles`/`celebrationKey` state.

`TaskCard.tsx` itself shrank accordingly (its local `DEPRESS_SCALE`/`POP_PEAK_SCALE`/etc. constants and the icon-animation/badge-celebration `useEffect`s were deleted, not just moved-and-kept) — verified behaviorally unchanged via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (no test coverage exists for component rendering per this project's stated testing scope, so this is a careful line-by-line extraction with the exact original JSX/logic relocated verbatim, not a rewrite).

**1. Streak badge visual emphasis.** Previously a flat `rgba(255,255,255,0.2)` pill regardless of status, with a separately-rendered `clock-outline` icon bolted on for the `expiring` case — a hand-rolled duplicate of the canonical status mapping. Replaced with a direct `getStreakBadgeStyle(task)` call (the same function `TaskCard`'s own front-face badge and `CardStats` already use), so the badge is now colored with the actual status color (red `fire`/orange `clock-outline`/gray `sleep`) instead of staying visually identical across every status. Also sized up considerably (icon 16→22, text 16→20, padding 6/12→8/16, pill radius 16→20) and gained a shadow (`shadowOpacity: 0.2`, `elevation: 3`) for a more "raised" presence against the solid-color header background. Now also runs the same `useFireCelebration` pop-and-particle animation `TaskCard`'s badge does, with its own re-estimated capsule `spawnArea` sized for the bigger badge.

**2. Prev/next task cycling.** `TaskDetailScreen` computes `cyclableTasks` (`tasks.filter(t => !t.archived)`, same store order used everywhere else tasks are listed) and the current task's index within it, and passes `onPrevTask`/`onNextTask` callbacks into `TaskHeader` — omitted entirely (no buttons render) when there's only one task to cycle between. Navigation uses `router.setParams({ taskId: ... })`, not `router.push`, specifically so switching tasks updates the *same* screen instance rather than stacking a new modal on top — this preserves `activeTab`'s local state (you stay on whichever tab you were viewing) and avoids an ever-growing modal stack from repeated cycling. Wraps around at both ends (`(index + length) % length`), since "cycle through tasks" implies wrapping, not stopping at the first/last task. Buttons are `chevron-left`/`chevron-right`, flanking the icon in a new `iconRow`.

**3. Interactive icon.** `TaskHeader` now owns its own `progress`/`isPressed`/`isCompleting` state and a `Pressable` wrapping `TaskProgressIcon`, reimplemented at this screen's level (not by reaching into `TaskCard`/`CardTask` directly, since this is a single large icon in a header, not a flippable card — there's no flip/calendar-face/stats-face concept here). `handleCompleted` mirrors `HomeScreen.handleLongPressTask` exactly: success haptic, `completeTask`, a toast with an `Undo` action wired to `undoCompleteTask`. An already-completed task's icon still renders correctly (solid fill, via `TaskProgressIcon`'s own `completed` check) but press/long-press are no-ops — unlike `TaskCard`'s own already-completed handling (which navigates to `task-detail`), there's nothing further to navigate to since this *is* `task-detail`. A `useEffect(() => setIsCompleting(false), [task])`, identical in spirit to `TaskCard`'s own, guards against stale completion-animation state carrying over when `task` changes out from under the component — both on a real completion (the store mutation swaps in a new task object with updated stats) and when cycling to a different task via the new prev/next buttons.

**Follow-up bug (2026-08-10), reported directly against the real header: the icon was effectively invisible.** `TaskProgressIcon` draws its ring/fill directly in `task.color` — fine sitting on `TaskCard`'s neutral `colors.surface` background, but `TaskHeader`'s own background *is* `task.color`, so the ring (and, while incomplete, the icon glyph itself) rendered in the exact same color as what it sat on. Two candidate fixes were weighed: recolor the icon for this one context (e.g. force white/neutral strokes when on a task-colored background), or give it a neutral backdrop to contrast against, matching how it already works on the card. Went with the backdrop — recoloring would mean the icon is no longer really showing the task's own color the way the card's version does, and would need `TaskProgressIcon` to know about (or be told) whatever background it happens to sit on, complicating a component whose whole value is being a faithful, unmodified reuse. New `iconBackdrop` style — a `colors.surface`-filled circle (deliberately the theme's actual surface color, not a generic white, so it reproduces the exact contrast conditions `DEFAULT_COLORS` were already chosen against — see that array's own comment on being checked against both light and dark surfaces) sized `ICON_SIZE + 20` so it still reads as a visible "plate" around the icon even in the completed state, where the ring's own solid fill circle nearly fills its full bounding box — an exact backdrop/icon size match would leave no visible border between the two. Also picked up a subtle shadow (`shadowOpacity: 0.15`, `elevation: 3`) for a "raised" feel consistent with the streak badge's own shadow treatment added alongside it.

**Tried and reverted (2026-08-10):** per explicit user direction, the backdrop was briefly made semi-transparent (`tinycolor(colors.surface).setAlpha(0.7).toRgbString()` instead of the plain opaque hex string) to soften the "harsh"/stark-cutout look. Rejected on sight ("Nah, don't like that") — reverted back to the plain fully-opaque `colors.surface` fill described above. Worth knowing if revisiting this backdrop again: a straight opacity reduction on `colors.surface` wasn't the right direction here, so a different softening approach (if wanted later) shouldn't just retry the same knob.

**Backdrop removed entirely (2026-08-10), per explicit user direction ("I really don't love the background circle... instead make the icon colors use inverted white (and the header background color) in this context? This also means previous white becomes colored").** Rather than giving the icon a neutral surface to sit on, `TaskProgressIcon` now accepts two optional color props — `color` (defaults to `task.color`, unset for every existing caller so `TaskCard`'s own look is byte-for-byte unchanged) for the ring/fill/incomplete-state icon glyph, and `onColor` (defaults to `'#fff'`) for whatever renders *on top of* a solid `color` fill — the completed-state icon glyph and the checkmark. Every internal `task.color`/hardcoded `'#fff'` reference in the SVG and icon rendering was replaced with `primaryColor`/`onColor` accordingly.

`TaskHeader` now passes `color="#fff"` and `onColor={task.color}` — a genuine inversion of the two roles, not just a different pair of colors: the ring/track/segments/progress-fill/press-hold-sweep all draw in white (visible directly against the header's own `task.color` background, no backdrop needed), and the completed state's solid fill circle becomes a solid *white* circle instead of solid `task.color` — which is exactly why the completed-state icon glyph and checkmark (previously always white, since they always sat on a solid `task.color` fill) needed to flip to `task.color` too, to stay legible against the now-white fill underneath them ("previous white becomes colored", per the user's own framing). The `iconBackdrop` style and its wrapping `View` were deleted outright, not just left unused. `TaskProgressIcon`'s existing `React.memo` and all of `TaskCard`'s own rendering are unaffected, since the new props are optional and every existing call site (`CardTask`) omits them, resolving to the exact same defaults it already relied on.

**Header restructured into two rows (2026-08-10), per explicit user direction, after being asked directly whether the element order made sense.** Previously Back/Edit flanked the *entire* icon+name+frequency+streak-badge stack (`centerContent`) as one tall row, vertically centered against however tall that whole block happened to be — which floated Back/Edit at an inconsistent height (varying per task on whether the streak badge or prev/next chevrons rendered) and left them feeling disconnected from the title itself, unlike every other screen's header. Recommended and implemented splitting it: a slim `headerTop` nav row — Back / task name (`numberOfLines={1}`, `flex: 1`, centered, 18px/700 — sized and weighted to match `DashboardScreen`'s own `headerTitle`, just kept white since this header's background is `task.color` rather than `colors.background`) / Edit — now matches the exact `headerTop` pattern `DashboardScreen`/`SettingsScreen`/`ArchivedTasksScreen` already use, giving Back/Edit a fixed, predictable position pinned to the title rather than to the icon block's own varying height. Below that, `iconSection` centers the icon row (prev/icon/next, unchanged) and a new `badgesRow` — frequency badge and streak badge now sit side by side horizontally under the icon instead of stacked vertically in a column with the (now-removed) title, since the title moved up into the nav row and no longer needs to anchor them. `centerContent`/`titleContainer`/`title` styles were replaced with `headerTop`/`headerTitle`/`iconSection`/`badgesRow` accordingly — no leftover unused styles.

**Follow-up (2026-08-14): tapping `badgesRow` now opens a dismissable plain-English status summary, closing a standing `TODO.md` item.** Per the item's own wording: "tap the row of items that shows the frequency, streak, status, etc" and get "a concise plain english readable sentence summing up the current state of the task," built via "branching/dynamic logic," not a single templated fill-in.

First pass was a single pure function, `buildTaskStatusSummary`, composing one flowing 2-3 sentence paragraph (schedule + today's status + a best-streak mention, all blended together). **Reworked the same day, per explicit user direction, into three deliberately separate elements instead**: *"I think I want to preserve the 3 elements rather than try to blend them all together: Schedule, Status, Best? ... First the schedule is shown clearly (fairly redundant to what's in the task header but repeated for convenience). Next is the current status, which should include whether today is due, the streak status, basically the branching language you've come up with around that. Third element, if it's best streak, is just a best streak acknowledgment."* Before rewording, the exact permutation table (every due/completion/streakStatus/quota combination the branching logic covers, with the wording each produced) was written out and shared for direct review, rather than guessing at which lines needed to change.

`buildTaskStatusSummary` was replaced with `getTaskStatusInfo(task, today?)` in `app/utils/taskStatusSummary.ts` (unit-tested, `__tests__/utils/taskStatusSummary.test.ts`, 12 cases), returning a `TaskStatusInfo` struct instead of one string:
```ts
interface TaskStatusBadge { icon: MaterialCommunityIconName; color: string; text: string }
interface TaskStatusInfo { scheduleLabel: string; status: TaskStatusBadge; best: TaskStatusBadge | null }
```
- `scheduleLabel` is just `formatFrequencyLabel(task)` directly (`"Daily"`, `"3x weekly"`, ...) -- no sentence-wrapping needed once it's its own labeled row instead of being woven into paragraph grammar (which is what forced the earlier version's awkward `"Scheduled: {label}."` fragment in the first place).
- `status` is the same branching logic as before (not due → completed, single- vs. multi-rep → partially completed → nothing done yet, branching further on `streakStatus`/quota-vs-due-day), but trimmed to a short standalone line with no streak-count or best-streak language mixed in (e.g. `"Action needed today to keep your streak alive."`, `"Completed all 3 reps for today."`) -- and now carries its own icon/color per branch, reusing `getStreakBadgeStyle`'s (`streaks.ts`) existing red/orange/gray language (`#FF6B6B`/`#FFA726`/`#90A4AE`) rather than inventing a second color scheme for the same "how healthy is this streak" signal.
- `best` is `null` unless `currentStreak === bestStreak` (`bestStreak > 0`) -- no longer a general "your record is N days" fact shown every time, purely a celebratory acknowledgment (`"This is your best streak yet — 10 days!"`) in this app's own established gold (`#FFD700`, the same "best streak" trophy color used throughout `DashboardStreaksView`/`TaskCard`/`TaskStatsScreen`/this same header's own trophy icon).

**UI**: `TaskHeader.tsx`'s `badgesRow` `View` became a `TouchableOpacity` (`accessibilityLabel="Habit status summary"`), opening a `Modal` — the app's first use of React Native's `Modal` component. A plain absolute-positioned overlay wouldn't have worked here: `TaskHeader` isn't mounted at the screen root (it's nested inside `TaskDetailScreen`, sitting above the tab content), so an absolute `View` declared inside it would only ever cover `TaskHeader`'s own bounds, not the Calendar/Stats/Streaks content below — `Modal` renders as a true full-screen layer regardless of where it's declared, which is what lets tapping anywhere (including that lower content) dismiss it. `transparent` + a manual semi-transparent backdrop (rather than the platform's own opaque default) keeps it reading as a lightweight popover rather than a full navigation; `onRequestClose` covers the Android back button for free. The card itself is a nested `Pressable` with a no-op `onPress` sitting inside the backdrop's own dismiss-`Pressable` — the same "inner Pressable swallows the touch so it doesn't fall through to the outer one" pattern already proven in `AchievementCelebration`'s unlock-history list, so tapping the card itself doesn't dismiss it. The card's title row shows the task's own icon (`task.icon`, not a generic "info" glyph, per direct user follow-up) in a `task.color` circle next to the task name, then three rows below -- Schedule/Status/(optional) Best -- each a small icon-in-a-tinted-circle plus its own text line, letting the popover be scanned at a glance rather than read as one paragraph.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (254/254). Not yet confirmed on-device — worth a look at: the popover's positioning/backdrop dismissal feeling right on a real device (first use of `Modal` in this app), that tapping through to the tab content behind it actually dismisses as intended, the per-row icon/color tints reading clearly against `colors.surface` in both themes, and reading through a few real tasks in different states to confirm the three rows read as a coherent, scannable summary rather than three disconnected facts.

**Follow-up (2026-08-14): the "completed today" branch of Status was a flat "Completed for today" with no streak info at all, per direct user report -- fixed to fold the streak back in, without re-treading into Best's own territory.** Now reads `"Completed today, keeping your {N}-day streak going."` when `currentStreak > 0` (dropping the streak clause entirely for the `currentStreak === 0` edge case), and specifically calls out the one real case where "completed today" doesn't mean the streak is fully secure: a quota-based task (`days_per_week`/`days_per_month`) can still read `streakStatus: 'expiring'` right after today's own reps are done, if every remaining day this period is now required to hit quota -- `"Completed today — but every remaining day this period is still needed."` `expired`/`never_started` were deliberately left out of this branch's own switch (unlike the "nothing done yet" branch, which handles all four) -- completing a due day always resolves the engine's own `streakStatus` back to `up_to_date` by the time stats recompute, so those two aren't actually reachable alongside a same-day completion. 3 new tests (14 total in this file) -- the "up_to_date" case, the quota-tail "expiring" case, and the zero-streak edge case. Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (256/256).

**Follow-up (2026-08-14), a batch of smaller adjustments after reviewing the full permutation table together, all in the same sitting:**
- **Icon**: every "up-to-date/active" outcome of the completed-today branch switched from a generic `check-circle-outline` to `fire` -- matching `getStreakBadgeStyle`'s own icon for the identical status elsewhere in the app, per direct user direction ("up to date streaks should use streak icon, not check circle").
- **New "streak started" branch**: `currentStreak === 1` (completed today, not expiring) now reads `"Completed today — streak started!"` instead of the slightly-odd `"...keeping your 1-day streak going"` a streak that just began would otherwise get.
- **Row 7's wording** (`expiring`, nothing done yet) changed from the flatter `"Action needed today to keep your streak alive."` to a more direct imperative, `"Complete today to keep your streak alive!"`.
- **Schedule became a full sentence, plus the task's own created-at date.** `formatFrequency.ts` gained two new exports: `getFrequencyBaseLabel` (the exact `base` computation `formatFrequencyLabel` already had inline, extracted so a second function could build off the identical value instead of re-deriving which frequency/daysOfWeek combination means what) and `formatFrequencySentence` (wraps that same base in real sentence grammar -- `"Happens every day."` / `"Happens on weekdays."` / `"Happens 3 times a week."` / `"Happens on Mon, Wed, Fri, 2 times a day."` -- with the identical "Daily collapses once a times-per-day count is shown" rule `formatFrequencyLabel` already has, so the two never disagree on that). `TaskStatusInfo.scheduleLabel` was renamed `scheduleSentence` and now reads `` `${formatFrequencySentence(task)} Started ${format(parseISO(task.createdAt), 'MMM d, yyyy')}.` `` -- per direct user request to "expand the frequency label into a full-ish sentence" plus "add a task created at date," landing both in the same row rather than adding a fourth element, since "when this started" is really part of the same "what's the schedule" fact as "how often."
- **A new conditional quota-explainer row**, per direct user request ("for quota based tasks, I want a brief little explanation about how quota and streaks are calculated") -- `TaskStatusInfo.quotaExplainer: string | null`, populated only for `days_per_week`/`days_per_month` tasks. Renders as its own row between Schedule and Status when present, in a deliberately quieter/smaller style (`summaryExplainerText`, 12px vs. the other rows' 14px) since it's supplementary context, not a headline fact like the other three.

  **Wording went through one real correction**: the first draft ("This one tracks by week, not by day — as long as you complete it N times before the week ends, your streak stays alive even if you skip individual days.") was factually backwards -- this app's own established design (see the "A note on `streak-N` titles saying 'Day'" entry earlier in this doc) is that streak *length* is always a plain day count regardless of frequency; a quota only decides which days count toward it, it never changes the counting unit to weeks/months. Per direct user request for something shorter that "leads with the quota requirement, not streak count," landed on: `` `You just need ${N} days a week — your streak still counts by day.` `` (or "a month"/`daysPerMonth`) -- states the actual requirement first, then corrects the exact misconception the original draft would have introduced.
- **Best's icon dropped its tinted circle background**, per direct user direction ("the best icon can be just a trophy, no circle halo needed for that one") -- unlike Schedule/Status/the quota explainer, which all still render their icon inside a `summaryRowIcon` tinted circle, Best now renders a bare, slightly larger (22px vs. 16px) trophy directly, width-matched (28px, via a new `summaryBestIcon` style) to the other rows' circles so the text column still lines up.
- The modal's own title icon (added the same day, per direct user note "the modal title should include the task icon") uses `task.icon` in a `task.color` circle -- confirmed already correct from the popover's very first pass, no change needed here.

New/updated tests: `__tests__/utils/formatFrequency.test.ts` gained a `describe('formatFrequencySentence', ...)` block (7 cases, covering every base shape plus the times-per-day collapse/combine rules); `taskStatusSummary.test.ts` gained coverage for `scheduleSentence`'s full format (including the created-at date) and both `quotaExplainer` branches, plus updated assertions for the new icon/wording throughout. Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (267/267, up from 256). Not yet confirmed on-device -- this is now the third layout/content pass on this same popover in one session, so worth a genuinely careful look this time: four possible rows (Schedule, the conditional quota explainer, Status, the conditional Best) stacking cleanly with `summaryCard`'s own `gap: 12`, the bare Best trophy's 28px-wide alignment actually lining up with the circled icons above it, and the fuller schedule sentence + quota explainer text not making the card feel overloaded now that it's grown from three short lines to as many as five.

**Follow-up (2026-08-14), two more wording corrections after further review, both about cutting redundancy rather than adding anything new:**
- **Quota explainer reworded once more and dropped the task name entirely**, per direct user direction ("reword for better flow" plus, moments later, "lets drop the {task name}, feels too redundant" once the task name was actually in front of them). Landed on: `` `Needs ${N} days a week. As long as you reach that, your streak — always counted in days — stays alive.` `` (or "a month"/`daysPerMonth`) -- leads with the bare requirement as its own short sentence, then a second sentence stating the day-count guarantee, with no task name at all now that the popover's own title row (task icon + name) already establishes which task this explainer belongs to.
- **Best dropped its own streak-count number entirely**, per direct user direction ("Best streak doesn't need to repeat the streak count, that should always be covered by the status") -- Status already states the current streak length whenever there is one (e.g. "keeping your 5-day streak going"), so Best repeating the same number a second time was pure duplication, not a second useful fact. `` `This is your best streak yet — ${N} day${N===1?'':'s'}!` `` collapsed to a flat, always-identical `'This is your best streak yet!'` -- no longer needs `currentStreak` in its own template at all (`getTaskStatusInfo`'s local `currentStreak` var stays in scope regardless, still needed for `isOnBestStreak`'s own comparison).

Both changes are pure copy -- no new branches, no new fields. Test updates: the two `quotaExplainer` tests' expected strings updated to the name-less wording; the old "shows a best-streak badge only when currently tied with the record" + "uses singular 'day' for a 1-day best streak" pair (the latter testing behavior that no longer exists, since the text is now invariant) collapsed into one test asserting the exact static string. Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (266/266, down one from the removed now-redundant singular-day test -- not a coverage gap, since the case it existed for, N-dependent pluralization, was deliberately removed from the code itself).

**Follow-up (2026-08-14): a non-due day that gets completed anyway now gets its own acknowledgment, instead of the flat "nothing needed" text, per explicit user direction ("When there's no due today, but there is a completion, I want there to be acknowledgement text, because this actually counts for the streak, but we don't need to count day reps").** Matches this app's own established engine design directly (see the `daily`/`specific_days_of_week` streak-walk notes elsewhere in this doc): a non-due day that's completed still adds to the running streak as a bonus day, so the popover was previously understating it -- the exact same "nothing needed right now" text as a genuinely empty non-due day, with no signal that the completion actually mattered.

The three-way "streak started / continuing / zero-streak edge case" wording that the due-and-completed branch already had was extracted into a shared `describeCompletedToday(leadClause, currentStreak)` helper (only the lead clause differs between the two call sites) rather than duplicated a second time -- matching this codebase's own consistent bias toward exactly this kind of extraction once the same three-way branch is needed twice (see `getQuotaPeriodInfo`'s own extraction history, elsewhere in this doc, for the identical reasoning). The non-due branch now checks `isCompletedToday` first: `describeCompletedToday('Not due today, but you completed it anyway', currentStreak)` on a full completion, falling back to the original "Not scheduled for today — nothing needed right now." otherwise.

Per the "we don't need to count day reps" half of the request, this deliberately skips the reps-counting nuance the due-and-completed branch has (`"Completed all N reps today"` vs plain `"Completed today"`) -- reps only matter for judging whether a *due* day met its own quota; a non-due day is binary for the streak engine (it either qualifies as a full completed bonus day or it doesn't), so a partial multi-rep completion on a non-due day is treated the same as no completion at all, still reading as "nothing needed."

4 new tests (20 total in this file, up from 16): the pre-existing "is due-unaware when not a scheduled day" test renamed for clarity (it's specifically the non-completed case now that a completed one exists alongside it) and left otherwise unchanged; new coverage for the freshly-started-streak wording, the continuing-streak wording, and the "partial reps on a non-due day still reads as nothing needed" simplification. Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (269/269, up from 266).

## Stats screen chart performance (2026-08-13)

`TaskStatsView`/`DashboardStatsView` were slow to render regardless of how little data a task/task-set had — diagnosed (not guessed) via direct source inspection before touching anything: `grep -rn "React.memo\|PureComponent\|shouldComponentUpdate" node_modules/react-native-chart-kit/dist/*.js` returned zero matches — the library has no internal render-skipping of its own, so every `LineChart`/`BarChart` re-render fully redraws every SVG path/gradient/label from scratch regardless of whether its props actually changed. Two things in these two screens made that expensive redraw happen far more often than necessary: each chart's own `chartConfig` was a fresh, unmemoized object literal spread together inline in JSX on every render (a brand-new object reference every time, even when nothing in it changed), and each screen's own `onLayout`-driven `chartAreaWidth` correction (see the pre-existing "measured, not guessed" comment on that state) triggers at least one extra full re-render shortly after mount, redrawing every chart on screen a second time for a change most of them don't actually care about (the day-of-week/hour-of-day histograms' bar count doesn't depend on width the way `getBarPercentage` might suggest — width only rescales, it doesn't change the underlying draw complexity, but the *whole chart* still fully re-executes either way).

**Fix 1: shared, `React.memo`'d chart-card components.** New `app/components/StatsCharts.tsx` extracts `CompletionsOverTimeChartCard` (the LineChart/BarChart cumulative toggle) and `HistogramChartCard` (the plain day-of-week/hour-of-day bar charts) — both screens' own chart JSX was byte-for-byte identical apart from color source (`task.color` vs. a fixed accent), so this is one shared implementation, not two independently-tuned ones that could drift (matching this codebase's own established bias against exactly that kind of duplication — see `getStreakBadgeStyle`/`getQuotaPeriodInfo`'s own extraction histories). Each card computes its own `baseChartConfig` via `useMemo` keyed on `colors` (itself already stable across unrelated re-renders per `useThemeColors`'s own established memoization), and is wrapped in `React.memo` so a parent re-render that doesn't change any of its actual props — `chartData`, `chartWidth`, `color`, `isCumulative` — skips the redraw entirely. Both screens already had their `chartData`/`dayOfWeekChartData`/`hourOfDayChartData` objects properly `useMemo`'d (a prior pass in this app's own performance-audit history), so their reference stability was already correct; the toggle's `onPress` handler needed a new `useCallback`-wrapped `handleToggleCumulative` in each screen (previously an inline arrow, a fresh closure every render — the same class of prop-identity churn that once defeated `TaskCard`'s own `React.memo`, documented at length in the "State management" section above) to keep from silently reintroducing the exact problem the memoization was meant to fix.

**Fix 2: `app/components/LazyMount.tsx` — a React Native answer to "defer rendering until scrolled into view," matching what a browser page would do with `IntersectionObserver`.** RN has no native IntersectionObserver equivalent, but the pieces to build one already exist and are already trusted elsewhere in this app: `View.measureLayout(relativeToNativeNode, onSuccess, onFail)` measures a view's position relative to a *specific* ancestor regardless of how many plain, non-offsetting Views sit in between — the identical technique `useOnboardingTarget.ts` already uses (deliberately not `measureInWindow`, for the same reason documented there: window coordinates don't reliably line up with a scrollable container's own content coordinates, especially with this app's edge-to-edge Android handling). `LazyMount` wraps a chart card, measures its own Y position relative to a `contentRef` (a plain `View` wrapping the *entire* scrollable content, added as the ScrollView's single child in both screens specifically so this measurement has a stable, content-offset-0 anchor to measure against), and compares that against the scroll container's own `scrollY`/`viewportHeight` (read from the parent's `onScroll`/`onLayout`, not tracked per-`LazyMount` — many `LazyMount`s sharing one scroll container don't each need their own subscription) plus a `rootMargin` (default 200px, so a chart starts rendering a bit before it's technically visible rather than popping in right at the viewport edge, matching `IntersectionObserver`'s own `rootMargin` option). Once a chart has been visible once, it renders forever after — this is lazy *initial* render, not virtualization/windowing; re-mounting a chart every time it scrolls in and out would cost more than rendering it once and leaving it, and would throw away any local state it holds (e.g. its own `isCumulative` toggle). Before its first real mount, a fixed-height placeholder `View` reserves the chart's approximate footprint so the rest of the scroll content doesn't visibly jump once it finally renders.

Both screens' three `react-native-chart-kit`-based cards (Completions Over Time, By Day of Week, By Time of Day) are wrapped in `LazyMount`; `DashboardStatsView`'s "Total Completions" per-task horizontal-bar breakdown is deliberately left eager/unwrapped — it's built from plain `View`s, not `react-native-chart-kit`, so it isn't part of the redraw problem this fix targets, and it's also the section's own first, most-likely-already-visible item, so deferring it would have no benefit.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (219/219, unaffected — pure extraction/memoization + a new additive component, no `utils/` logic touched). Not yet confirmed on-device — worth prioritizing given this is squarely a *performance* fix, the one class of change this app's own stated testing scope (pure `utils/` functions only) has no automated way to verify: whether the two Stats screens actually feel snappier to open/scroll now, that `LazyMount`'s placeholder-to-real-content swap doesn't visibly jank the scroll position, and that a chart genuinely renders once it's scrolled near (not stuck showing its placeholder) across both light data (a brand-new task) and heavier data (a task/task-set with a long history).

## Free-tier task cap (2026-08-08)

Per `MONETIZATION.md`'s planned Pro tier ("unlimited active tasks" as the main paid lever), a free-tier cap of **6 active (non-archived) tasks** is implemented now, deliberately ahead of the Pro unlock itself — per explicit user direction, shipping the cap before there's anything to buy means it's never a feature taken away from existing users, only ever something the app has always done. `app/utils/taskLimits.ts` is the single source of truth: `MAX_ACTIVE_TASKS = 6`, `getActiveTaskCount(tasks)`, `hasReachedActiveTaskLimit(tasks)` (both operate on the full `tasks` array, filtering out `archived` internally), and a shared `ACTIVE_TASK_LIMIT_MESSAGE` toast string — covered by `__tests__/utils/taskLimits.test.ts`. There was no pre-existing shared "active tasks" helper anywhere in the codebase (every screen inlined its own `.filter(t => !t.archived)`), so this is a new small pure-logic module rather than a refactor of an existing one.

Three real enforcement points, all found by tracing every mutator that can turn a task active or add a new task record (`app/stores/taskStore.ts`'s `addTask`, `restoreTask`, `restoreDeletedTask`, `importTasks`):

1. **`AddTaskScreen.tsx` (creation)** — the actual authority. `atTaskLimit` (`!isEditing && hasReachedActiveTaskLimit(tasks)`, editing never changes the count so it's exempt) disables the Save button and shows an inline `taskLimitText` message below it, mirroring the existing `isNameValid` pattern exactly (memoized boolean gate, disables Save, plus a redundant guard-and-toast at the top of `handleSave` in case the button is somehow bypassed).
2. **`HomeScreen.tsx` (FAB + empty-state "Add a Habit" button)** — both now go through a shared `handleAddTask` that checks the cap *before* navigating, so a capped user gets an immediate toast instead of a dead-end trip to a form they can't submit. (The empty-state button technically can never be at cap — it only renders when there are zero active tasks at all — but routed through the same handler anyway for consistency, at no cost.)
3. **`ArchivedTasksScreen.tsx` (restore)** — explicit "Restore" taps are the one standing (non-ephemeral) path that could otherwise be used to cycle around the cap indefinitely (archive to make room, restore anytime), so it's gated the same way: a `handleRestore` wrapper checks the cap first and shows the same toast instead of calling `restoreTask` directly.

**Deliberately left uncapped, both considered and rejected as enforcement points:**
- **Import (`SettingsScreen.tsx`'s `handleImport`, both merge and replace modes)** — `mergeTaskLists` in `app/utils/importExport.ts` can freely push someone over the cap (any incoming task with a non-matching `id` is added outright, uncapped), but import was left alone on purpose: `MONETIZATION.md`'s free tier explicitly states data export/import are "deliberately never gated... paywalling access to a user's own data would undermine the app's core 'your data is yours' promise." Blocking or trimming an import to enforce the cap would be exactly that. If this creates a real bypass in practice (import as a way around the cap for good), it's a tradeoff explicitly accepted in favor of the data-ownership principle, not an oversight.
- **`restoreDeletedTask` / `restoreTask` as invoked from a toast's Undo action** (in `AddTaskScreen.tsx`'s `handleDelete`/`handleArchiveToggle`) — not gated, since these reverse the user's *own* immediate action within the toast's ~8s window; the only way this could exceed the cap is creating several new tasks in that same handful of seconds right after deleting/archiving one, then tapping Undo — an edge case judged not worth complicating the Undo UX with a possible failure mode for.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` only (83/83 passing) — not yet confirmed on-device, per the project's standing convention for changes like this.

**Follow-up (2026-08-08): no "free"/"Pro" language in the UI yet, per explicit user direction — there's nothing to upgrade to until Pro actually exists.** `ACTIVE_TASK_LIMIT_MESSAGE` originally read "Free plan supports up to 6 active tasks..."; changed to "You can have up to 6 active tasks. Archive one to add another." — states the limit as just how the app works right now, with no reference to a tier system the app doesn't have yet. The doc-only mentions of "free tier"/"Pro" in `MONETIZATION.md` and code comments (`taskLimits.ts`, explaining *why* the cap exists) are unaffected — this was specifically about user-facing strings.

## Congratulation stingers / achievements (2026-08-10)

Addressed a standing `TODO.md` wishlist item (New best streak / Perfect day / First streak / Milestone completions) as a real feature: event-triggered celebration popups ("stingers"), a persisted history browsable as a "Trophy Case," and a Settings toggle. Deliberately **purely cosmetic for now** — a future point system may hang off this same recorded data, but wasn't part of this build, per explicit user direction. Planned via a full Plan Mode session (validated by a Plan agent that re-read every relevant file) before any code was written — see the two real bugs it caught, below.

**Twelve achievement kinds**, defined in a new pure module, `app/utils/achievements.ts` (mirrors `streaks.ts`/`taskLimits.ts`'s no-React/no-store-imports testability constraint): `first-streak`, `new-best-streak`, five current-*streak-length* tiers (`streak-10`/`25`/`50`/`100`/`1000`), three lifetime-*total-completions* tiers (`milestone-10`/`100`/`1000`), `perfect-day` (global), and `comeback`. `streak-N` and `milestone-N` are deliberately two separate families, not one — reaching a 10-day streak and hitting 10 total completions aren't the same signal for a non-daily task (e.g. a 2x/week task can rack up 10 completions in ~5 weeks while never holding a 10-day streak at all). All twelve share one gold (`#FFD700`) accent — the same color already used app-wide for "best streak" (`DashboardStreaksView`, `TaskCard`, `TaskHeader`, `TaskStatsScreen`) — rather than a color per kind, and `streak-N` deliberately reuses the `fire` icon across all five tiers (the same glyph `getStreakBadgeStyle` already uses for an active streak) rather than a different icon per tier, so the new feature's icon language stays integrated with the app's existing "streak = fire" convention instead of introducing a separate one.

`ACHIEVEMENT_META: Record<AchievementKind, { icon, title, describe }>` is the single source of icon/title/description text — both `AchievementStinger` and `TrophiesScreen` read from it, so the two never drift out of sync with each other.

**Two real bugs caught during planning, before any code existed:**
1. **A spam bug in the original `new-best-streak` design.** `streaks.ts` defines `bestStreak = Math.max(longestRunIncludingClose(history), currentStreak)` — for a brand-new task with no prior closed run, this makes `bestStreak === currentStreak` on *every single day* of its first unbroken climb (there's nothing to compare against yet). A naive "`currentStreak > previous bestStreak`" condition would therefore have fired on day 4, 5, 6, 7... of any new task's very first streak, forever — exactly the spam the feature was supposed to avoid, and redundant with the app's existing ambient celebration (`useFireCelebration`'s particle burst already fires on every streak-extending completion). Fixed by requiring a genuine record to both *exist* and be *broken*: `prevTask.stats.currentStreak < prevTask.stats.bestStreak` (a real prior ceiling existed above where this run started) `&& nextTask.stats.currentStreak > prevTask.stats.bestStreak` (this completion just crossed it) `&& nextTask.stats.currentStreak >= 2` (filters trivially small "records," matching `first-streak`'s own threshold). This only fires on a genuine beat-your-old-record moment — never during a first-ever still-climbing streak, since `prevCurrent === prevBest` always holds there, failing the first clause. Regression-tested directly in `__tests__/utils/achievements.test.ts` (a 10-day simulated first climb, asserting `new-best-streak` never once appears).
2. **A UI collision the original design hadn't addressed.** All three completion call sites (`HomeScreen.handleLongPressTask`, `TaskHeader.handleCompleted`, `TaskCalendarScreen.handleDayPress`) already show a bottom-anchored `ToastBanner` ("Task completed" + Undo) on every completion — the exact same event that can also earn an achievement. Fixed by anchoring the new Stinger at the **top** of the screen instead of the bottom, so both can coexist without fighting for space or hit-testing.

**Trigger point: `taskStore.ts`'s `completeTask`, and only that mutator.** Not `restoreCompletion`/`uncompleteTask`/`undoCompleteTask`/`importTasks` — those are corrections or bulk data operations, not a user completing a task right now. `completeTask` now captures `prevTask`/`nextTask` from inside the same `.map()` that performs the mutation (guaranteeing the exact before/after pair for *this* task, no risk of racing a concurrent update to some other task in the array), then — after `set()` completes — calls `useAchievementsStore.getState().recordCompletionAchievements(prevTask, nextTask, get().tasks, date)`, the same cross-store-call pattern `importTasks` already uses for `useLastImportStore.getState().setLastImport(...)`. This covers all three completion call sites for free, with zero changes needed at any of them. Critically, `date` is the date the completion was actually *for* (defaulting to `new Date()`, but `TaskCalendarScreen.handleDayPress` can pass an arbitrary past date when backfilling) — `perfect-day`'s due-check and dedup key are evaluated against that date, not `new Date()`, so backfilling a past day is judged against whether *that* day was perfect, not today.

**New store, `app/stores/achievementsStore.ts`** — `achievements: Achievement[]` (persisted, AsyncStorage key `'achievements'`) + `pendingCelebrations: Achievement[]` (NOT persisted — an ephemeral queue `AchievementStinger` drains one at a time, deliberately separate from `achievements` itself rather than e.g. an `unseen` flag on each record, so the celebration UI has nothing to do with history bookkeeping at all). Since `'achievements'` is a brand-new AsyncStorage key with no pre-Zustand legacy shape to migrate (unlike `tasks`/`appSettings`/`lastImport`, which predate the Zustand migration and need custom `PersistStorage` to detect their old bare-value shape), this store uses plain `createJSONStorage(() => AsyncStorage)` rather than the other three stores' custom migration-aware storage object. `recordCompletionAchievements` builds the "already earned" dedup set from `ONE_TIME_KINDS`-filtered history, calls the pure detector, assigns each result an id (`` `${Date.now()}-${index}` ``, not bare `Date.now()` — a single completion can legitimately earn more than one achievement in the same synchronous call, e.g. crossing a streak tier and a completion-count tier together, and millisecond-resolution timestamps alone could collide across that batch) and `earnedAt`, then appends to **both** `achievements` and `pendingCelebrations` unconditionally — the celebration setting (below) only ever gates whether the popup shows, never whether history gets recorded.

**Hydration**: joins `_layout.tsx`'s `AppGate` gate as a fourth `hasHydrated` check, alongside settings/tasks/lastImport — without it, a completion firing before this store's own async AsyncStorage read resolves would append to an in-memory empty array that the real persisted data then silently overwrites moments later, losing the just-earned trophy.

**Settings**: new `achievementCelebrationsEnabled` field (default `true`) on `settingsStore.ts`, same 3-line pattern as `showCardBackground`/`showTaskName`/`showTaskCounter`. A new "Achievements" section in `SettingsScreen.tsx` (placed after "Task Cards," before "Data" — groups with the other personalization toggles): a switch row ("Celebrate Achievements") plus a nav row ("Trophy Case" → `/trophies`, trailing a live `achievements.length` count, matching the About row's trailing-version-text pattern).

**Celebration UI, `app/components/AchievementStinger.tsx`** — mounted once at the app root (`_layout.tsx`, sibling of `<ToastBanner />` inside `<ToastProvider>`, already under `GestureHandlerRootView`).
