**Follow-up (2026-08-12): two of the three new gemstones reworked, per direct user feedback ("1- Sapphire is too close to Diamond (blue) -- can you pick a different colored gemstone? 2- Redo Amythyst, I like the idea of a purple gemstone but gold makes no sense, make it a more consistent vibrant purple gemstone theme"), plus a small mid-turn copy fix.**

**`perfect-day`: Sapphire -> Emerald.** A real, fair complaint -- Sapphire's royal blue (`#1565C0`) and Diamond's cyan (`#00B8D4`) are both blue-family, and sitting in the same 14-color catalog they read as redundant rather than as two distinct gems. Replaced with **Emerald** (`base: '#00A86B'`, `glow: '#66BB6A'`, `accent: '#E8F5E9'`) -- a vivid green with a pale mint sparkle. "Flawless" pairs with an emerald exactly as naturally as it did with a sapphire, and green was otherwise completely absent from the whole 14-kind catalog before this, so it reads as clearly its own thing rather than close to anything else in the palette (unlike blue, which was already spoken for by Diamond).

**`comeback`: Amethyst's mismatched gold accent replaced with a matching purple one.** The prior Amethyst entry had simply inherited its hex values wholesale from this kind's earlier name/story, Rebirth ("a deep violet night warming through a magenta-violet glow into a bright gold-dawn accent") -- consistent as a *narrative*, but not as a *gemstone*, since real amethyst has no gold in it at all. Per direct follow-up, `accent` changed from `#FFD600` (gold) to `#EA80FC` (a vivid violet-magenta, Material Purple A100) -- `base`/`glow` (`#6A1B9A`/`#AB47BC`) were already genuinely purple and stayed untouched, so the whole three-color set is now consistently one gem's color family rather than two unrelated hues sharing a kind. Contrast re-checked against the new pairing (4.02:1, still comfortably clears the "accent brighter than base" rule established for the fire ladder).

**Small mid-turn fix, same kind**: `comeback`'s ribbon banner text changed from `"REVIVED"` to `"COMEBACK"` (`ACHIEVEMENT_META.comeback.ribbon`), per direct request ("Instead of the banner 'REVIVED' use 'COMEBACK'"). Two stale code comments referencing the old ribbon word (in `achievements.ts`'s own `RibbonSpec` doc comment and `TrophyBadge.tsx`'s `ribbonText` prop doc comment) were updated to match, so a future reader grepping for the actual current ribbon text finds it.

**Follow-up (2026-08-12): a "duotone flame" redesign of the fire ladder tried, then reverted the same turn, per blunt direct feedback ("Undo. The new colors suck ass.").** The ask was real and reasonable -- "the hotter ones also end up looking too dull... I want the fire to transition both hot and vibrant... use more of a duotone flame theme than a monotone flame theme" -- and the diagnosis was sound (the existing ladder's `accent` desaturated toward near-white as intensity rose, e.g. Inferno's `#FFFDE7`, which is technically bright but reads as pale/dull rather than hot). The attempted fix replaced that with a genuine two-hue duotone: `base`/`glow` staying in a saturated red-orange flame-body family throughout, `accent` walking an increasingly electric second pole (gold -> hot pink -> magenta -> violet -> cyan) so nothing ever faded toward white. It shipped, verified clean via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest`, and was rejected outright on sight.

Reverted in full: all five tiers (Fire/Intense Fire/Blazing Fire/Raging Fire/Inferno) back to their exact pre-duotone hex values, and the doc comment's design-rationale paragraph reverted to the "accent brighter than base" explanation from the round before, with one new short note appended flagging that the duotone direction was tried and rejected -- specifically so a future pass doesn't rediscover the same "looks dull" complaint, reach for the same duotone idea, and re-implement something already shown not to work for this user's taste. The fire ladder's actual "not dull, more vibrant" problem remains unresolved -- worth a different approach next time (this doc's own note suggests hue-walking-while-saturated wasn't it; a future attempt might need to stay much closer to the original hue family while only pushing saturation/vividness, rather than introducing pink/magenta/cyan into a flame ladder at all).

Also incidentally confirmed while investigating: `app/utils/achievements.ts` and every other file this whole achievement feature touches (`AchievementCelebration.tsx`, `Confetti.tsx`, `TrophyBadge.tsx`, `TrophiesScreen.tsx`, `achievementsStore.ts`, `trophies.tsx`) are still fully untracked in git (`git status` shows `??`, not `M`) -- expected, not a problem, given this entire feature was built across sessions under the user's own standing "never commit unless explicitly asked" instruction and no commit has been requested yet; noted here only because it came up while double-checking the revert was clean via `git diff`, not because it needs action.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (174/174, unaffected -- pure revert, no logic touched).

**Follow-up (2026-08-12): a targeted, same-hue vibrancy fix for just the two flat-looking tiers, per direct follow-up ("streak 2,5,10 all look great. 25 and 50 are the ones that start to look flat -- you need to up the vibrancy -- not new color, just brighter color").** Confirms Fire/Intense Fire/Blazing Fire (the reverted colors) landed well; narrows the actual problem to specifically Raging Fire (25) and Inferno (50), whose `glow`/`accent` had drifted onto Material's *plain* numbered tiers (`#FFCA28`/`#FFF9C4` and `#FFE082`/`#FFFDE7`) -- a family that desaturates as it lightens, reading as pale/flat rather than vibrant even though it's technically bright. Swapped both to Material's "A" (accent) series instead, which stays fully saturated/electric all the way to full brightness: Raging Fire's `glow`/`accent` -> `#FFAB00`/`#FFD600`; Inferno's -> `#FFCA28`/`#FFFF00`. `base` (`#FFA000`/`#FFB300`) deliberately untouched on both, per the explicit "not new color, just brighter" framing -- this is a same-hue saturation fix, not another hue-introducing redesign like the just-reverted duotone attempt.

**Follow-up (2026-08-12): Raging Fire (25) and Inferno (50) fully reinvented, one round after the "up the vibrancy" same-hue fix, per direct follow-up ("Re-invent streak 25,50 -- make the color scheme look like very intense heat using both yellow and orange, very bright").** The prior fix (swapping just `glow`/`accent` onto Material's "A" series while keeping the original `base`) was a real improvement but this ask ("re-invent") is a genuine redesign, not another tuning pass -- both tiers now deliberately combine *two* hues on purpose (an orange `base`/`glow` pair plus a vivid yellow `accent`, matching "using both yellow and orange" literally) rather than one hue lightened into an accent:

- **Raging Fire (25)**: `#FF3D00` (a hot, red-leaning orange) -> `#FF9800` (pure vivid orange) -> `#FFEB3B` (bright yellow).
- **Inferno (50)**: `#FF6F00` (a warmer, more amber/gold-leaning orange than Raging Fire's redder one -- the two tiers' oranges are deliberately different shades of orange, not the same one repeated) -> `#FFA726` -> `#FFFF00` (the single most saturated, fully electric yellow anywhere in the 14-kind palette -- the ladder's white-hot peak immediately before Magic Fire breaks into violet).

Both tiers stay strictly within the orange/yellow family -- unlike the earlier "duotone flame" attempt that got rejected outright for introducing unrelated hues (pink/magenta/violet/cyan) into the fire ladder, this reinvention deliberately keeps every color a believable "hot metal/flame" tone, just genuinely two of them (orange + yellow) rather than one hue with a paler tint standing in as the second color. Confirmed no hex collisions against the rest of the 14-kind palette.

`AchievementMeta.color`'s own doc comment updated a third time for this same pair of kinds in as many rounds, documenting the full sequence (flat -> same-hue vibrancy fix -> full orange+yellow reinvention) so the reasoning behind the final values doesn't need to be re-derived later.

**Follow-up (2026-08-12): Inferno redesigned again, with a real diagnosis this time, per direct follow-up ("streak 25 now looks great. streak 50 looks GOOD but is too close to streak 5, try a different approach").** Comparing the actual hex values confirmed the complaint precisely: the just-shipped Inferno (`#FF6F00`/`#FFA726`/`#FFFF00`) and Intense Fire (`#FF6D00`/`#FFAB40`/`#FFEA00`) are both a medium-bright orange warming into a medium-bright yellow -- nearly identical in character, differing only by a handful of hex digits per channel. "A different approach" meant not just nudging those same three hues further apart (which risks the same near-miss again), but changing the *shape* of the gradient instead: Inferno now spans deep-dark to blazing-bright (`#BF360C`, a scorched, almost-black-red-orange ember base) through a vivid mid-orange (`#FF5722`) up to the same electric `#FFFF00` peak -- a much wider value range than Intense Fire's flat, uniformly-bright one, so the two now read as genuinely different in character (a dramatic ember-to-white-hot arc vs. a steady medium-bright glow), not just two similar oranges-into-yellows. Raging Fire (25, confirmed good) untouched.

Deliberately picked *lighter and more electric* than Magic Fire's own deeper `#7B1FA2` base, so Inferno reads as a still-real flame whose hottest point is just beginning to flicker unnatural, not as Magic Fire's own colors borrowed wholesale one tier early -- the same "blue is the actual hottest real flame color" reasoning this file has used before for contrast purposes, here repurposed as a genuine narrative device (the ladder's literal hand-off point from grounded fire into the magical/cosmic tiers).

**Immediately reverted and redirected, same turn, per direct pushback ("that's way too much purple. I liked the prior accent color, maybe just tweak the glow and base color to have a bit of a mystical feel, but just slight, still more fire than magic").** `accent` reverted straight back to `#FFFF00`. The "hint of magic" idea moved to `base`/`glow` instead, applied subtly: nudged from the plain scorched red-orange (`#BF360C`/`#FF5722`) toward a deep wine/crimson undertone (`#8E2A3D`/`#D6435A`) -- still unambiguously a warm red family, just with a slightly unnatural, almost blood-magic cast rather than plain fire.

**One more round, same turn, per direct follow-up ("Make the glow more purple, and the base more bright").** `base` brightened from the dark, muted wine-red into a vivid, punchy crimson (`#E53950`); `glow` pushed further from a rose-crimson into a genuinely purple magenta-violet (`#C2449E`). Final Inferno set: `base: '#E53950', glow: '#C2449E', accent: '#FFFF00'` -- a bright hot red warming into a clearly purple-tinged glow, grounded by a plain yellow "hottest point," carrying the "starting to turn magical" story entirely in `base`/`glow` rather than in `accent`.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` after each of the three changes above (174/174 throughout, unaffected -- pure hex-value/doc changes, no collisions anywhere in the 14-kind palette at any point). Every other kind untouched this whole round.

**Follow-up (2026-08-12): `new-best-streak` moved to its own Topaz theme and a real new achievement kind (`anniversary`) was invented to keep Diamond alive, per explicit user direction ("change new best streak -- it should be an orange gemstone, and use the trophy icon, for better consistency with how we represent 'best' throughout the app. Keep the Diamond color scheme and come up with a new achievement it could be used for").** The catalog grew from 14 kinds to 15 for the first time since the achievements feature's original build.

**`new-best-streak`**: icon changed from `trophy-award` to plain `trophy`, confirmed (via a repo-wide grep) to be the exact glyph every other "best streak" surface in the app already uses -- `DashboardStreaksView`, `TaskStatsScreen`, `TaskCard`'s own stats face, all paired with `#FFD700` gold. Theme changed from Diamond to a new **Topaz** (`base: '#F57C00'`, `glow: '#FFA726'`, `accent: '#FFD700'`) -- the accent deliberately reuses that exact same `#FFD700` gold rather than picking a fresh "orange gemstone" accent from scratch, directly tying this kind's own sparkle back to the app's already-established "trophy = gold" visual language, per the literal "for better consistency" ask.

**A brand-new kind, `anniversary`**, was invented to keep Diamond's own color set in active use rather than letting it go to waste: fires once a task has been alive for a full year (365 days since `createdAt`). This needed a genuinely new `ProgressStrategy` variant, `{ type: 'task-age'; days: number }` -- the first new strategy type added since the achievements feature's original build (every kind since then has reused one of the four already-existing patterns) -- since none of the existing strategies measure sheer elapsed time rather than something the user actively did:
- **Detection** (`detectCompletionAchievements`): a new generic loop over `TASK_AGE_ENTRIES` (derived from `ACHIEVEMENT_META` the same way `FIXED_THRESHOLD_ENTRIES`/`RATIO_TO_OWN_BEST_KINDS` already are, so a future second `task-age` kind needs zero new detection code), checking `differenceInCalendarDays(date, parseISO(nextTask.createdAt)) >= days`. Evaluated against `date` (the day the completion was actually *for*), matching `perfect-day`'s own established reasoning for why a backfilled past completion should be judged against that day, not "today" -- covered directly by a dedicated regression test.
- **Repeatability**: marked `repeatable: false`, joining `first-streak` as the second `ONE_TIME_KINDS` member -- correct because a task can only turn 1 year old once, ever, so the store's existing dedup-by-scope guard (already built for `first-streak`) is sufficient with no new edge-trigger logic needed, unlike the currentStreak-based kinds which need a genuine prev/next transition check since they can re-cross the same threshold after a reset.
- **Trophy Case progress** (`getAchievementCardStatus`): a new `'task-age'` case, structurally identical to `fixed-threshold`'s own "closest not-yet-earned task, capped at target" shape, just reading a task's age instead of a live stat.
- **Retroactive scan** (`detectRetroactiveAchievements`): extended with its own `TASK_AGE_ENTRIES` loop and gained a `today: Date = new Date()` parameter (defaulted, so the existing store call site needed no changes) for testability -- deliberately included here (unlike `readiness`/`today-progress`, which stay excluded since they can't be reconstructed from a snapshot) since a task's age *can* be computed purely from `createdAt` and the current date, and this is exactly the kind of pre-existing history (a task older than the whole achievements feature) the retroactive scan exists to catch.
- **Content**: icon `cake-variant`, title "One Year Strong!", flavor text "A full trip around the sun, and you never missed a beat," ribbon shows the day count. `AchievementMeta.color`'s own catalog-description doc comment updated to explain both changes and why Diamond's cultural "long-standing anniversary" association fits this new kind better than it ever fit "beat your own record."

Every other file in this feature (`TrophiesScreen.tsx`, `AchievementCelebration.tsx`, `TrophyBadge.tsx`, `achievementsStore.ts`) needed zero changes -- confirmed via a repo-wide grep that nothing outside `achievements.ts` referenced `new-best-streak` or `trophy-award` by name, so the whole "no kind-based branching, dispatch on strategy-type/data instead" architecture this file has maintained since its own refactor picked up both changes automatically.

7 new tests in `__tests__/utils/achievements.test.ts` (174 → 181): `anniversary` fires exactly at the 365-day mark, doesn't fire before it, doesn't re-fire once dedup-recorded, evaluates against the completion's own date rather than "today" (mirroring `perfect-day`'s own test), the retroactive scan catches an already-over-a-year-old task while correctly skipping a younger one, and `getAchievementCardStatus`'s new `task-age` case reports the closest not-yet-earned task's age (capped at target, matching `fixed-threshold`'s own overshoot-capping behavior) while excluding an already-earned task from progress. One existing test updated to match the intentional design change: `ONE_TIME_KINDS` now asserts `['anniversary', 'first-streak']` instead of just `['first-streak']`.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, all passing -- 7 new, 1 updated to match the intentional catalog growth, the rest unchanged and still passing without modification). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth a careful look at: `new-best-streak`'s trophy icon/gold accent actually reading as visually consistent with the app's other "best streak" trophy indicators, the new Anniversary achievement actually firing for a task old enough to qualify (either naturally or via the retroactive scan), and the Trophy Case grid now showing 15 cards instead of 14 without any layout issue.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (160/160, unaffected -- pure removal, no logic touched).

**Follow-up (2026-08-11): every `TrophyBadge` emblem now always shows a ribbon, per direct user direction ("On the emblem, I want there to ALWAYS be a ribbon/banner. We already have one for count-based achievements, add one with appropriate little text for others").** `comeback` was the one kind that opted out entirely -- `AchievementCelebration.tsx`'s `getRibbonText` explicitly `return undefined`'d for it, since it has no clean numeric value the way the others do (`achievement.value` for `comeback` is the revived streak length, not a meaningful "the" number to summarize on a ribbon the way "10 DAYS"/"100 DONE" work for the others). Gave it its own short, non-numeric label instead: `'REVIVED'`. `getRibbonText`'s return type dropped `| undefined` entirely (now always returns a real string) -- the `achievement.value === undefined` branch (previously the trigger for returning `undefined`) is now just a defensive fallback (`'EARNED'`) for a case that can't actually occur in practice (every kind `detectCompletionAchievements` can produce always sets a `value`; only `comeback`, now handled first, didn't have a *meaningful* one).

`TrophyBadge`'s own `ribbonText` prop changed from optional (`ribbonText?: string`) to required (`ribbonText: string`), and the JSX's `{ribbonText && (...)}` conditional around the whole ribbon row was removed -- it now renders unconditionally. This pushes the "always show one" invariant to the type level rather than leaving it as "every caller happens to always pass something truthy": the one real call site (`AchievementCelebration.tsx`) is now required by TypeScript to always supply a ribbon string, so a future kind added without updating `getRibbonText` would be a compile error, not a silently-missing ribbon.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (160/160, unaffected -- pure content/type change, no layout or animation logic touched). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth a quick look specifically at the `comeback` celebration now that it has a ribbon for the first time, confirming "REVIVED" fits the ribbon's own width comfortably (it's a fixed-height banner sized around whatever text it's given, per `ribbonBarShadowWrap`'s auto-sizing).

**Follow-up (2026-08-11): a "pseudo flat shadow" added to all three of `TrophyBadge`'s solid shapes (the ring, the face, the ribbon banner), per direct user direction ("I want to add a psuedo flat shadow style to the emblem and banner... use it as a mask for a shadow that is a diagonal shape splitting the shape in half, so the lower half is a darkened color from the base color").**

**The ring had to become a real shape first.** The white rim was previously a plain RN `borderWidth`/`borderColor` on the face circle itself -- a border is drawn by the native layer as one uniform color with no paintable child region, so there was no way to independently shade half of *just* the ring the way the request asked. Restructured into two genuinely separate nested circles: `badgeRing` (a new outer white `BADGE_SIZE` circle, `overflow: 'hidden'`) with `badgeFace` (renamed from `badgeClip`, now inset by a new `RING_WIDTH` constant on each side, `BADGE_FACE_SIZE = BADGE_SIZE - RING_WIDTH * 2`) centered inside it -- visually identical to the old border (a white circle with a smaller colored circle centered on top, leaving a `RING_WIDTH`-wide white band around the edge), just as two independently shadeable shapes instead of one shape plus a border property. This shrank the face's own effective diameter slightly (120 -> 112), so the sheen's geometry (`SHEEN_TRAVEL`, and the `sheen` style's `top`/`left`/`height`) was updated to reference `BADGE_FACE_SIZE` instead of `BADGE_SIZE` for correctness, though the 8px difference was negligible either way given how oversized that geometry already was.

**The shadow technique itself, `diagonalShadowOverlay`, is one shared style reused across all three shapes** (the ring, the face, and `ribbonBarClip`) rather than three separate implementations: a plain solid-color square (`SHADOW_OVERLAY_SIZE = 260`, oversized relative to any of the three shapes it's used on), rotated 45° and positioned so that, after rotation, one of its own edges passes exactly through the parent's center -- the parent's own `overflow: 'hidden'` + shape (circle, ring, or plain rectangle) does the actual clipping, so the identical style works for all three shape types with zero shape-specific path math and no SVG needed (despite `react-native-svg` being available elsewhere in this app for exactly this class of problem -- a plain rotated View, reusing the same "oversized rotated rectangle, let overflow:hidden clip it" trick this file already uses for the sheen bars, was simpler here).

The offset math (`SHADOW_OVERLAY_OFFSET = SHADOW_OVERLAY_SIZE * (0.5 - 1/(2*Math.SQRT2))`) took two real derivation attempts to get right, both fully worked out and the final one numerically verified (via a throwaway Node script checking four cardinal test points against the resulting diamond) before committing to it -- the first attempt had the offset's *sign* backwards (positioning the square's center away from the target point rather than toward it), which would have shaded the wrong half entirely; the corrected derivation is written out in full in the constant's own comment, including the specific "easy sign error" callout so it isn't reintroduced by a future edit. Confirmed numerically: East and South test points (relative to a shape's center) fall inside the shadowed region, West and North don't -- i.e. the shadow reads as "light from the upper-left, shadow toward the lower-right," satisfying "the lower half is a darkened color" directly.

**Colors**: two new values computed once in the component body via `tinycolor(...).darken(SHADOW_DARKEN_AMOUNT).toHexString()` (`SHADOW_DARKEN_AMOUNT = 18`, one shared amount so the "flat shading" reads as one consistent light source across the whole medallion) -- `ringShadowColor` (darkened white, i.e. a light gray, for the ring) and `faceShadowColor` (darkened `color`, reused for *both* the face and the ribbon banner, since both already share that same accent color). `tinycolor` was re-added as an import to this file (it had been removed during an earlier round of face-tint experiments that were later reverted).

**Layering, per shape**, each overlay rendered as the bottom-most child (painting first, so everything else -- icon, ribbon text, the animated sheens -- stays crisp and unaffected on top of the static shading, matching that the user's request named the three *shapes* specifically, not the icon or ribbon text): `badgeRing`'s overlay renders before `badgeFace`; `badgeFace`'s own overlay renders before the icon and sheen; `ribbonBarClip`'s overlay renders before the ribbon text and its own sheen.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (160/160, unaffected -- pure visual addition, no other logic touched). Not yet confirmed on-device -- this is a genuinely new technique for this file (a shared oversized-rotated-square mask reused across three different shape types) so worth a careful first look, particularly: the shadow direction actually reads as "lower-right darker" rather than some other angle, the ring's now-separate shape didn't introduce any visible seam or size shift at the face/ring boundary, and the shadow doesn't visually fight with the existing frame-ring burst/pulse animations sitting just outside the badge.

**Follow-up (2026-08-11), three tweaks in one turn: a flatter shadow angle, a more subtle shadow color, and a much tighter badge-sheen travel distance, all per direct user follow-up.**

**1. Shadow angle, 45° → -20°, per "I want it to be more horizontally flat (still angled, just not a full 45 degrees)".** The original derivation was specific to a 45° rotation (it exploited the fact that a 45°-rotated square's corners land exactly on the axes through its center, which doesn't hold at other angles). Generalized to an arbitrary angle θ: for a square's "top" edge (the one whose un-rotated outward normal points straight up) to pass through target point T after rotating by θ about its own center C, `C = T + (L/2)·(-sinθ, cosθ)` -- derived by writing that edge's line equation post-rotation and solving for where it crosses T, converted into the `marginTop`/`marginLeft` the style actually needs (`marginTop = (L/2)(cosθ-1)`, `marginLeft = -(L/2)(sinθ+1)`, since `top/left: '50%'` positions the square's *corner*, not its center, and rotation happens about the element's own center after that positioning). Critically, *which sign of θ* leans the shadow toward the lower-right (matching the already-approved "light from upper-left" look) rather than the lower-left isn't obvious from the formula alone -- confirmed numerically via a throwaway Node script checking which cardinal test points (N/S/E/W around a circle's center) land inside the resulting shape, for both the new formula and, as a sanity check, its exact reduction back to the original 45° case (θ=-45° reproduces the identical N/S/E/W pattern the original, separately-derived 45°-specific formula produced) before committing to the new general version. `SHADOW_ANGLE_DEGREES = -20` is now a single tunable constant driving both the rotation transform and the margin math together, so it can't drift out of sync the way two independently-set values could.

**2. Shadow color, `SHADOW_DARKEN_AMOUNT` 18 → 12, per "I want the shadow to be a just a touch more subtle... try an adjustment that is a little bit less."** A one-line value change -- `tinycolor(...).darken(12)` instead of `darken(18)` -- for both the ring's (white → light gray) and the face/banner's (accent color → darker accent) shaded halves, keeping the two in sync since they share the one constant.

**3. Badge sheen travel, `BADGE_FACE_SIZE * 1.6` → `BADGE_FACE_SIZE * 0.75`, per "on the circle, I notice that it starts and ends far outside the circle -- a significant portion of the shimmer's animation is completely unseen."** At the old 1.6x multiplier, the sheen bar's center traveled roughly ±179px from the badge's own center at its extremes -- nearly double the face circle's own ~56px radius -- meaning most of the (now 2600ms-long) sweep played out entirely off-clip, invisible, before the bar ever entered the visible circle. 0.75x tightens this to roughly radius + half the bar's own 28px width + a small buffer for its 20° rotation, so the sweep now starts/ends just outside the visible edge instead of far beyond it -- the same underlying "clipping does the hiding, no separate opacity toggle needed" property is preserved, just with a much smaller invisible margin. The ribbon's own sheen travel (`RIBBON_SHEEN_TRAVEL`) was left untouched -- this complaint was specifically about the circle.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (160/160, unaffected -- pure animation-math/color-value changes, no other logic touched). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth checking that the flatter -20° shadow still reads clearly as "angled, not purely horizontal," and that the tightened sheen travel doesn't clip the bar's own edges awkwardly mid-sweep now that its margin past the circle's radius is much smaller than before.

**Follow-up (2026-08-11): the banner's own shadow line pushed lower, and the darken amount tried at 9, per direct follow-up ("The banner shadow should be lower. Also try darken(9)").**

**Banner shadow lowered.** `diagonalShadowOverlay`'s `top/left: '50%'` centers the boundary line on whatever parent it's placed in -- fine for the badge's ring/face (both tall enough that "vertically centered" reads naturally as "about half shadowed"), but the ribbon banner is a short rectangle, so the same centering put its own line through its vertical middle too, without a request for that specifically. New `RIBBON_SHADOW_VERTICAL_OFFSET = 6` is added *only* to the ribbon's own shadow-overlay instance (`marginTop: SHADOW_OVERLAY_MARGIN_TOP + RIBBON_SHADOW_VERTICAL_OFFSET`, an inline override on that one `View`, not a change to the shared `diagonalShadowOverlay` style itself) -- pushing its boundary line down, shrinking the shadowed strip at the bottom of the banner. A pure vertical translation applied *before* rotation shifts the resulting line down by exactly that px amount regardless of the rotation angle, so this is a simple additive offset rather than needing its own angle-aware re-derivation. The badge's ring and face shadows are untouched -- this was specifically about the banner.

**Darken amount, 12 → 9** (the second reduction in as many rounds, following an earlier 18 → 12) -- one more one-line value change to `SHADOW_DARKEN_AMOUNT`, still shared by the ring's white and the face/banner's accent-color shading so both stay in sync.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (160/160, unaffected -- pure color-value/margin changes, no other logic touched). Not yet confirmed on-device.

**Follow-up (2026-08-11): the ribbon's own drop shadow strengthened, per direct follow-up ("I think the central rectangle dropshadow needs to be stronger").** `ribbonBarShadowWrap`'s shadow properties all bumped up: `shadowOffset` height 2→3, `shadowOpacity` 0.25→0.4, `shadowRadius` 3→5, `elevation` 4→7 -- still well short of `badgeShadowWrap`'s own considerably larger values, which were never meant as a target to match (that shadow is cast by a much bigger shape). This is the ribbon bar's genuine drop shadow (the wrapper carrying `shadowColor`/`shadowOffset`/`shadowOpacity`/`shadowRadius`/`elevation`), distinct from the flat "pseudo shadow" diagonal-half overlay added a few turns earlier -- the two tail triangles (`ribbonTailLeft`/`ribbonTailRight`) still have no drop shadow of their own, unchanged.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (160/160, unaffected -- pure style-value change, no logic touched).

**Follow-up (2026-08-11): the drop shadow's own values weren't the real problem -- it doesn't spill onto the tail triangles at all on Android, per direct on-device report ("I'm zooming in and I don't see any dropshadow of the central rectangle over top of the triangles. That's part of what I want").** This is a real, well-known React Native cross-platform gap, not a magnitude issue: `shadowColor`/`shadowOffset`/`shadowOpacity`/`shadowRadius` are iOS-only in their actual effect -- Android instead renders shadow purely from the `elevation` prop via its own Material shadow algorithm, and that Android elevation shadow is known to stay tightly clipped to the casting view's own bounds rather than genuinely extending over adjacent sibling views the way a real box-shadow blur would. No amount of further shadowRadius/elevation tuning (this file already went through one "make it stronger" round) was going to fix this on the actual target platform, since the mechanism itself doesn't support cross-sibling spill on Android at all.

Fixed with the same tactic this file already uses elsewhere for exactly this class of "the platform's native effect doesn't reliably do what I need" problem (the flat-shadow diagonal overlays, the sheen bars): a plain, explicitly hand-drawn spill layer, `ribbonShadowSpill` -- a semi-transparent (`rgba(0,0,0,0.22)`) rectangle, absolutely positioned inside `ribbonBarShadowWrap`, extending 8px past the bar's own left/right edges (matching the tail triangles' own `borderRightWidth`/`borderLeftWidth`, so it visibly crosses most of the way over each one) and 2px past top/bottom. `ribbonBarShadowWrap`'s existing `zIndex: 1` (relative to the triangle siblings outside it) is what makes this render above them -- no zIndex needed on the spill view itself, since z-index only competes among siblings sharing one parent, not across nesting levels. The genuine native shadow (`shadowRadius`/`elevation`/etc.) was left in place underneath this, still providing whatever soft lift it can on either platform -- this is a deliberate addition on top, not a replacement.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (160/160, unaffected -- pure additive View, no logic touched). Not yet confirmed on-device -- this was specifically reported from a real device, so worth re-checking there first: that the spill now visibly darkens the top of both tail triangles, and that 0.22 opacity reads as a shadow rather than either too faint to notice or too heavy/flat-looking.

**Follow-up (2026-08-11): the spill pulled back from reaching the triangle tips, made asymmetric per-edge, and given rounded corners, per direct follow-up ("the shadow is now extending left/right to the tips of the triangles, I want it only casting from the central rectangle -- a tiny visible at the top, a little on the left and right, a bit more on the bottom. Also can you round the shadow corners?").** `ribbonShadowSpill`'s per-edge extension changed from a uniform `left/right: -8` (matching -- and reaching all the way to -- the tail triangles' own full width) + `top/bottom: -2` to explicitly asymmetric values matching the requested hierarchy: `top: -1` (barely visible), `left`/`right: -3` (a little), `bottom: -5` (the most) -- reading as a shadow cast mostly downward by a light source roughly overhead, rather than a uniform halo bleeding evenly in every direction. `borderRadius: 4` added for the requested rounded corners, softening what was otherwise a plain hard-edged rectangle.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (160/160, unaffected -- pure style-value change, no logic touched).

**Follow-up (2026-08-11): tightened further and made more transparent, per direct follow-up ("Tighten up the shadow a little bit on all edges and make it a little more transparent").** `ribbonShadowSpill`'s per-edge extension reduced proportionally across the board (`top` -1 → -0.5, `left`/`right` -3 → -2, `bottom` -5 → -3 -- the same asymmetric top-least/bottom-most hierarchy from the entry above preserved, just each edge pulled in further) and `backgroundColor` opacity reduced 0.22 → 0.15.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (160/160, unaffected -- pure style-value change, no logic touched).

**Follow-up (2026-08-11): `perfect-day`'s ribbon text changed from "N TASKS" to a plain "PERFECT", per direct user direction ("Perfect Day's banner text should say 'PERFECT' ot 'n tasks'").** `getRibbonText`'s `perfect-day` case now returns a fixed `'PERFECT'` regardless of `achievement.value` -- moved ahead of the `value === undefined` fallback check entirely (it no longer needs to look at value at all), reasoning that the count of tasks due that day isn't really the interesting fact about a perfect day the way a streak length or completion count is for the other kinds (it doesn't escalate/grow the way those do, it's just whatever happened to be due), so a plain declaration reads better than a number here.

The `metaBlock` `RevealSlot`'s `minHeight` dropped 48 → 40, back to its value from before that same earlier round, now that there's one fewer element to reserve space for.

**Follow-up (2026-08-11): a third pass on three specific lines, per explicit user direction to make `streak-1000` genuinely "epic" ("like 'Behold I am become death, destroyer of worlds' except that's dark and destructive not congratulatory") and to redo `streak-50`/`milestone-1000` as "more creative and maybe pun or meme inspired."**

`streak-1000` → `'Behold: a thousand days of triumph. Let it be written.'` — deliberately borrows the grand, declarative *cadence* of the referenced Oppenheimer/Bhagavad Gita line ("Behold, I am become...") without any of its dark/destructive content — a proclamation of triumph rather than doom, positioned as the single most over-the-top line in the whole map since it's the app's highest tier. This is the one deliberate exception to every other entry's short, punchy length — a slightly longer, more ceremonial phrasing is part of what makes it read as "epic" rather than just another quip.

`streak-50` → `'Fifty and fabulous — no midlife crisis required.'` — a pun on the "turning 50" birthday/midlife-crisis cultural trope rather than anything streak-specific, landing on this over an early alternative ("50 Shades of Dedicated") specifically to avoid an R-rated pop-culture reference in an otherwise all-ages app.

`milestone-1000` → `'Welcome to the four-digit club.'` — an exclusive-club meme format (e.g. "the mile-high club"), playing on 1,000 being the point completions first reach four digits, rather than the earlier `'Somewhere, a trophy case just got a lot fuller.'`

**Follow-up (2026-08-11): rejected all three, redone once more.** Direct feedback ("Ugh, start over on those 3. Don't like them.") on the batch above. Rather than guess a fourth time, presented four fresh witty options per line (a mix of registers -- mythic, deadpan, pun, meme -- for each) and let the user pick directly. Final picks:
- `streak-1000` → `"You've entered myth territory. Godspeed, legend."`
- `streak-50` → `'High five, times ten.'` (the "5 x 10 = 50" wordplay)

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (160/160, unaffected — pure copy change). Not yet confirmed on-device.

**Follow-up (2026-08-11): a real architecture refactor -- every `kind`-based branch in downstream code eliminated in favor of dispatching on data, per explicit user direction ("I want to avoid having `kind` conditionally handled in code, for example getRibbonText, instead I want the achievement meta to describe the achievement in ways that are fully dynamically handled downstream, so we can add new achievements without having to modify downstream code").** Mid-turn, the scope grew further: `first-streak` needed to become a global (not per-task) one-time moment, two new per-task tiers (`streak-2`, `streak-5`) needed adding, and every kind except `first-streak` needed to become repeatable. Both requests turned out to reinforce the same design, not conflict with it.

**Audit, before writing anything.** Traced every `achievement.kind === '...'` / `kind.startsWith('...')` branch across the feature: `achievements.ts`'s own `progressMetric` (milestone-vs-streak metric selection) and `getAchievementCardStatus` (an if/else chain: fixed-threshold → new-best-streak → comeback → perfect-day-by-elimination), and `AchievementCelebration.tsx`'s `getRibbonText`, the `DescriptionText` component's `if (kind === 'perfect-day')` special case, plus three per-kind lookup maps (`NUMBER_BLOCK_TEXT`, `FLAVOR_TEXT`, `TRIGGER_SUFFIX`) that, while already *data* (Record lookups, not if/else), still lived scattered across a second file rather than alongside the rest of each kind's definition. `TrophiesScreen.tsx` and `TrophyBadge.tsx` were confirmed to already be fully generic (no kind branches at all) -- no changes needed there.

**The design: strategy-type dispatch instead of kind dispatch.** `AchievementMeta` (in `achievements.ts`, the single source of truth both other files read from) grew from `{icon, title, describe}` to also carry `flavorText`, `numberBlock?`, `ribbon` (`{kind:'count', unit}` or `{kind:'fixed', text}`), `triggerSuffix?`/`triggerStandalone?`, `repeatable`, `scope` (`'task' | 'global'`), and `progressStrategy` -- a closed, discriminated union of *computation patterns* (`fixed-threshold`, `ratio-to-own-best`, `readiness`, `today-progress`), not one variant per kind. Every downstream consumer now switches on `progressStrategy.type` or checks a data field (`achievement.taskName` presence, `ribbon.kind`) -- never on `achievement.kind` itself. Concretely:
- `getRibbonText` moved into `achievements.ts` itself (exported, used by `AchievementCelebration.tsx`) and shrank to reading `ACHIEVEMENT_META[kind].ribbon` -- no more `if (kind === 'comeback')`/`if (kind === 'perfect-day')`/`startsWith('milestone-')` chain.
- `DescriptionText`'s trigger-sentence rendering now branches on whether *this achievement instance* has a `taskName` (a per-task kind's `triggerSuffix` gets used when it does; a global kind's `triggerStandalone` when it doesn't) -- so a *future* taskless kind works correctly here with zero new code, as long as its own metadata sets `triggerStandalone`.
- `getAchievementCardStatus` is now a `switch (strategy.type)` with exactly four handler blocks (one per pattern, not per kind) -- `fixed-threshold` covers first-streak/streak-2/streak-5/streak-10/25/50/100/1000/milestone-10/100/1000 all at once; `ratio-to-own-best` covers new-best-streak; `readiness` covers comeback; `today-progress` covers perfect-day.
- `detectCompletionAchievements`'s fixed-threshold detection collapsed from a special-cased first-streak block plus separate streak-tier/milestone-tier loops into *one* generic loop over `FIXED_THRESHOLD_ENTRIES` -- itself derived directly from `ACHIEVEMENT_META` (filtering for `progressStrategy.type === 'fixed-threshold'`) rather than a separately maintained tier list, so a new fixed-threshold kind is detected the instant it's added to the metadata table, no new code. `new-best-streak`/`comeback`/`perfect-day` stay their own bespoke blocks -- deliberately: they're genuinely different *rules* (not additional data points in an existing pattern), and a brand-new rule will always need its own logic no matter how this is architected. What the refactor eliminates is the much more common case -- adding another *tier* of an existing pattern -- not the rare case of inventing an entirely new kind of check.
- `ONE_TIME_KINDS` is now derived from `ACHIEVEMENT_META`'s own `repeatable` flag (`Object.keys(...).filter(k => !repeatable)`) instead of a manually-synced array that could drift from the per-kind data.

**`first-streak` going global, folded into the same generic loop as the per-task tiers.** A new `scope: 'task' | 'global'` field on `AchievementMeta` decides two things inside the *same* fixed-threshold detection loop: whether task identity (`taskId`/`taskName`/`taskIcon`/`taskColor`) gets attached to the earned record, and whether the dedup key is the task's own id or a fixed `'global'` string. This is what lets `first-streak` (global, one-time, no task attached) and the new `streak-2` (per-task, repeatable, same threshold) coexist as two achievements from the *exact same underlying crossing* -- a task's streak going from 1 to 2 fires both `streak-2` (celebrating that task specifically) and, the very first time *any* task ever does this, `first-streak` (a one-time, user-wide "you got started" moment) -- without either needing its own special-cased detection block. `first-streak`'s `describe`/`triggerStandalone` were rewritten to not reference a task at all (previously `describe` used a `taskName(a) ?? 'This task'` fallback that would have read as the literal string "This task" once the achievement stopped carrying a real task name).

**New tiers.** `streak-2` ("Streak Started!" -- the per-task, repeatable counterpart to the now-global first-streak) and `streak-5` ("5-Day Streak!") both slot into the existing `streak-N` family with zero new detection code, just new `ACHIEVEMENT_META` entries with `progressStrategy: {type:'fixed-threshold', metric:'currentStreak', target: 2 or 5}`. `ACHIEVEMENT_KIND_ORDER` (still a manually-curated display-order array -- registering a new kind's *display position* is an unavoidable minimal step, not the kind of downstream branching this refactor targeted) grew to 14 entries.

**Repeatability.** Per explicit direction, only `first-streak` stays one-time; every other kind (including the previously-one-time streak-N/milestone-N tiers and perfect-day) is now `repeatable: true`. In practice this mostly matters for streak-N (a task can now re-earn e.g. `streak-10` if its streak resets and climbs back to 10 again -- the existing "crossed the threshold" transition check already naturally gates re-firing to only the actual crossing moment, so repeatability doesn't risk spamming every day the streak happens to sit above 10). It's a no-op in practice for milestone-N (a monotonically-increasing lifetime counter can only cross a fixed threshold once, ever, repeatable or not) and mostly a no-op for perfect-day (its own per-date crossing logic already only fires once per date in normal completion order; removing it from `ONE_TIME_KINDS` mainly removes a same-date re-entrancy safety net rather than changing normal behavior).

**Test updates** (`__tests__/utils/achievements.test.ts`, 160 → 164 tests): two existing assertions needed updating to match the new, intentional design rather than being regressions -- the dedup test's pre-recorded scope changed from `'first-streak:t1'` to `'first-streak:global'`, and the catalog-length assertion changed from a hardcoded `12` to `ACHIEVEMENT_KIND_ORDER.length` (so it can't silently drift out of sync again). Four new tests added: `first-streak` carries no task identity while its `streak-2` sibling does (verifying the scope split); a `streak-2`-specific describe block; a direct `ONE_TIME_KINDS` equality check (`['first-streak']`) as the real, precise way to pin down "only first-streak is one-time" rather than an indirect behavioral proxy; and confirming the `fixed-threshold` progress handler works identically for a global kind (first-streak) as any per-task one, since progress computation doesn't care about scope at all.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (164/164, all passing -- 4 new, 2 updated to match the intentional redesign, the rest unchanged and still passing without modification, confirming existing behavior was preserved everywhere this refactor didn't deliberately change it). Not yet confirmed on-device -- this is the biggest structural change to the achievement feature since it was first built, so worth a careful full pass: crossing a task's streak from 1→2 for the first time ever ideally shows *two* trophies in the Trophy Case (first-streak and streak-2) rather than one; re-crossing an already-earned streak-N tier after a reset actually re-fires and re-celebrates; and the celebration screen's copy for first-streak (now taskless) reads naturally without an awkward "This task" placeholder anywhere.

**Follow-up (2026-08-11): Trophy Case display order made fully data-driven too, per direct user direction ("I want it grouped and sorted in a way that makes sense"), closing the one gap the refactor above explicitly left open (display order was still "the one place a new kind still needs manual registration").** `ACHIEVEMENT_KIND_ORDER` -- previously a hand-written flat array -- is now *derived*: two new fields on `AchievementMeta`, `category: 'streak' | 'completion' | 'moment'` and `order: number` (a sort key within category, kept as a plain explicit number rather than derived from `progressStrategy.target`, since `first-streak` and `streak-2` share the same target of 2 and still need a deterministic relative order), combined with a small `CATEGORY_ORDER` array (`['streak', 'completion', 'moment']`) describing the *catalog's* own group order. `ACHIEVEMENT_KIND_ORDER` is now `Object.keys(ACHIEVEMENT_META).sort(...)`, grouping by category then by `order` within it. A new kind now needs only its own `ACHIEVEMENT_META` entry (category + order included) to land in the right place -- no separate array to remember to update, the same "no downstream code changes" property the rest of this refactor already established, now covering display position too.

The resulting order reproduced the prior hand-written sequence almost exactly, with one deliberate change: `new-best-streak` moved from the middle of the streak ladder (between `streak-2` and `streak-5`) to the end of it (`order: 9`, after `streak-1000`) -- it's not a rung on the fixed streak-length ladder the way the others are (no fixed target, repeatable indefinitely), so grouping it at the ladder's tail reads more sensibly than wedging it partway through the ascending sequence. Final order: **Streaks** (first-streak → streak-2 → streak-5 → streak-10 → streak-25 → streak-50 → streak-100 → streak-1000 → new-best-streak) → **Completions** (milestone-10 → 100 → 1000) → **Moments** (perfect-day, comeback).

No test changes needed -- `getAllAchievementCardStatuses`'s existing "in catalog order" test already asserted against `ACHIEVEMENT_KIND_ORDER` dynamically (not a hardcoded sequence), so it validated the new derived order automatically.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (164/164, unaffected -- 0 new/changed tests, pure internal derivation change with an externally-observable result verified directly via a throwaway script rather than a new automated test, since "does this specific order look right" is a display/curation judgment rather than a behavior worth locking into a permanent test assertion). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth a look at the actual Trophy Case grid to confirm the three groups read as visually coherent even without explicit section headers/dividers between them (none were added -- this only reordered the flat grid, per the request's literal scope; visual section headers would be a separate, larger UI change if wanted later).

**Follow-up (2026-08-11): the category/order approach above was a wrong turn, corrected per direct user pushback the moment it shipped ("That's not really what I meant by category, I just meant to visually group based on existing meta data. So it's just a multi-sort: first group by [unlocked, in progress, not started]... then within unlocked sort by date of last completion, within in progress sort by progress, and for not started sort simply by original fixed order").** The `category`/`order` fields on `AchievementMeta` were reaching for the wrong kind of data entirely -- static, hand-authored taxonomy metadata invented for this feature, when what was actually wanted is a grouping computed from each kind's own *live, already-existing* status data (the same `unlocked`/`progress`/`opportunityAvailable` fields `AchievementCardStatus` already carries for the progress bars). Reverted outright: `category`/`order`/`AchievementCategory`/`CATEGORY_ORDER` are all gone from `achievements.ts`. `ACHIEVEMENT_KIND_ORDER` is a plain hand-curated list again (preserving the one genuine improvement the reverted design produced -- `new-best-streak` staying at the tail of the streak family rather than wedged mid-ladder -- since that ordering call was sound even though the mechanism that derived it wasn't), expressed as `Object.keys(ACHIEVEMENT_META).sort(...)` against a `Record<AchievementKind, number>` index (`ACHIEVEMENT_ORDER_INDEX`) rather than a bare array specifically so tsc still forces every kind to have an entry -- a bare array could silently omit a newly-added kind with zero compile-time signal, the exact failure mode the original category-derivation was trying to avoid, just achieved a different, correct way.

New export, `getGroupedAchievementCardStatuses(achievements, activeTasks, today?)`, replaces `ACHIEVEMENT_KIND_ORDER` as `TrophiesScreen`'s actual data source. It's the real multi-sort the user described, computed in one pass over `getAllAchievementCardStatuses`'s own output (no new state, no new per-kind metadata):
- **Unlocked** -- `status.unlocked`, sorted by `latest.earnedAt` descending (a plain string comparison; ISO timestamps sort lexically in chronological order, so no date parsing needed).
- **In Progress** -- locked, but with some nonzero "closeness". A new `progressFraction(status)` helper puts every progress shape on one common 0-1 scale so kinds with genuinely different `progressStrategy`s can be compared against each other in one sort: `progress.current / progress.target` for the three numeric strategies, and, for the one strategy with no fraction at all (`comeback`'s `readiness`), `opportunityAvailable ? 1 : 0` -- a live opportunity is treated as maximally close, since it could fire on the very next completion with no count to wait on, at least as "close" as any numeric progress gets. Sorted by that fraction descending.
- **Not Started** -- locked, zero fraction, sorted by `ACHIEVEMENT_ORDER_INDEX` -- this is the one place the old hand-curated catalog order still matters, exactly matching the user's own "for not started sort simply by original fixed order."

Groups with zero cards are omitted entirely (a fresh install with nothing unlocked yet shouldn't show an empty "Unlocked" heading with nothing under it) rather than always rendering all three.

**`TrophiesScreen.tsx` needed real UI restructuring, not just a new data source** -- `FlatList`'s `numColumns` grid mode has no concept of a full-width header row mixed into a multi-column grid, and `SectionList` (RN's usual answer for grouped lists) doesn't support `numColumns` at all, which would have broken the existing responsive 2/3/4-column layout. Solved by chunking by hand: a new `buildListItems(groups, columnCount)` flattens the three groups into a single array of `{type:'header', label}` and `{type:'row', statuses}` items (each row pre-sliced to `columnCount` cards), and the `FlatList` itself drops `numColumns`/`columnWrapperStyle` entirely -- `renderItem` now branches on `item.type`, rendering either a plain `sectionHeader` `Text` (a small uppercase label, matching this app's existing small-caps section-label language elsewhere, e.g. `AchievementCelebration`'s `unlockedLabel`) or a manually-built `row` `View` (`flexDirection:'row', gap: GRID_SPACING` -- previously this same `gap` lived on a `columnWrapperStyle` object that FlatList itself rendered as a row container, so `flexDirection:'row'` had to be added explicitly now that the row `View` is hand-built rather than FlatList-provided) containing up to `columnCount` `AchievementBadgeCard`s. A trailing partial row (fewer than `columnCount` cards) naturally left-packs rather than stretching or centering, matching the prior grid's own default behavior. The header's `unlockedCount`/`totalCount` subtitle now sums across all three groups' own `statuses.length` rather than reading a single flat `statuses` array, since that array no longer exists at this screen's top level.

4 new tests in `__tests__/utils/achievements.test.ts` (164 → 168): the all-not-started/no-progress case (every kind lands in one omitted-elsewhere `not-started` group, in catalog order); Unlocked sorts newest-first across three different kinds/scopes (a per-task kind, a global date-scoped kind, and another per-task kind, deliberately mixed to confirm the sort doesn't accidentally depend on scope); In Progress sorts multiple fixed-threshold tiers sharing one task's `currentStreak` by their own independently-capped closeness (first-streak/streak-2, both already at their low cap, rank above streak-5, which ranks above streak-10); and `comeback`'s readiness/`opportunityAvailable` case lands in In Progress (not Not Started), confirming the cross-strategy `progressFraction` treatment actually applies to the one kind that needed it.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (168/168). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth a careful look at: the three section headings actually reading as distinct visual groups against `sectionHeader`'s fairly quiet styling, a trailing partial row per group not looking oddly truncated, and the Not Started group (likely the biggest bucket on a fresh install) not feeling overwhelming compared to the other two.

**Follow-up (2026-08-11): the section headings centered with flanking dividers, per direct user request ("for the section headers, center them and add a divider on each side").** The Unlocked group's own sort was double-checked against the same request's "sort them by unlock date (most recent at top)" line first -- already correct (`getGroupedAchievementCardStatuses`'s existing `.sort((a, b) => (b.latest?.earnedAt ?? '').localeCompare(a.latest?.earnedAt ?? ''))`, confirmed by its own passing test), so no code change was needed there, just this styling one.

`TrophiesScreen.tsx`'s header `renderItem` branch wraps the label in a new `sectionHeaderRow` (`flexDirection:'row', alignItems:'center', gap:10`) alongside two `sectionHeaderDivider` views (`flex:1, height:1, backgroundColor: colors.border`) flanking it on both sides -- a standard "text with rules on either side" layout, where the two equal-`flex:1` dividers automatically split whatever space the label's own width doesn't take, keeping the label centered regardless of how long its text is. `sectionHeader` itself picked up `textAlign:'center'` (harmless alongside the flex-based centering, but a correct fallback if the row's width ever isn't evenly split for some reason).

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (168/168, unaffected -- pure styling addition, no logic touched).

**Follow-up (2026-08-11): a manual "catch up" retroactive scan added to the Trophy Case, per explicit user direction ("Add a button to the bottom of the trophy case that will retroactively run all achievements against the current data (still respecting duplication rules) -- this should not show any congratulations message but can show a toast of how many achievements were updated").** This revisits (without contradicting) this feature's own original "No historical backfill" scope decision (documented earlier in this doc, under the initial achievements build) -- that decision was about *automatic* backfill happening silently the moment the feature shipped; this is the opposite, an explicit, user-initiated, one-tap sweep, which the original doc's own wording anticipated ("A full backfill... is possible later but is out of scope here").

**`detectRetroactiveAchievements(achievements, activeTasks)`** (new export, `achievements.ts`) is a deliberately narrower sibling of `detectCompletionAchievements` -- it evaluates every kind against the task list's *current* `.stats` snapshot rather than a specific completion's before/after transition, since there's no historical per-day transition data to replay (this app's `Task.stats` is always the live, precomputed-at-mutation-time current state, not a day-by-day ledger). Two of the four `progressStrategy` types genuinely can't be reconstructed from a single snapshot and are skipped entirely, not guessed at: `readiness` (comeback) needs to know the task was actually lapsed *immediately before* this moment, and `today-progress` (perfect-day) needs a specific day's own due-state, not "right now." Only `fixed-threshold` (reusing the exact same `FIXED_THRESHOLD_ENTRIES` list `detectCompletionAchievements` already derives from `ACHIEVEMENT_META`) and `ratio-to-own-best` (a new, equally-derived `RATIO_TO_OWN_BEST_KINDS` list, currently just `new-best-streak`) are evaluated -- for `fixed-threshold`, a task/global scope qualifies once its current stat meets the target; for `ratio-to-own-best`, a task qualifies when it's currently sitting *at* its own record (`currentStreak >= bestStreak`, with the same `FIRST_STREAK_THRESHOLD` floor `detectCompletionAchievements`'s own new-best-streak block uses) -- an imperfect but reasonable proxy for "has genuinely set a record," since a snapshot alone can't distinguish "just beat an old record" from "still climbing its first-ever streak."

**Dedup here is deliberately stricter than the live detector's own.** `detectCompletionAchievements`'s `alreadyEarnedScopes` set (built by the store) only ever contains `ONE_TIME_KINDS`, since a live, transition-driven detection naturally only re-fires a repeatable kind on a genuine fresh crossing. A snapshot-based scan has no transition to gate on, so re-running it with nothing changed would re-award every already-qualifying repeatable kind again and again if it used that same narrower set. `detectRetroactiveAchievements` instead builds its own dedup set from *every* existing achievement regardless of kind (`achievements.map(a => dedupKey(a.kind, a.dedupScope))`) -- once a (kind, scope) pair has been credited once by this scan (or by real live play), it's never re-credited by a later scan, full stop.

**Store**: `achievementsStore.ts` gained `runRetroactiveScan(activeTasks): number`, mirroring `recordCompletionAchievements`'s id/earnedAt-assignment pattern (same index-suffixed id scheme, since one scan can surface several achievements in one synchronous call) but appending only to `achievements`, deliberately never to `pendingCelebrations` -- per the explicit "should not show any congratulations message" direction, this can never trigger `AchievementCelebration`'s full-screen takeover, only the count it returns.

**UI**: `TrophiesScreen.tsx` gained a `ListFooterComponent` button ("Check for Missed Achievements", a `history` icon, styled as a plain outlined secondary button matching the app's existing subtle-action language rather than a primary CTA) below the grid. `handleRetroactiveScan` fires a light haptic (matching this app's convention of a light tap for a non-destructive, non-completion action), calls the store action with `activeTasks`, and shows a plain info toast via the existing `ToastContext` with the count -- "Found N achievement(s) you'd already earned!" or "No new achievements found — you're all caught up." No `action`/Undo on this toast, since there's nothing to undo (an already-qualifying achievement getting recorded isn't a mistake to reverse) and it's not the kind of destructive/reversible action this app's toast-Undo convention exists for.

6 new tests in `__tests__/utils/achievements.test.ts` (168 → 174): fixed-threshold awarding stops at the highest currently-qualifying tier; an already-recorded (kind, scope) pair is never re-awarded while sibling not-yet-earned tiers still are; the global first-streak awards exactly once across multiple qualifying tasks with no task identity attached; new-best-streak only fires for a task genuinely at its own record, not one still below it; comeback/perfect-day never appear in retroactive output regardless of task state; and a direct regression test confirming a second scan against the exact same, unchanged tasks produces nothing (the core "respecting duplication rules" guarantee).

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (174/174). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth checking: the toast wording reads clearly for both the zero-found and found-some cases, the button doesn't feel like it's competing with the section content above it, and (most importantly, since this is new data-mutating logic) that tapping it on a real populated task list produces sensible, non-duplicate results and genuinely never triggers the celebration screen.

**Follow-up (2026-08-12): the Trophy Case's three-heading grouping (Unlocked / In Progress / Not Started) collapsed to two (Unlocked / Locked), per explicit user direction ("lets just have unlocked and locked, but in the locked section we put all the in-progress first, followed by all the not yet started, preserving the sorting within those lists").** `AchievementStatusGroup` narrowed from `'unlocked' | 'in-progress' | 'not-started'` to `'unlocked' | 'locked'`. `getGroupedAchievementCardStatuses` still computes the exact same three internal buckets with the exact same sorts it always did (`unlocked` by most-recent-`earnedAt`; `inProgress` by `progressFraction` descending; `notStarted` by the catalog's own fixed `ACHIEVEMENT_ORDER_INDEX`) -- the only change is in how they're packaged for the caller: `inProgress` and `notStarted` are now concatenated (`[...inProgress, ...notStarted]`) into one `locked` group instead of being returned as two separately-headed groups, so a still-in-progress kind always sorts ahead of every not-yet-started kind within the merged section, and each sub-list's own internal order is untouched. `TrophiesScreen.tsx` needed no logic or JSX changes at all -- confirmed via grep that nothing in it branches on the specific string `'in-progress'`/`'not-started'` anywhere; it already renders whatever `group.label`/`group.statuses` the function hands it generically (the same "no downstream kind/group branching" property this feature's earlier architecture refactor established for `AchievementKind` itself now holds for grouping too). Only a stale explanatory comment above the screen's `renderItem` (describing the old three-heading model) was updated to match.

3 tests in `__tests__/utils/achievements.test.ts` updated to the new two-group shape (no new tests needed -- the underlying sort behavior these tests exercise is unchanged, only which group label each assertion looks up against): the all-not-started/no-progress case now expects `groups.map(g => g.group)` to equal `['locked']` (not `['not-started']`); the closeness-ordering test now looks up the single `'locked'` group and additionally asserts a genuinely not-started kind (`milestone-10`, zero progress from this test's fixture) sorts after every in-progress kind within that same merged list, not just checking the in-progress kinds' own relative order against each other; and the readiness/`opportunityAvailable` test (`comeback`) now asserts it lands at index `0` of the merged `locked` list -- the only kind with any progress in that fixture, so its "sorts ahead of every not-started kind" guarantee reduces to a simple leading-position check.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected in count -- 3 tests updated in place, none added or removed). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth a look at whether the merged Locked section, likely now the single largest scrollable block in the grid (previously split visually across two headings), still reads as clearly organized (in-progress cards up top, not-started trailing below) without an internal divider between the two sub-portions -- none was added, since the request was specifically to merge them into one section, not to keep a soft seam between the two.

**Follow-up (2026-08-12): two tweaks to `AchievementBadgeCard`'s unlocked tiles, per explicit user direction ("1- I want to always show the unlock date, 2- for the earned count, I want a distinct little bubble badge tag that says times/count").** Previously the card's caption line did double duty -- `timesEarned > 1 ? "Earned N×" : format(date)` -- so a kind earned more than once (by two different tasks, or a repeatable kind re-crossed) never showed *when* it was most recently earned, only the count. Split into two separate, always-independent pieces instead of one either/or line:

- **The caption is now unconditionally the unlock date** (`format(parseISO(latest!.earnedAt), 'MMM d, yyyy')`) -- the `timesEarned > 1` branch was removed from this text entirely, so every unlocked card always shows its most recent unlock date regardless of how many times it's been earned.
- **A new `countBadge`** -- a small solid pill (`meta.color.base` fill, white `Nx` text, a `colors.surface` border matching `lockBadge`'s own ring-against-the-card treatment) renders only when `unlocked && timesEarned > 1`, in the same bottom-right corner slot `lockBadge` already occupies -- the two are mutually exclusive by construction (`lockBadge` only renders `!unlocked`, `countBadge` only renders `unlocked`), so reusing that one corner slot needed no new positioning logic and can't ever visually collide with the top-right testing delete button either.

Pure UI/styling change, no logic in `achievements.ts` touched (`timesEarned` and `latest` were already present on `AchievementCardStatus`, just recombined differently at the render site). Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth a quick look that the count pill's `Nx` text doesn't feel cramped at its `minWidth: 22` for a 2-digit count, and that it reads clearly against the emblem's own ribbon banner sitting just above it.

**Follow-up (2026-08-12): the count pill moved to the emblem's upper-right corner and switched to a fixed, theme-independent fill, per explicit user direction ("Try putting the counter in the upper right of the emblem. Also, it needs better contrasting coloring, keeping in mind it should work okay over various emblem color themes").** `countBadge` moved from `bottom:-2,right:-2` (the corner slot shared with `lockBadge`) to `top:-2,right:-2` -- badgeWrap's own top edge coincides with the ring/coin's own top edge (the halo behind it is `position:'absolute'` and doesn't contribute to badgeWrap's layout box, and the ribbon row sits below the ring in normal flow), so this lands the pill directly on the coin's own upper-right corner rather than floating relative to the taller ribbon-inclusive stack -- confirmed by tracing `TrophyEmblem`'s own layout (`emblemStyles.wrap`/`shadowWrap`/`ribbonRow`) rather than assumed. This also sidesteps any risk of colliding with the (testing-only) delete button in the card's own top-right corner, since `badgeWrap` is centered and considerably narrower than the full card width at every column count.

The pill's background changed from `meta.color.base` (the achievement's own theme color) to a fixed `#1C1C1E` near-black -- per the "keeping in mind it should work okay over various emblem color themes" ask, using a per-kind color here was the actual defect: this feature's 14/15 kinds span a huge base-color range (pale cream Gold through deep violet Magic Fire), so no single fixed text color could stay reliably legible against all of them, and a light base (e.g. Gold's `#B8860B`-adjacent cream) would have made white `Nx` text read poorly. A solid dark chip with white text guarantees the same contrast regardless of which kind's card it sits on -- the same "always-solid/dark, theme-and-color-independent" precedent this app already establishes elsewhere for exactly this legibility reason (`ToastBanner`, `OnboardingHint`'s bubble). `countBadgeText`/the pill's border/sizing are otherwise unchanged.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected -- pure styling change). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth checking the pill doesn't visually crowd the coin's icon at this small scale now that it overlaps the ring's own upper-right edge rather than sitting below the ribbon.

**Tried and reverted, same day: anchoring the pill's `left` edge (instead of `right`) so it grows outward to the right, plus a slightly translucent fill.** Attempted per explicit user direction ("I want it to flow out to the right, not left... ie the left of the tag should be fixed on the upper right. Also make the background just sliiightly transparent") -- `left: EMBLEM_SIZE - 2` in place of `right: -2`, and `backgroundColor: 'rgba(28, 28, 30, 0.85)'` in place of the opaque `#1C1C1E`. Rejected on sight ("Hm, Undo that") and reverted in full back to the prior `right: -2` / opaque `#1C1C1E` state described just above. Worth knowing if this is revisited: whichever edge reads as "wrong" wasn't the fix wanted here, so a future attempt at "flows to the right" shouldn't just retry the same `left`-anchor swap.

**Follow-up (2026-08-12): a much smaller nudge to the right, per direct follow-up ("I want it moved to the right just a little bit, not nearly as much as before").** After the full `left`-anchor flip above was rejected as too much, `countBadge`'s `right` offset changed from `-2` to `-6` -- still anchored on the right edge (so a wider two-digit count still grows leftward back over the coin, unchanged from the pre-flip behavior, which the user didn't ask to revisit here), just pushed a little further outward past the coin's own corner than before.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected -- pure styling change).

**Follow-up (2026-08-12): two real fixes on the full-screen Congratulations celebration itself, per explicit user direction -- "1- The positioning of the particles is wrong, its appearing in the upper left, it should be over the emblem+glow area. 2- The Unlock history title should juse be the unlock title it used to be, even if there are multiuple unlocks, use the original. And if and only if there are multiple unlocks show the history list below, but it should be a list that doesn't take up all the space, just as much as needed for the number of unlocks up to 3-5 then be a scrolling list".**

**1. A real positioning bug in `TrophyBadge.tsx`'s sparkle particles, root-caused rather than nudged.** The twinkling sparkle burst (`ParticleSystem`, wrapped in `styles.centeredFill`) was rendering clustered in the stack's own upper-left corner instead of scattered around the coin. Traced directly rather than guessed: each particle (`ParticleComponent`'s `wrapperStaticStyle`) is absolutely positioned with an explicit `left: 0, top: 0`, then translated outward by its own randomized `originX`/`originY` -- so the particles' effective origin point is wherever `left:0,top:0` actually resolves to, which is always the top-left corner of whichever "positioned" ancestor box directly wraps `<ParticleSystem>`, *not* that box's visual center. `centeredFill` (`...StyleSheet.absoluteFillObject, alignItems:'center', justifyContent:'center'`) fills the *entire* 260px `STACK_SIZE` box -- `alignItems`/`justifyContent` correctly centers the badge/halo/rings, which are ordinary (non-absolutely-positioned) children, but has zero effect on the particles, which already pin their own `left`/`top` and so never consult the parent's alignment at all. The particles' `(0,0)` origin was therefore the *stack's* own top-left corner, nowhere near the coin centered within it.

Fixed with a new `sparkleOrigin` style -- `position:'absolute', top:'50%', left:'50%'`, deliberately with **no** width/height. Absolutely positioned children don't contribute to a parent's own intrinsic size, so with nothing else inside it, this box collapses to a true zero-size point, landing exactly at the stack's geometric center (the same point `centeredFill`'s alignment already centers the badge/halo on) -- and since it's zero-size, its own top-left corner *is* that center point, so the particles' `left:0,top:0` origin now resolves there instead of the stack's actual corner. This is the same coordinate-space subtlety `TaskCard.tsx`'s own `ESTIMATED_BADGE_SPAWN_AREA` already gets right by a different route (that wrapper is sized/positioned to exactly match the badge itself, so 0,0 there already coincides with the badge's own corner) -- `TrophyBadge`'s bug was specifically that its wrapper was sized to the whole *stack*, not the coin, while still relying on `alignItems:'center'` centering that has no effect on absolutely-positioned children.

**2. `AchievementCelebration.tsx`'s meta block reworked: the original per-instance "Unlocked {date}" label is back, and the scrollable history list only appears (and only takes the room it needs) when there's actually more than one unlock to show.** A recent pass had replaced the original single "Unlocked {date}" label (naming *this* specific celebrated instance) with a generic "Unlock History" caption once the scrollable multi-instance list was added underneath it -- reverted per direct correction: the label is `Unlocked {format(parseISO(achievement.earnedAt), 'MMM d, yyyy')}` again, using this achievement instance's own `earnedAt`, unconditionally (regardless of how many times the kind has been earned overall).

`UnlockHistoryList` now only renders `if and only if` the kind has more than one recorded instance (`kindInstanceCount > 1`, computed once in `CelebrationContent` from `allAchievements`) -- a single unlock shows just the label and nothing more, matching "use the original" for the common case. Its own internal guard also tightened from `instances.length === 0` to `instances.length <= 1` as a second line of defense.

The list's own height changed from a flat, always-reserved `HISTORY_LIST_HEIGHT = 96` constant to `Math.min(kindInstanceCount, HISTORY_MAX_VISIBLE_ROWS) * HISTORY_ROW_HEIGHT` -- new `HISTORY_ROW_HEIGHT = 30` (an estimate of one row's real footprint: `paddingVertical: 6` top+bottom, a ~12px text line, the 1px divider border -- the same "estimate a known fixed-shape bit of content" shortcut this codebase already uses for `TaskCard`'s `ESTIMATED_BADGE_SIZE`/`DashboardStreaksView`'s `STREAK_ROW_HEIGHT`) and `HISTORY_MAX_VISIBLE_ROWS = 4` (splitting the requested "3-5" range). `UnlockHistoryList` gained a `maxHeight` prop (replacing the removed fixed `height` on `styles.historyList`) so the `ScrollView` genuinely shrinks to fit fewer rows -- two unlocks now renders a two-row-tall box, not a box padded out to a fixed size with empty space -- and only starts clipping/scrolling once the count actually exceeds the 4-row cap. `historyListHeight` is computed once per mount (stable for the achievement instance's whole lifetime, the same pattern `titleMinHeight` already established) and feeds directly into the `metaBlock` `RevealSlot`'s own `minHeight` (`64 + historyListHeight`, `0` when there's no list), so there's no layout shift once the slot reveals regardless of whether this kind has one instance or fifty.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected -- both fixes are pure positioning/layout logic, no tested `utils/` module touched). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth a careful look at: the sparkle burst now genuinely reads as centered over the coin+halo rather than off to one side, a single-unlock celebration shows just the plain date label with no empty list box beneath it, and a kind with exactly 2-4 unlocks shows a snugly-sized (not over-tall) list while 5+ correctly caps and scrolls.

**Follow-up (2026-08-12): two more fixes on the unlock history list, per explicit user direction ("1- the scrolling conflicts with the tap anywhere to close, it should be an exception to that behavior. 2- make it look a bit more like a timeline than a regular list").**

**1. Tap-to-dismiss conflict.** The whole celebration screen is one big `Pressable` (`onPress={handleDismiss}`) wrapping everything, including this list -- a touch inside the list that isn't claimed by anything else (a tap on a row, or a light scroll gesture that doesn't fully register as a drag) fell through to that outer handler and closed the screen mid-interaction. Fixed with the standard React Native fix for exactly this: `UnlockHistoryList`'s returned `View` became a no-op `Pressable` (`onPress={() => {}}`) wrapping the `ScrollView` instead -- once a touch starts inside this inner `Pressable`, it claims the responder for that touch sequence, so the outer dismiss `Pressable` never sees it at all. `ScrollView`'s own native drag/scroll handling is unaffected by this (it's negotiated independently of a simple tap-release `onPress`), so scrolling still works exactly as before -- only stray taps and light touches within the list's bounds no longer bubble up and close the screen.

**2. Restyled from a plain divided list into a vertical timeline.** Previously each row was icon-in-a-circle + task name + date sharing one line, separated by a `borderBottomWidth` divider.
