Redesigned into a proper timeline: a new `historyTrack` column (24px wide) to the left of each row holds a small colored dot (`historyDot`, shrunk 20→16px) with a thin connecting line (`historyConnector`, 2px) running through it -- each row contributes one line segment above its own dot and one below via `flex: 1` on both, so consecutive rows' segments meet exactly at the shared boundary between them and read as one continuous thread rather than separate dashes; the very first row's top segment and the very last row's bottom segment are hidden (`historyConnectorHidden`, transparent) since the timeline has nothing to connect to past either end. The task name and date, previously crammed onto one row next to the icon, now stack vertically in a new `historyContent` block beside the dot (title above, timestamp below) -- reading as a proper timeline "entry" rather than a data-table row. The old per-row `borderBottomColor` divider is gone entirely, replaced by the connector line doing that separating job visually instead. `historyRow` gained a `minHeight: HISTORY_ROW_HEIGHT` so the two flex connector segments have a reliable, consistent span to divide evenly around the dot, rather than being at the mercy of however tall that row's own text content happens to be.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected -- pure interaction/layout change, no tested `utils/` module touched). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth a careful look at: scrolling the list no longer dismisses the screen (the actual reported bug), that a genuine tap-outside-the-list still dismisses as expected (confirming the fix is properly scoped to just this one element, not swallowing dismissal globally), and that the connecting line reads as a clean unbroken thread across row boundaries rather than visibly gapping or overlapping at each seam.

**Follow-up (2026-08-12): a real regression in the tap-conflict fix above, caught and fixed the same day, reported directly ("The timeline is neither rendering correctly (its skinny, the first icon appears near the bottom and I can see no text) nor scrollable").** The no-op `Pressable` wrapper's *styling*, not the wrapping itself, was the actual bug: the fix moved `historyList`'s sizing/chrome style (`width`, `maxWidth`, `backgroundColor`, `borderRadius`, `paddingHorizontal`) plus the per-instance `maxHeight` off the `ScrollView` and onto the new outer `Pressable`, leaving the `ScrollView` itself with no style at all. That broke two independent things at once: React Native's `ScrollView`, unlike a plain `View`, does **not** auto-stretch to fill an unstyled parent -- it needs its own explicit width/height to know its own bounds, so without one it renders squashed to its narrowest content (explaining "skinny" and "no text visible" -- the text was there, just squeezed into a near-zero-width box); and with no bounded height of its own, it has nothing to compute scroll overflow against, so instead of clipping and scrolling it just renders unclamped (explaining "not scrollable" and the first row appearing to sit somewhere unexpected, since the whole unclamped list no longer matched `RevealSlot`'s own reserved `historyListHeight` space).

Fixed by reverting the styling back onto the `ScrollView` exactly as it worked before this Pressable wrapper existed (`style={[styles.historyList, { maxHeight }]}` back on `<ScrollView>`) and leaving the wrapping `Pressable` completely bare (`<Pressable onPress={() => {}}>`, no style prop at all) -- an unstyled `Pressable` simply shrink-wraps around whatever size its single child already declares, so it adds nothing to the layout beyond its touch-capturing role, which was the only thing it was ever meant to do.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected -- pure styling-placement fix, no logic changed). Not yet confirmed on-device -- this is the second pass on this exact list in one sitting, so worth confirming carefully this time: full width, all row text visible, correct scrolling, and the tap-swallowing behavior from the prior fix still holds with the styling moved back.

**Follow-up (2026-08-12): the list was still reported broken ("Sparkles look good. Unlock history list still totally broken. Compeltely redo and rethink the unlock history timeline") even after the styling-placement fix above -- rewritten from scratch rather than patched a third time, eliminating every remaining source of layout ambiguity instead of chasing one more guess.**

Since the immediately-prior fix (all sizing back on `ScrollView`, an empty `Pressable` wrapper) is exactly what shipped and was still reportedly broken, two *unconfirmed assumptions* underlying that whole approach were both eliminated at once rather than picking one to investigate further without the ability to test live on-device:

1. **Percentage width, replaced with a fixed pixel width.** `historyList`'s `width: '100%'` had to resolve against its own immediate parent (the raw wrapper, or `Pressable`), which itself sits inside a chain of `alignItems: 'center'` containers (`RevealSlot`'s wrapper -> `metaBlock` -> the list) that never declare their own explicit width -- each one shrink-wraps to its content by design. Whether a deeply-nested `'100%'` reliably resolves against real screen width through that kind of chain, rather than against something indeterminate, was never actually confirmed on a real device for this component (every prior note in this feature's history says "not yet confirmed on-device"). Rather than keep trusting that assumption, `historyList` now uses a plain fixed `width: HISTORY_LIST_WIDTH` (260px, a new constant) -- no percentage anywhere in the chain, nothing left to resolve ambiguously. 260px comfortably fits within `content`'s own inner width (screen width minus 64px of padding) on any realistic Android phone (this app's real target platform, generally >=360dp wide).
2. **Cross-axis `stretch` inference, replaced with an explicit shared height.** `historyTrack`'s two `flex: 1` connector segments need a *known* height to split evenly around the dot -- previously that height came from `historyRow`'s `minHeight` combined with default `alignItems: 'stretch'` propagating the row's actual (content-driven) height down to `historyTrack`, which itself had no explicit height of its own. Whether that stretch-then-split chain reliably resolves through an unstyled middle container was, again, unconfirmed. Now every element in a row -- `historyRow`, `historyTrack`, and `historyContent` -- shares the exact same explicit `height: HISTORY_ROW_HEIGHT` (bumped 30 -> 40, generous enough for the two-line task-name/date content plus real breathing room). Nothing is inferred from a sibling's content or a parent's stretch behavior; the connector math has a fixed, known quantity to divide regardless of how Yoga would otherwise have resolved it.

**The touch-swallowing wrapper also changed, from `Pressable` (with an empty `onPress`) to the lower-level `onStartShouldSetResponder`/`onResponderTerminationRequest` props directly on a plain `View`** -- the same primitive `Pressable` itself is built on, just without routing through `Pressable`'s own internal `Pressability` press-state machinery (highlight/delay/hitSlop handling, none of which this purely no-op "swallow the touch" role needs). `onStartShouldSetResponder={() => true}` claims the responder for any touch starting inside the list (so the full-screen dismiss `Pressable` never sees it -- the same reasoning the `Pressable` wrapper already had); `onResponderTerminationRequest={() => true}` explicitly allows `ScrollView`'s own native scroll gesture to take the responder away once an actual drag is detected, which is what lets scrolling still work. This removes `Pressable`-specific behavior as a variable, on the chance that was contributing to the reported breakage, without giving up the underlying "swallow touches here" mechanism.

`historyList` also picked up `overflow: 'hidden'` as a deliberate belt-and-suspenders safety net -- the `ScrollView`'s own `maxHeight` (still applied directly on it, not on the wrapper) is what should actually bound/clip its content, but this guarantees nothing can visibly spill past the box's edges even if that math is ever off.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected -- pure layout/interaction rewrite, no logic touched, no test coverage exists or is expected for this component-level work per this project's stated testing scope). Not yet confirmed on-device -- this is the third pass on this exact list; given the previous two both shipped "verified clean" via static checks and were still reported broken, static verification alone clearly wasn't sufficient here, so this one specifically needs an actual on-device look before being treated as resolved: full width at the fixed 260px, all row text visible and correctly positioned beside (not overlapping) the dot column, the connecting line reading as one continuous thread across row boundaries, genuine scrolling once there are more than 4 unlocks, and taps/scrolls within the list no longer dismissing the celebration screen.

**Follow-up (2026-08-12), same day: the rewrite above resolved rendering and tap-swallowing but not scrolling itself, per direct on-device report ("It looks great. It even doesn't close the view when tapping on the list. However I still can't scroll the list").** `ScrollView`'s import in `AchievementCelebration.tsx` switched from core `'react-native'` to `'react-native-gesture-handler'` -- the wrapping raw responder `View` (`onStartShouldSetResponder`/`onResponderTerminationRequest`, confirmed working for its actual job of swallowing taps) was left untouched, since the remaining failure was specifically about the *scroll gesture itself* never successfully taking the responder away from that wrapper, not about touch-swallowing being too aggressive. Core RN's `ScrollView` leans on the platform's own native touch interception to steal a drag away from whatever JS component currently holds the responder -- exactly the class of nested-touch negotiation `react-native-gesture-handler` exists to fix, and this app already depends on it for other reasons (`GestureHandlerRootView` wraps the whole app in `_layout.tsx`; `ToastBanner` already uses its `Gesture.Pan()`), so this is a well-precedented swap, not a new dependency. RNGH's `ScrollView` participates in its own more robust gesture arena, designed specifically to correctly negotiate against ancestor touch-claimers (both other RNGH gestures and legacy responder-based components like this file's raw `View` wrapper).

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected -- a single import swap, no other logic touched).

**Follow-up (2026-08-12), same day: the RNGH `ScrollView` swap above *still* didn't fix scrolling, per direct on-device report ("The unlock history scroll is still not working").** Rather than attempt a fourth touch/scroll-negotiation fix, the dismiss mechanism was redesigned to remove the conflict at its source, per explicit user direction ("Maybe we need to ditch the press anywhere behavior and instead add an explicit close button"), plus a second, related request ("I would like the device back button to close the congratulations rather than nav backward").

**The whole-screen tap-anywhere-to-dismiss `Pressable` is gone.** `CelebrationContent`'s outer element changed from `<Pressable onPress={handleDismiss} ...>` to a plain `<View>` with no press handling at all -- nothing in `content` needs to claim, swallow, or negotiate for touches anymore, which is what let `UnlockHistoryList`'s own touch-negotiation workarounds be removed entirely (the raw responder `View` wrapper deleted outright, and `ScrollView`'s import reverted from `react-native-gesture-handler` back to plain core `'react-native'` -- the RNGH swap was solving a problem that no longer exists once there's no ancestor `Pressable` competing against it). This is the real fix: three separate attempts to make scrolling work *despite* an ancestor claiming touches (a raw responder `View`, then an RNGH `ScrollView`) all failed on-device; removing the ancestor's claim entirely sidesteps the whole class of problem rather than trying to win the negotiation.

**Two explicit, unambiguous replacements for dismissal, both calling the same `handleDismiss`:**
1. **A close (X) button**, top-right, `insets.top + 12` down from the safe area (a new `closeButton` style -- a 40px translucent circular backdrop, not a bare icon, so it stays legible regardless of what's passing behind it in that region, since the badge/rings/confetti all sweep through there at various points in the sequence). Rendered unconditionally from the very first frame (not gated behind any reveal timer) so there's always a visible way out, rather than making the user wait through the ~8s reveal sequence with no escape.
2. **The Android hardware/gesture back button**, via `BackHandler.addEventListener('hardwareBackPress', ...)` in a new mount-scoped `useEffect` -- the handler calls `handleDismiss()` and unconditionally returns `true` (marking the event handled, preventing the default back-navigation that would otherwise pop the router stack underneath this screen). A no-op on iOS/web (no hardware back button there), so no platform guard was needed. Cleaned up via the subscription object's own `.remove()` (the modern `BackHandler.addEventListener` return shape, supported since well before this app's RN 0.81).

**Removed as a consequence**: the "Tap anywhere to continue" hint text and its `RevealSlot` (no longer applicable now that tapping the background does nothing), and the `spacer` style that existed purely to anchor that hint to the screen's bottom edge. `dismissHint`'s own style was deleted as now-unused.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected -- pure interaction/structure change, no logic in a tested `utils/` module touched). Not yet confirmed on-device -- this directly addresses a bug that survived three prior "verified clean" attempts, so it specifically needs a real device check before being treated as resolved: the close button dismisses correctly and stays tappable throughout the sequence, the Android back button dismisses instead of navigating away, and -- the actual original goal -- the unlock history list now genuinely scrolls with nothing left to compete for its touches.

**A new `milestone-50` tier added (2026-08-12), per explicit user direction ("Lets add a 50 milestone. Move the themes down and add a new top tier metal theme for 1000").** The three existing completion-count tiers (10/100/1000, Bronze/Silver/Gold) grew a fourth, slotted between the original 10 and 100. Rather than inventing a brand-new theme for the new *middle* slot, the existing medal themes shifted down one rank to make room, with a genuinely new top-tier metal added only at the very top:
- `milestone-10` -- Bronze, unchanged.
- `milestone-50` (new) -- inherited Silver's exact color set (`base: '#607D8B', glow: '#B0BEC5', accent: '#E1F5FE'`), moved down from the old `milestone-100`.
- `milestone-100` -- inherited Gold's exact color set (`base: '#B8860B', glow: '#DAA520', accent: '#FFF9C4'`), moved down from the old `milestone-1000`.
- `milestone-1000` -- a genuinely new theme, **Platinum** (`base: '#37474F', glow: '#90A4AE', accent: '#ECEFF1'`), deliberately a darker, cooler blue-grey than Silver's own lighter one (not a near-duplicate of it) -- read as more refined/premium than gold, matching the classic bronze-silver-gold-platinum rank convention many ranking systems already use.

Mechanically, adding the new tier required exactly one new `ACHIEVEMENT_META` entry (`{ type: 'fixed-threshold', metric: 'totalCompletions', target: 50 }`, icon `medal` matching the 100/1000 tiers' own icon, title "50 Completions!", a new flavor line "Halfway to triple digits and still going strong.") plus one line added to the hand-curated `ACHIEVEMENT_ORDER_INDEX` (renumbering the entries after it) -- no detection code changed at all, confirming this architecture's own "adding a new tier of an existing pattern needs one entry and nothing else" design goal directly: the generic `FIXED_THRESHOLD_ENTRIES` loop (derived straight from `ACHIEVEMENT_META`) picked the new tier up automatically, as did the Trophy Case's progress computation and every downstream file. Re-verified the full 16-kind palette for hex collisions after adding Platinum -- none found.

**Test coverage**: `__tests__/utils/achievements.test.ts`'s existing "fires each tier exactly once at its own threshold" test simply gained a `[49, 50]` case alongside its existing `[9,10]/[99,100]/[999,1000]` ones -- every other assertion in the file (catalog length, catalog order, grouping) already derived dynamically from `ACHIEVEMENT_KIND_ORDER`/`ACHIEVEMENT_META` rather than a hardcoded count, so the whole suite picked up the new kind automatically with zero other changes, all 181 tests still passing (same count, since this extended an existing test rather than adding a new one).

**Demo data**: `generate-achievements-test-data.py`'s `MILESTONE_TIERS` list gained a `(50, '#45B7D1')` entry -- the generation loop already builds each tier generically (non-consecutive every-other-day completions leaving `totalCompletions` one below the tier and `currentStreak` trivially small), so no other code changes were needed there either. Task count in both the script's own docstring and `demo-data/README.md`'s checklist bumped 11 -> 12 to match, with a new "Milestone 50 Demo" row added to the README's fire/action table (also firing Comeback alongside Milestone 50, same reasoning as the other milestone demo tasks' sparse completion history).

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth a look at the new Platinum theme's actual on-screen contrast/legibility (dark blue-grey base under white ribbon text) and that it reads as clearly distinct from Silver's own lighter blue-grey at a glance, not a near-duplicate.

**Follow-up (2026-08-12): all four `milestone-N` tiers switched from `repeatable: true` to `repeatable: false`, per explicit user direction after a discussion of the catalog's repeatability model ("make the milestones not repeatable. This still allows each task to complete the milestone, though, yes?" -- confirmed yes, and implemented as confirmed).** Repeatability here is scoped per-task (dedup keys are `${kind}:${taskId}` for any task-scoped kind), so this only ever blocked *the same task* from re-earning *the same* tier a second time -- something that, in practice, was already vanishingly rare, since `totalCompletions` is a lifetime counter that never un-crosses a threshold under normal use (the milestone tiers were already "self-limiting" while still marked repeatable, per this file's own prior documentation). Setting `repeatable: false` turns that soft, usually-true assumption into a hard guarantee -- closing the one real edge case where it wasn't airtight: undoing a completion via the app's own Undo affordance decrements `totalCompletions`, so without this, a subsequent redo could theoretically re-cross the same threshold a second time for the same task. It does **not** change anything about different tasks each earning their own independent copy of a tier -- that was never gated by `repeatable` in the first place, only by each task's own distinct dedup scope.

`ONE_TIME_KINDS` (derived automatically from the `repeatable` flag) grew from 2 entries (`first-completion`, `anniversary`) to 6, picking up `milestone-10`/`50`/`100`/`1000` with zero other code changes -- confirming the same "a kind's dedup behavior is a property of the kind itself" design this catalog has relied on throughout. `AchievementMeta`'s own `repeatable` field doc comment was substantially rewritten to properly distinguish two different reasons a kind ends up one-time (a structurally singular event, like `first-completion`/`anniversary`, vs. a softened-into-a-hard-guarantee self-limiting counter, like the milestones now) -- worth reading directly if this area is touched again, since the old comment's "totalCompletions-based kinds are naturally self-limiting anyway" reasoning was previously used to justify leaving them `repeatable: true`, and that same reasoning now justifies the opposite; the underlying fact didn't change, only which side of the tradeoff was chosen.

**Test coverage**: the existing `describe('repeatability', ...)` test's `ONE_TIME_KINDS` assertion updated to include all six one-time kinds (with an explanatory comment covering the "still per-task, not per-app" nuance directly, so a future reader doesn't have to re-derive it). No other test changes were needed -- the milestone dedup tests already construct their own `alreadyEarnedScopes` `Set` by hand rather than deriving it from `ONE_TIME_KINDS`, so they were already exercising the underlying per-task dedup mechanism independent of the `repeatable` flag's own value.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected -- one test updated in place, none added or removed).

**A one-shot "shockwave" ring added to `TrophyBadge`'s impact animation (2026-08-12), per explicit user direction: "when the emblem hits, I want to animate a ring that starts VERY thick, scales to much larger than the emblem, even larger than the glow, with aggressive eases out (start fast, ends slow) and fade to 1px line."** New `ShockwaveRing` component, distinct from the existing ambient `PulseRing` pool (which loops forever, three staggered instances) -- this fires exactly *once*, mounted (not delayed-then-animated) at `IMPACT_DELAY`, the same touchdown moment the frame rings/halo/shimmer already key off.

Same underlying technique as `PulseRing` (a single `progress` shared value driving `width`/`height`/`borderRadius`, `borderWidth`, and `opacity` together via one `withTiming`), but every constant pushed to match "shockwave" rather than "sonar ping": `SHOCKWAVE_MIN_SIZE` starts at `BADGE_SIZE` (120) and grows to `SHOCKWAVE_MAX_SIZE = BADGE_SIZE * 4` (480) -- deliberately clearing not just `GLOW_SIZE` (200, the halo) but also `PulseRing`'s own `PULSE_MAX_SIZE` (360), since "even larger than the glow" meant the *largest* existing ring in the whole medallion, not just the halo specifically. `SHOCKWAVE_START_BORDER` (26) is considerably thicker than `PulseRing`'s own `PULSE_MAX_BORDER` (8) for the requested "VERY thick" start, thinning to `SHOCKWAVE_END_BORDER = 1` (the "fade to 1px line"). Duration is a punchy `800`ms -- deliberately much shorter than `PulseRing`'s ambient `PULSE_DURATION` (4500ms), reading as a quick, explosive burst rather than a slow atmospheric pulse.

**Easing**: `Easing.out(Easing.poly(4))` -- a plain `Easing.out(Easing.cubic)` already has the requested "start fast, ends slow" shape, but `poly(4)` (one step more aggressive than cubic on the same quad→cubic→quart→quint power progression this file already established for `SHEEN_EASING`) pushes it further per the explicit "aggressive" ask.

**Wiring**: a `showShockwave` boolean (mounted via `setTimeout(..., IMPACT_DELAY)` in the same effect that already schedules the halo/frame-ring/sparkle timers, with matching cleanup) gates the component's first appearance -- no ongoing state needed beyond that, since it's a genuine one-shot with no repeat/pulsate phase to chain into afterward (unlike the halo/frame rings, which settle into an idle breathing loop once their own burst finishes). Rendered in the badge's JSX stack right after the halo and before the ambient `PulseRing` pool -- paints on top of the halo but underneath the badge itself, so it visibly expands outward from behind the coin. Colored with `glow` (the aura color), matching the halo/outer-frame-rings' own role in the base/glow/accent three-color scheme.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected -- pure additive animation component, no logic in a tested `utils/` module touched). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth a careful look at: the shockwave reads as clearly larger/more dramatic than the existing halo and pulse rings rather than blending into them, the 800ms duration feels punchy rather than either too fast to notice or dragging, and the quartic ease-out doesn't clip awkwardly against the badge's own bounce (which is still settling around the same IMPACT_DELAY moment).

**Follow-up (2026-08-12): four constants retuned, per direct follow-up ("Make the shockwave a little less large ending radius, a little thicker on initial, a little more aggressive ease out (fast at start, slower at end), and the whole animation lasts a bit longer").** All four moved by a modest, single step each:
- `SHOCKWAVE_MAX_SIZE`: `BADGE_SIZE * 4` (480) -> `BADGE_SIZE * 3.5` (420) -- still comfortably clears both `GLOW_SIZE` (200) and `PulseRing`'s own `PULSE_MAX_SIZE` (360), just not by quite as much.
- `SHOCKWAVE_START_BORDER`: `26` -> `32`.
- `SHOCKWAVE_EASING`: `Easing.out(Easing.poly(4))` -> `Easing.out(Easing.poly(5))` -- one step further up the same quad->cubic->quart->quint power progression this file already uses for `SHEEN_EASING`'s own "more dramatic" tuning.
- `SHOCKWAVE_DURATION`: `800` -> `1050`.

`SHOCKWAVE_END_BORDER` (still `1`, "fade to 1px line") and `SHOCKWAVE_MAX_OPACITY` (still `0.8`) were untouched -- not mentioned in this follow-up.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected -- pure constant-value changes, no logic touched).

**Follow-up (2026-08-12), same day: a second tuning pass, per direct follow-up ("Make the final size even small, the duration even longer, and I want the initial thickness to essentially be a full circle").**
- `SHOCKWAVE_MAX_SIZE`: `BADGE_SIZE * 3.5` (420) -> `BADGE_SIZE * 3` (360) -- still clears `GLOW_SIZE` (200) by a healthy margin (the one explicit "even larger than the glow" requirement from the original ask), now landing exactly at `PulseRing`'s own `PULSE_MAX_SIZE` (360) rather than past it.
- `SHOCKWAVE_DURATION`: `1050` -> `1400`.
- `SHOCKWAVE_START_BORDER`: replaced entirely -- previously two hand-picked fixed numbers in a row (`26`, then `32`), neither of which was ever meant to fully close the ring into a disc, just read as "thick." "Essentially a full circle" is a real geometric threshold, not another nudge: a ring's own inner opening has diameter `size - 2*borderWidth`, so a border of exactly `size / 2` closes that opening to nothing. Expressed as `SHOCKWAVE_MIN_SIZE / 2` (a derived value, not a new hardcoded number) so the two constants stay geometrically consistent if either is ever changed again, rather than needing to be hand-recomputed together.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected -- pure constant-value changes, no logic touched).

**Follow-up (2026-08-12), same day: opacity now starts fully solid, per direct follow-up ("Make the shockwave also start out full opacity").** `SHOCKWAVE_MAX_OPACITY` (which directly scales the ring's own `opacity = SHOCKWAVE_MAX_OPACITY * (1 - progress)` formula) went from `0.8` to `1` -- the ring now starts genuinely fully opaque at frame one instead of already 20% faded, still fading down to fully transparent by the animation's own end exactly as before. `SHOCKWAVE_END_BORDER`/duration/max-size were untouched -- this was purely the opacity ceiling.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected -- a single constant-value change, no logic touched).

**Follow-up (2026-08-12): every gemstone theme's `glow` color made more vibrant, per explicit user direction ("Make all the gemstone color themes use vibrant glow color").** Scoped to the five actual *gemstone* themes (Ruby, Topaz, Diamond, Emerald, Amethyst) -- Bronze/Silver/Gold (the medal family) were deliberately left untouched, since this file's own documentation already distinguishes them as a medal, not a gemstone, theme. Each moved from a comparatively muted/pastel Material shade to a saturated "A"-series equivalent -- the same "A-series stays fully saturated at high brightness where the plain numbered series desaturates toward pale" distinction already established and applied to the fire ladder's own accent tuning earlier in this feature's history:
- Topaz (`new-best-streak`): `#FFA726` -> `#FF9100` (Orange 400 -> Orange A400).
- Diamond (`anniversary`): `#84FFFF` -> `#18FFFF` (Cyan A100 -> Cyan A200; Cyan A400's own `#00E5FF` was ruled out since `streak-1000`'s own accent already uses it).
- Emerald (`perfect-day`): `#66BB6A` -> `#00E676` (Green 400 -> Green A400).
- Amethyst (`comeback`): `#AB47BC` -> `#D500F9` (Purple 400 -> Purple A400).

Ruby's glow was already `#FF1744` (Red A400) from an earlier pass this session (the `first-completion` rewrite below), so it needed no change here. `base`/`accent` were untouched across all five. Re-verified the full 15-kind palette for hex collisions after every change (`grep -oE "'#[0-9A-Fa-f]{6}'" ... | sort | uniq -d`) -- none found.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected -- pure hex-value changes).

**A real feature change (2026-08-12): the `first-streak` achievement kind was retired entirely and replaced with a new global kind, `first-completion`, per explicit user direction ("First Streak is basically redundant to streak started -- come up with a different, perhaps global, achievement in its place").** `first-streak` and `streak-2` fired at the exact same moment (a task's own `currentStreak` first reaching `FIRST_STREAK_THRESHOLD`, 2) -- the only thing distinguishing them was scope (global/one-time vs. per-task/repeatable), which read as two trophies for the literal same action rather than two genuinely different moments, exactly as the user observed.

`first-completion` instead celebrates the *very first completion of anything, ever* -- a genuinely earlier, distinct moment (day 1, not day 2 of a streak) that a per-task "streak started" kind structurally can't already cover. It reuses the exact same `fixed-threshold` mechanism every other kind in this catalog already uses -- `{ type: 'fixed-threshold', metric: 'totalCompletions', target: 1 }`, `scope: 'global'` -- requiring **zero new detection code**: the generic `FIXED_THRESHOLD_ENTRIES` loop already fires the instant *any* task's own `totalCompletions` first crosses 0 -> 1, and global dedup (a fixed `'global'` key, not per-task) means it can only ever fire once, for whichever task happens to be completed first, exactly mirroring how `first-streak` itself worked mechanically -- only the metric changed. This is the architecture doing exactly what it was built for: a new kind that fits an existing `ProgressStrategy` pattern needed one `ACHIEVEMENT_META` entry and nothing else. Confirmed via grep that no file outside `achievements.ts` needed any logic change -- only stale comments referencing the old kind name in `TrophiesScreen.tsx`/`AchievementCelebration.tsx` needed updating.

**Content**: icon `shoe-print` (distinct from `streak-2`'s `fire`, evoking "the first step of a journey" rather than reusing the streak-ladder's own flame language), title "First Steps!", flavor text "The hardest part is usually just starting. Nailed it.", ribbon `{ kind: 'fixed', text: 'FIRST STEP' }` (not a count -- see below). Deliberately **no `numberBlock`** -- the underlying value is always exactly 1 (the first completion, by definition), and animating a count-up to "1" would read as pointless rather than dramatic; falls back to the celebration screen's plain `title`, the same treatment `comeback` (no meaningful number of its own) already gets. Inherited Ruby's exact color set from the retired `first-streak` (base `#C62828`, accent `#FFEBEE` unchanged; glow bumped `#EF5350` -> `#FF1744` as part of the same pass, ahead of the broader gemstone-glow-vibrancy follow-up above).

**Every reference to the old kind was tracked down and either renamed or, where genuinely historical, left as an accurate past-tense record** (this file's own established practice -- prose describing what a past decision *was* stays intact rather than being retroactively rewritten to match current code, since first-streak genuinely did exist and did get a Ruby theme at the time). Functional/current-behavior comments across `achievements.ts`, `TrophiesScreen.tsx`, and `AchievementCelebration.tsx` were updated to `first-completion`; the two truly historical narrative blocks (documenting the original Ruby gemstone-theme decision, and the fire-ladder color history that once compared against `first-streak`'s old mirrored colors) were left intact with a short added note pointing to the rename, rather than rewritten as if `first-streak` never existed.

**Demo data**: `generate-achievements-test-data.py`'s "First Streak Demo" task (previously created with one pre-existing completion, engineered so tapping "complete today" crossed the old 2-day streak threshold) was repurposed into "First Completion Demo" -- now created with **zero** completions, since `first-completion` fires on a task's own `totalCompletions` crossing 0 -> 1, not a streak length. Every other task in this dataset already has pre-existing completions from import (so none of *their* first taps can cross that same 0 -> 1 boundary), making this the dataset's only task capable of firing `first-completion`, regardless of what order the demo tasks are tapped in -- confirmed by tracing the detection logic, not just assumed. `demo-data/README.md`'s checklist table updated to match (row renamed, "First Streak" -> "First Steps").

**Test coverage**: `__tests__/utils/achievements.test.ts`'s entire `describe('first-streak', ...)` block rewritten for the new metric (`totalCompletions` overrides instead of `currentStreak`), plus five other scattered call sites (a retroactive-detection test, two `getAchievementCardStatus` tests, the `ONE_TIME_KINDS` assertion, and the Trophy Case closeness-sort test) updated to match -- the closeness-sort test in particular needed a genuine behavioral update, not just a rename: since `first-completion` now reads a *different* stat than the `currentStreak`-based tiers it used to sort alongside, a task with `totalCompletions: 0` now correctly places it in the *not-started* portion of that test's fixture (same bucket as `milestone-10`) rather than the *in-progress* one `first-streak` used to land in -- a real, intentional consequence of the metric change, not a broken assertion to paper over.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, all passing -- test count unchanged, since this replaced/updated existing tests rather than adding new ones). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth prioritizing given this replaces a real detection path: completing a genuinely brand-new task (its very first-ever completion) should show the "First Steps!" celebration with the Ruby theme, and should never fire again afterward regardless of which task is completed next.

**Follow-up (2026-08-12): the Trophy Case's locked-card preview (TESTING ONLY) now shows "Locked" instead of a fabricated unlock date, per explicit user direction ("When viewing a congratulations for a locked achievement, just show 'locked' instead of the 'unlocked at' date").** `TrophiesScreen.tsx`'s `buildPreviewAchievement` (a synthetic, never-persisted `Achievement` built purely so a locked card can preview its celebration) stamps `dedupScope: 'preview'` and a throwaway `earnedAt` of "right now" -- `AchievementCelebration.tsx`'s meta-block label now checks `achievement.dedupScope === 'preview'` and renders the literal text "Locked" instead of formatting that fake timestamp as a real unlock date. Marked with a `TESTING ONLY -- REMOVE WHEN TOLD` comment tying it to the same preview feature already tracked for removal (session task #33) -- once that feature goes, this branch and its `dedupScope` check should go with it, since every *real* `Achievement` always carries a genuine `earnedAt`.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected -- a small conditional-rendering change, no logic touched).

**Mid-turn follow-up (2026-08-12): two tweaks to `TrophyBadge.tsx`'s sparkle particles, per direct user feedback ("The particle sparkles look pretty good but I want their spawn radious to be bigger, and it shouldn't appear until after the whole intro animation has completed").**

1. **Spawn radius widened.** `getSparkleConfig`'s `distance`/`distanceVariance` went from `BADGE_SIZE / 2 + 14` / `22` (a tight ring hugging just past the coin's own edge, roughly 52-96px from center) to `BADGE_SIZE / 2 + 50` / `30` (roughly 80-140px from center) -- now spans closer to the halo's own radius (`GLOW_SIZE/2` = 100) and the outermost frame ring's (~108), so the twinkle reads as scattered across the whole medallion's aura rather than clinging tightly to the coin.
2. **Delayed until the intro fully settles.** The sparkle burst (`<ParticleSystem>`) previously mounted unconditionally from the very first render, meaning it started spawning (staggered across its own `spawnWindow`) before the badge had even finished falling. A new `showSparkles` state (default `false`) now gates its first render, flipped via a `setTimeout(..., DROP_DURATION)` in the same mount effect that already drives the drop/halo/ring timers -- deliberately `DROP_DURATION` (when the badge's own fall+bounce `withTiming` fully completes, per `dropStyle`'s `remaining = 1 - drop.value` reaching exactly `0`), not the earlier `IMPACT_DELAY` (the first touchdown, before the decaying rebounds) that the frame rings/halo/shimmer key off -- those are meant to react to *impact*, while sparkles were asked to wait for the *entire* intro sequence, bounces included, to finish. Once shown for the first time, the existing periodic re-burst cycle (`sparkleKey`/`handleSparkleComplete`) is unaffected -- this only delays the very first appearance.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected -- pure config-value/timing change, no logic touched). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth checking that DROP_DURATION (1200ms) reads as the right "intro is over" moment for sparkles to kick in, rather than feeling like an oddly long wait before the first twinkle appears.

**A note on `streak-N` titles saying "Day" (2026-08-12): confirmed intentional, not a bug.** A review pass of this feature flagged the hardcoded "X-Day Streak!" titles/`numberBlock` units as potentially misleading for `days_per_week`/`days_per_month` tasks, reasoning that `currentStreak` might represent *periods* (weeks/months) rather than days for those frequencies. Directly corrected by the user: this app's own explicit design is that **every streak, regardless of frequency, is always counted in days** -- a quota (days_per_week/days_per_month) only decides *which* days qualify toward the streak, it never changes the counting unit itself. Confirmed directly against `streaks.ts`'s own quota-streak calculation (`currentStreak = priorChainIntact ? priorTrailingDays + currentPeriodDays : currentPeriodDays`, both terms literal day counts) -- the "Day Streak" language in every achievement title is therefore accurate as-is; no fix needed. Worth remembering for any future work in this area: `currentStreak`/`bestStreak` are day-counts everywhere in this codebase, never period-counts, regardless of frequency.

**Testing-only Trophy Case code fully removed (2026-08-12), per explicit user direction ("lets go ahead and remove that stuff now"), closing out session tasks #33 and #34.** Both pieces flagged `TESTING ONLY -- REMOVE WHEN TOLD` throughout this feature's development are gone:
- `TrophiesScreen.tsx`'s `buildPreviewAchievement` (the synthetic, never-persisted `Achievement` that let a *locked* card preview its celebration) deleted outright, along with its own explanatory comment block.
- `AchievementBadgeCard`'s `handlePlay` reverted to its original, pre-testing behavior -- only unlocked cards replay their celebration (`if (!unlocked || !latest) return;`); locked cards are no longer tappable at all (`disabled={!unlocked}` on the card's own `TouchableOpacity`, `accessibilityState={{ disabled: !unlocked }}`, and the accessibility label drops the "(testing)" suffix).
- The per-kind delete button (trash-can icon, top-right of unlocked cards) removed from `AchievementBadgeCard` entirely, along with its `handleDelete` handler, the now-unused `deleteButton` style, and the `onDelete` prop threaded down from `TrophiesScreen`.
- `deleteAchievementsByKind` removed from `achievementsStore.ts` entirely (both the interface entry and its implementation) -- confirmed via repo-wide grep it had no other caller, so this was genuinely dead code once the button using it was gone, not something to leave around "just in case."
- `AchievementCelebration.tsx`'s `dedupScope === 'preview'` check (which showed "Locked" instead of a real unlock date for a preview instance) reverted to its unconditional original: always `Unlocked {date}`, since no achievement can carry `dedupScope: 'preview'` anymore.
- `colors`/`ThemeColors` and `AchievementKind` props/imports that were only load-bearing for the removed delete button were cleaned up from `TrophiesScreen.tsx` too, rather than left as unused dead parameters.

**A new feature added in the same pass, replacing part of what the testing code used to make convenient**: turning the "Celebrate Achievements" switch **on** in Settings now offers a retroactive catch-up scan, per explicit user direction ("add a feature when achievements are switching on in settings, offer the option to retroactively check for achievements"). `SettingsScreen.tsx`'s new `handleAchievementCelebrationsToggle` still calls `setAchievementCelebrationsEnabled` unconditionally, but when the *new* value is `true` (turning on, not off -- there's nothing to offer when disabling), it also shows a toast with an action: "Achievements enabled! Want to check for ones you already qualify for?" / **Check Now**. Tapping the action runs the exact same `runRetroactiveScan` (from `achievementsStore.ts`) the Trophy Case's own "Check for Missed Achievements" button already uses, against the current non-archived task list, then shows a plain follow-up toast reporting the count -- identical message wording to `TrophiesScreen.handleRetroactiveScan`'s own toast, just triggered from a different entry point. Deliberately a toast-with-action rather than running the scan automatically -- this is a genuinely optional, user-initiated action (matching the app's own established "offer, don't decide for the user" toast pattern), not something to perform silently on the user's behalf just because a switch moved. `ToastBanner`'s own existing action-tap haptic (`impactAsync(Light)`, already baked into the banner itself for any toast with an `action`) covers the interaction haptic for free -- no new haptics code needed at the call site.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (181/181, unaffected -- removals and one additive handler, no logic in a tested `utils/` module touched).

**Locked-card preview restored, then six brand-new achievement kinds added on top (2026-08-12), per explicit user direction ("Actually add back in the preview achievement behavior, then add these new achievements you proposed earlier: Perfect Week, Century Club, Habit Collector, Early Bird... Night Owl... Beast Mode").**

**Preview restored first.** The exact locked-card-tap-to-preview behavior removed in the "Testing-only Trophy Case code fully removed" entry just above came back, close to its original shape: `TrophiesScreen.tsx`'s `buildPreviewAchievement(status)` (a synthetic, never-persisted `Achievement` built from whatever the card's own live progress calculation already found -- `dedupScope: 'preview'`, `id: \`preview-${status.kind}-${Date.now()}\`` -- so previewing a locked card can't fabricate real history) is back, and `AchievementBadgeCard`'s `handlePlay` again falls back to it for a locked card (`onPlay(unlocked && latest ? latest : buildPreviewAchievement(status))`) rather than being a no-op. The card's `TouchableOpacity` dropped the `disabled={!unlocked}`/`accessibilityState={{disabled}}` pair the removal added, and `accessibilityLabel` branches again ("Replay ... celebration" vs. "Preview ... celebration"). `AchievementCelebration.tsx`'s `dedupScope === 'preview'` check (showing "Locked" instead of a fabricated unlock date) is back too. Unlike the delete button (still gone, and not brought back -- this request only named the preview behavior), this is now treated as a **permanent** feature, not a testing-only one flagged for later removal -- the screen-level doc comments were reworded accordingly rather than re-adding a "REMOVE WHEN TOLD" marker.

**Six new kinds**, taking the catalog from 16 to 22 (`AchievementKind` union, `ACHIEVEMENT_META`, `ACHIEVEMENT_ORDER_INDEX` all extended). Four needed genuinely new `ProgressStrategy` variants -- none of the five existing patterns (`fixed-threshold`, `ratio-to-own-best`, `readiness`, `today-progress`, `task-age`) fit a cross-task aggregate sum, a live active-task count, a rolling time-of-day ratio, or a multi-day consecutive lookback:

- **Perfect Week** (`perfect-week`, icon `calendar-week`, Sapphire theme) -- a direct escalation of Perfect Day: 7 consecutive fully-perfect days (every due, non-archived task completed each day), not just one. New `{ type: 'perfect-day-streak'; days }` strategy. Global, repeatable, dedup scope is the window's own end date. Detection walks backward from the completion's own `date` reusing the exact same per-day due/completed check Perfect Day's own block already does, and -- critically -- only fires on the *crossing* day (today's trailing 7-day window is perfect, but yesterday's own trailing window wasn't), the same "fire on the crossing, not on every day the condition keeps holding" convention every other kind in this file already follows; without that second check, a long perfect streak would re-fire this every single day past day 7, not just once. `getAchievementCardStatus`'s matching case reuses the identical backward-walk against `today` for the Trophy Case's own live progress bar.
- **Century Club** (`century-club`, icon `crown`, a near-black Obsidian theme with a gold accent) -- a global, lifetime *sum* of `totalCompletions` across every active task, crossing a target of **1,000** (deliberately not the literal "100" the name evokes -- 100 combined completions across even two moderately-used tasks would trivially trip almost immediately, undermining the "milestone" framing the whole feature is going for; "Century" is used idiomatically here as a big round number, matching how the name works for a cricket/baseball "century" of runs rather than literally 100). New `{ type: 'total-completions-sum'; target }` strategy. One-time (a lifetime sum never un-crosses a threshold in normal use). Detection derives `prevSum`/`nextSum` from `activeTasksAfter`'s own current sum plus the isolated delta this one completion contributed to `nextTask`'s own `totalCompletions` -- no separate "every task's stats before this mutation" snapshot needed, since every *other* task's stats are already unchanged in `allTasksAfter`.
- **Habit Collector** (`habit-collector`, icon `view-grid`, Turquoise theme) -- reaching the free tier's own active-task cap (`MAX_ACTIVE_TASKS`, reused directly from `taskLimits.ts` rather than a duplicated constant) for the first time. New `{ type: 'active-task-count'; target }` strategy. One-time, global. Unlike every other kind, detection isn't a before/after transition check at all -- completing a task doesn't itself change how many tasks exist -- so it's just a fresh `activeTasksAfter.length >= MAX_ACTIVE_TASKS` check on every completion. This means it's necessarily detected **lazily**: only on the *next* completion after the 6th active task was actually added, not the instant it's added, since `completeTask` is this whole feature's only trigger point (there's no hook on `addTask` itself). Documented as an accepted architectural tradeoff, not a bug -- adding a second trigger point just for this one kind wasn't judged worth it, especially since the Trophy Case's own live progress bar (and the Settings "check for missed achievements" retroactive scan) both already show/catch this correctly regardless of timing.
- **Early Bird** (`early-bird`, icon `weather-sunset-up`, Dawn theme) / **Night Owl** (`night-owl`, icon `weather-night`, Midnight theme) -- a behavior-pattern pair, not a performance one: fires the first time at least half of your most recent completions (across every active task, combined) land before 7am / at-or-after 9pm. The first two kinds in this whole file to read raw completion **timestamps** (`TaskCompletion.completedAt`) rather than precomputed `.stats` -- everything else here has only ever needed a task's own streak/completion-count numbers. New `{ type: 'time-of-day-ratio'; hour; direction; window; minSamples }` strategy (`window: 14`, `minSamples: 10` -- "last X days" was interpreted as "last N completion *records*", a sliding window over the data actually available, rather than a literal last-N-calendar-days span, since a calendar-day window would silently degrade for a task completed less than once a day and doesn't have an obvious meaning across multiple tasks with different frequencies anyway). One-time each (a "you're an early riser" personality trait to note once, not something to re-celebrate every time a rolling window happens to qualify again). Both kinds share one detection block (sorting all active tasks' completions by `completedAt` descending, slicing the trailing window once) rather than two separate scans of the same data.
- **Beast Mode** (`beast-mode`, icon `lightning-bolt`, Voltage theme) -- layers a speed constraint on top of Perfect Day's own exact condition: every due, non-archived task completed *and* the span between that day's earliest and latest completion timestamp fitting within `BEAST_MODE_DURATION_MINUTES` (10). Global, per-day, **repeatable** (dedup scope is the date, same as Perfect Day -- a different day blitzing through everything again earns its own copy). Deliberately reuses Perfect Day's own existing `{ type: 'today-progress' }` strategy for its Trophy Case progress bar rather than inventing a new strategy type just for this one kind -- "how many of today's due tasks are done" is a real, meaningful prerequisite signal even though it can't capture the speed dimension on its own; a documented, accepted simplification rather than a new mechanism built for a single kind.

**`detectRetroactiveAchievements`** (the Settings/Trophy Case "check for missed achievements" scan) picked up matching loops for five of the six -- `perfect-week`/`century-club`/`habit-collector`/`early-bird`/`night-owl` are all reconstructable from a current task-list snapshot, following the exact same derived-entry-list pattern (`PERFECT_DAY_STREAK_ENTRIES`, `TOTAL_COMPLETIONS_SUM_ENTRIES`, `ACTIVE_TASK_COUNT_ENTRIES`, `TIME_OF_DAY_RATIO_ENTRIES`, each mechanically derived from `ACHIEVEMENT_META` the same way `FIXED_THRESHOLD_ENTRIES`/`TASK_AGE_ENTRIES` already are) this file has used for every strategy type since its own earlier architecture refactor. **Beast Mode is deliberately excluded**, joining Perfect Day's own pre-existing exclusion for the identical reason: it needs a *specific day's* own due-state and completion timestamps (which day, and how tightly clustered that day's completions were) -- there's no "current day" a snapshot-based scan can retroactively evaluate that against.

Two hex collisions/near-misses caught before finalizing the six new color themes: an exact duplicate `#FF7043` (Fire's own `glow` vs. Early Bird's first-draft `base`) was resolved by shifting Early Bird's base to `#FF8A65`; every other one of the 6 new themes' `{base, glow, accent}` triples was checked clean against the full 22-kind palette via a repo grep for duplicate hex literals.

17 new `detectCompletionAchievements` tests + 5 new `getAchievementCardStatus` progress tests added to `__tests__/utils/achievements.test.ts` (204 total, up from 181), covering each new kind's own crossing/threshold behavior, dedup, and (where relevant) its Trophy Case progress calculation -- following this file's own established house style (local fixture builders, plain `describe`/`it`). Two pre-existing tests needed updating to reflect the intentionally-grown catalog rather than being genuine regressions: `ONE_TIME_KINDS`'s expected set grew by the four new one-time kinds (`century-club`/`habit-collector`/`early-bird`/`night-owl`), and `getGroupedAchievementCardStatuses`'s "nothing unlocked or in progress" fixture test needed a real behavioral carve-out for `habit-collector` specifically -- its own progress metric (active task count) always reads *some* nonzero progress the instant even one task exists, which no other kind's progress metric does, so a fixture with exactly one task now legitimately shows it as the sole "in progress" kind rather than "not started" like everything else. One test written during this pass was itself wrong, not the code: an "excludes archived tasks from the sum" test for Century Club's `getAchievementCardStatus` case failed because that function has never filtered archived tasks itself -- by established convention (see every other strategy case in the same switch) it trusts the caller's `activeTasks` parameter is already archive-filtered (`TrophiesScreen` passes `tasks.filter(t => !t.archived)`); the test was removed rather than "fixing" the code to duplicate a filter the calling contract already guarantees.

`demo-data/README.md`'s Achievements test dataset section now flags a known gap: the dataset predates this addition and has no dedicated tasks/checklist rows for any of the six new kinds yet, with a note on what each would actually need (Perfect Week's genuine 7-consecutive-day span, Century Club's large cross-task sum, Habit Collector's "just needs the count, no completion trigger" quirk, Early Bird/Night Owl's need for the generator to stamp specific *hours* on `completedAt` rather than just day-level dates, Beast Mode's tightly-clustered same-day timestamps) -- left as a flagged follow-up rather than attempted in this same pass.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (204/204). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth prioritizing: Perfect Week firing on a genuine real 7-day perfect run (not just a synthetic one), Century Club/Habit Collector's global (not per-task) celebration reads sensibly with no task chip in the description, Early Bird/Night Owl actually reading real device-local hours off `completedAt` correctly, Beast Mode's minute-span math against real back-to-back completions, and the restored locked-card preview behavior still working for all 22 kinds including the six new ones.

**A round of on-device color-theme touch-ups (2026-08-12) across several of the six new kinds, plus one real legibility fix.** All via direct back-and-forth hex tuning (no structural changes) -- each verified for palette-wide hex collisions and `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (204/204) after every change:
- **Night Owl**: accent flipped from a pale periwinkle (`#C5CAE9`) to a near-black navy (`#10102B`), then that near-black and the base (`#283593`) were swapped outright -- base is now the near-black, accent the indigo.
- **Early Bird**: accent went through several rounds (pale cream → vivid orange `#FF6F00` → bright gold `#FFDD00` → a lighter `#FFF176`) chasing "brighter," then "lighter," landing on `#FFF176`.
- **Beast Mode**: accent swapped from a washed-out pale lavender (`#EDE7F6`) to an electric lime (`#C6FF00`) for a real "spark" against the purple base/glow, per "give it an interesting accent color."
- **Streak-50 (Inferno)**: went through the most iteration. The accent alone was retuned five times (yellow → pink → pale pink → peach) without landing, since the underlying problem was the *base/glow* themselves (a crimson-to-magenta pair from an earlier session, meant to bridge toward streak-100's violet Magic Fire) no longer reading as cohesive. Two full-triple reworks followed: first a fresh "char-red ember + white-hot core" concept (rejected, "not great"), then -- per direct redirect ("start with the colors in streak-25 and just make it stronger") -- the final version derives directly from streak-25's own Raging Fire triple (`#FF3D00`/`#FF9800`/`#FFEB3B`) with every channel pushed further in the same direction: `base: '#D50000'` (deeper red), `glow: '#FF6100'` (hotter orange), `accent: '#FFB300'` (warmer amber-gold, nudged from an initial `#FFD600` per "a little more orange"). Reads as a genuine escalation of Raging Fire's own hue family now, not a swap to an unrelated one.
- **New Best Streak**: theme renamed from Topaz to **Gilded**, per explicit direction to mirror Century Club's own Obsidian structure (a dark, near-neutral base/glow carrying a single bright accent) but warm instead of cold -- `base: '#3E2E00'` (shadowed bronze-black), `glow: '#8C6D1F'` (antique bronze), `accent: '#FFD700'` kept unchanged (this app's own established "best streak" gold, already used by `DashboardStreaksView`/`TaskStatsScreen`/`TaskCard`'s stats face outside this file).

**The real fix, then a correction**: with Gilded and Obsidian both landing on a genuinely dark `base`, `AchievementCelebration.tsx`'s hero number (`bigNumber`) -- which had always been colored `kindColor` (i.e. `meta.color.base`) -- was reading as nearly invisible against the celebration screen's own dark backdrop for those two kinds. First fix switched the color source to `kindAccentColor` for *every* kind, reasoning that accent is already this file's established "boldest of the three, always legible" role (the same color `TrophyBadge`'s own coin icon/ring border already use). Reverted almost immediately per direct user feedback ("I actually liked the base color for the text color for most of the others") -- for most themes, `base` was the genuinely preferred counter color, and the universal swap only should have applied to the couple of kinds actually dark enough to need it.

Replaced with a proper per-kind opt-in instead: `AchievementMeta.color` gained an optional `useAccentText?: boolean` field (defaulting to falsy -- use `base`, the normal/preferred case), set `true` only on `new-best-streak` (Gilded) and `century-club` (Obsidian), the two kinds actually flagged as too-dark. `bigNumber`'s color now reads `meta.color.useAccentText ? kindAccentColor : kindColor`. `bigNumber`'s other consumers (`TrophyBadge`'s `color` prop, `DescriptionText`'s fallback `taskColor`, `Confetti`'s `baseColor`, `UnlockHistoryList`'s `kindColor`) were left untouched throughout both passes -- this was scoped to the one element specifically flagged (the animated counter text), not a blanket base-to-accent change across the screen. Worth noting: Night Owl's own base is now also a near-black (`#10102B`, after an earlier base/accent swap this same session) and could plausibly need the identical `useAccentText: true` treatment, but wasn't flagged and wasn't touched here -- worth checking on-device.

**The Trophy Case's own "Check for Missed Achievements" button removed (2026-08-12), per explicit direction -- the Settings-side equivalent deliberately kept.** `TrophiesScreen.tsx`'s `handleRetroactiveScan` handler, its `ListFooterComponent` button, and the now-unused `scanButton`/`scanButtonText` styles were all deleted; the `runRetroactiveScan` store selector and `useToast` import (only ever used by that one handler in this file) were removed too, since neither had any other caller here. `useAchievementsStore`'s own `runRetroactiveScan` action itself is untouched and still very much in use -- `SettingsScreen.tsx`'s "Celebrate Achievements" toggle still offers the identical scan via its own toast-with-action ("Achievements enabled! Want to check for ones you already qualify for?" / **Check Now**, see this section's own earlier entry) when the setting is switched on, which the user explicitly asked to keep. The Trophy Case grid itself is otherwise unaffected -- cards, sections, and the per-kind preview/replay behavior all work exactly as before, just without the standalone scan entry point at the bottom of the list.

**`detectRetroactiveAchievements` rebuilt as a genuine chronological replay (2026-08-12), replacing its original one-shot current-stats snapshot check.** Prompted by a direct question: since `TaskCompletion` already carries real dates/timestamps, couldn't the retroactive scan replay a task's *entire* history instead of only ever seeing its current state? Answer: yes, and the old snapshot approach had a real, previously-undocumented blind spot as a result -- a **repeatable** kind's current state can only ever reveal its *most recent* crossing. A task whose streak has broken and restarted three times only shows one live "Streak Started!" moment in a snapshot of `currentStreak`, even though it genuinely earned that moment three separate times across its real history; the old scan could never recover the other two.

**The core mechanism**: `app/utils/streaks.ts`'s `calculateTaskStats` (and its two frequency-specific helpers, `calculateDueDayStats`/`calculateQuotaStats`) gained an optional `asOfDate: Date = new Date()` parameter -- every one of the two functions' own two hardcoded `startOfDay(new Date())` lines became `startOfDay(asOfDate)`. Fully backward compatible (every existing call site across the app omits it and gets byte-for-byte the same behavior as before), but it's what makes a genuine historical replay possible at all: without it, `calculateTaskStats` could only ever answer "what are this task's stats *right now*," with no way to ask "what would they have been on some past date." All existing `calculateTaskStats` tests/call sites needed zero changes (confirmed via a full repo grep before touching it).

**The replay itself**, in `achievements.ts`: every completion across every active task becomes one "event," and -- critically -- events are ordered and evaluated by **`completion.completedAt`** (the real-world moment the press was actually recorded), not `completion.date` (which calendar day it counts *for*). These two dates matter for genuinely different reasons and conflating them was the source of two real bugs caught before this shipped (both found via hand-tracing `calculateDueDayStats`'s actual logic against constructed fixtures, then confirmed with a throwaway diagnostic script -- guessing the day-by-day streak math by hand proved unreliable enough that direct verification was necessary at several points):
- `completedAt` drives both replay *order* and the `asOfDate` passed to `calculateTaskStats` for that event's prev/next stats -- because that's what live completion actually does: `taskStore.completeTask` (via `withUpdatedStats`) always computes stats against real "now" at press time, *regardless* of which day the press is actually *for*. This is what lets a genuine backfill (completing a past day via the Calendar tab, well after the fact) "bridge" an old closed run and an already-open one into a single jump past a prior record in one step -- the *only* way `new-best-streak` can ever fire at all, since plain +1/day growth can tie a prior best but never single-day-cross it (a pre-existing, already-documented constraint this rewrite didn't change, just finally modeled correctly). Evaluating by `completion.date` instead (the first attempt) got this wrong in a subtle way: it correctly captured an *immediate*, same-day bridge but couldn't represent an actual backfill recorded well after an open run already existed, since the open run's own later dates hadn't been "reached" yet by a date-ordered walk.
- `completion.date` still drives `detectCompletionAchievements`' own `date` parameter unchanged -- perfect-day/perfect-week/beast-mode/anniversary's date-scoped logic is about which calendar day something happened *on*, not when it was recorded.
- A related, second bug: the first replay draft reused a task's own cached snapshot as `prevTask` between events without recomputing its `.stats` fresh at the *current* event's asOfDate -- `comeback` specifically depends on `streakStatus` reading `'expired'` "as of right now," and a stale snapshot (computed at an earlier event's own asOfDate) silently misjudged that, since a due day that read as merely "pending" at the time it was cached needs to be re-evaluated as a real, settled miss once enough real time has actually passed.

**Dedup got genuinely more subtle**, since a naive "have I seen this exact scope before" check can't distinguish a repeatable task-scoped kind's *already-recorded* crossing from a *new* one -- every instance of e.g. `streak-10` for the same task shares the identical dedup scope (`task.id`) regardless of which restart produced it. New `REPEATABLE_TASK_SCOPED_KINDS` (derived from `repeatable && scope === 'task'`, covering the seven `streak-N` tiers plus `new-best-streak`/`comeback`) gets a **counting**-based guard instead of set-membership: how many times each `(kind, taskId)` pair is *already* on record, decremented as the replay rediscovers them in chronological order -- only a crossing beyond what's already recorded counts as genuinely new. Every other kind (one-time, or date-scoped-and-repeatable like `perfect-day`/`perfect-week`/`beast-mode`, whose dedup scope already varies per instance) keeps the simpler exact-scope check, mutated as the replay discovers new ones, exactly mirroring how the live store's own `alreadyEarnedScopes` grows across real completions.

**A genuine capability upgrade, not just a bug fix**: `comeback` and `perfect-day` (and, by the same mechanism, `perfect-week`/`beast-mode`) are now correctly detectable retroactively -- the *old* scan explicitly couldn't reach either (documented at the time as "neither can be reconstructed from a current snapshot alone"), since both need to know state *as of a specific past moment*, which only a real replay -- not a snapshot -- can answer. `task-age` (anniversary) and `active-task-count` (habit-collector) deliberately stay as their own simple current-snapshot checks outside the replay, since neither is completion-driven and both are one-time, so replay wouldn't add anything.

**Two tests needed real fixture rewrites**, not just adjustment: `detectRetroactiveAchievements`'s whole test suite previously constructed tasks via pre-set `.stats` overrides with no real `completions` array -- meaningless for a replay that's entirely driven by completion records. Rewritten with genuine completion histories (`consecutiveCompletions`/`daysAgo` helpers against a fixed `REF_TODAY`, so tests stay deterministic). The `new-best-streak` fixture mirrors the demo dataset's own already-proven "closed run + gap + already-open run, backfill the gap" construction exactly, with the gap completion's own `completedAt` deliberately set to `REF_TODAY` (well after its `date`) to actually simulate a backfill rather than an ordinary same-day tap -- confirmed to award the full bridged value (9: 6 old + 1 bridge + 2 open) via a throwaway diagnostic script before trusting it as a real assertion. The `comeback` fixture needed *exactly* one missed day (not several) between the closed run and the revival, per this app's own pre-existing "more than one trailing miss decays `streakStatus` all the way to `'never_started'`, not `'expired'`" behavior (already documented elsewhere in this file, and already worked around identically by the demo dataset's own "Comeback Demo" task) -- also caught by direct diagnostic verification, not assumed. One new test also caught a real fixture bug of its own: a `perfect-day` test's two tasks relied on `makeTask`'s default `createdAt` (real wall-clock "now" at test-run time), which -- since the fixed `REF_TODAY` reference sits in the past relative to whenever these tests actually run -- made the replay's own "did this task exist yet" filter wrongly exclude both tasks from the due-task check; fixed by giving both an explicit, safely-past `createdAt`.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (205/205, +1 from a stronger `new-best-streak` value assertion) across every touched file (`streaks.ts`, `achievements.ts`, `achievements.test.ts`). Performance: one `calculateTaskStats` call pair per completion event (each O(that task's own completion count so far)) plus a cheap per-event `.filter()`/`.map()` over the active task list -- for realistic personal-habit-tracker data (a handful of tasks, at most a few thousand days of combined history), comfortably fine for a manual, occasional "check for missed achievements" scan. Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth prioritizing given how much this rewrite changed: running the scan against a task with a genuinely multi-restart streak history and confirming multiple distinct `streak-N` instances actually appear (not just one), and confirming a real Calendar-tab backfill still correctly triggers `new-best-streak` live (unaffected by this change, but worth reconfirming given how subtle the `completedAt`-vs-`date` distinction turned out to be).

**Night Owl's `useAccentText` flag set (2026-08-12), joining Gilded/Obsidian.** Its base is the same near-black `#10102B` from the earlier base/accent swap, so the celebration screen's hero number was flagged as likely needing the identical treatment when that flag was first introduced -- confirmed and applied here.

**The celebration screen's multi-unlock label upgraded (2026-08-12).** Previously, once a kind had more than one recorded instance, the meta block still only ever showed the single celebrated instance's own "Unlocked {date}" -- with a full scrollable history list right below it, that read as understating what's actually there. Now, whenever `showHistoryList` is true (more than one instance), the label itself switches to "Unlock History" alongside a small inline "N×" count bubble (reusing the same solid-dark/white-text pill language `TrophiesScreen`'s own `countBadge` already established, just laid out inline in a row rather than absolutely-positioned on a card corner) -- new `unlockedHistoryHeader`/`unlockCountBadge`/`unlockCountBadgeText` styles in `AchievementCelebration.tsx`. A single unlock still shows the original plain per-instance date, unchanged.

**Perfect Day and Perfect Week both gained a minimum due-task requirement (2026-08-12), per explicit user direction, after a design conversation about Beast Mode surfaced the identical gap in these two kinds.** Both had always required *every* due task be completed, but only guarded against a day with literally *nothing* due (`dueTasks.length > 0`) -- a day (or a whole week of days) where exactly one task happened to be due still fully qualified as "perfect," with nothing really at stake. New `PERFECT_DAY_MIN_DUE_TASKS = 2` constant -- the smallest change that rules out the trivial one-task case without meaningfully raising the bar for someone with a modest task list -- now gates both: `detectCompletionAchievements`' own `perfect-day` block and `isPerfectDayOn` helper (which `perfect-week`'s own crossing check is built on) both require `dueTasks.length >= PERFECT_DAY_MIN_DUE_TASKS`, and `getAchievementCardStatus`'s matching `today-progress`/`perfect-day-streak` progress-bar cases were updated identically, so the Trophy Case's own live progress reads never disagree with what detection would actually award. Five existing tests (four `detectCompletionAchievements` perfect-day/perfect-week tests, one `getAchievementCardStatus` progress test) needed a second due-and-completed task added to their fixtures to keep testing what they originally intended, rather than now passing vacuously off the new minimum alone; a new dedicated regression test (`does not fire when only a single task is due, even if completed`) covers the minimum itself directly. Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (206/206, +1 new test).

**A live design conversation about Beast Mode surfaced two real, un-fixed gaps in its own condition, not yet acted on.** Directly asked "does it trigger with only 1 due task?" -- confirmed yes: with a single completion that day, the earliest/latest timestamp span is trivially `0` minutes, so it fires automatically with no actual speed being tested. Also confirmed the 10-minute window is a flat constant with no scaling by due-task count, making it implicitly *easier* on light days and *harder* on busy ones -- the opposite of what "how fast did you blitz through everything" should probably feel like. Per direct follow-up ("I don't think Beast Mode really works... give me some completely fresh creative achievement ideas"), four alternative concepts were proposed but none chosen yet: **Full Send** (every active task has a live `currentStreak > 0` at once), **Unwavering** (a task holds >90% `completionRate` over a 60+ day lifetime), **Phoenix** (a bigger Comeback -- reviving a streak that sat expired 14+ days, not just one missed day), and **Weekend Warrior** (every task due both Saturday and Sunday, several weekends running) -- all deliberately avoid needing any notion of "how long a task should take," the root complaint about Beast Mode. Beast Mode's own copy was still brought into present tense alongside every other kind's text (see below) since it's still live pending a decision, but its actual detection logic is untouched -- worth returning to once a direction is picked.

**Every achievement's user-facing text converted to present tense (2026-08-12), per explicit direction.** `describe`/`triggerSuffix`/`triggerStandalone` across all 22 `ACHIEVEMENT_META` entries, plus the clearest past-tense `flavorText` lines, were rewritten -- e.g. streak-N's `"${taskName}" hit a 5-day streak` → `hits a 5-day streak`, `reached a ${v}-day streak` → `reaches`; milestone-N's `logged its Nth completion` → `logs`; perfect-day's `Every task completed today` → `Every task is completed today`; first-completion's `Nailed it.` → `Nailing it.`; new-best-streak's `you just outdid yourself!` → `you're outdoing yourself!`; streak-1000's `You've entered myth territory` → `You enter myth territory`; comeback's `Like you never left.` → `You're right back where you belong.`; century-club's `You've officially put in the reps.` → `You're officially putting in the reps.`. Two entries deliberately kept present-*perfect* phrasing rather than forcing simple present, since the alternative reads wrong: anniversary's `"${taskName}" has been going for a full year` (an ongoing duration culminating now genuinely calls for present perfect -- "goes for a year" doesn't mean the same thing) and its own `has been going strong for ${v} days` trigger text stayed as-is, alongside the newly-added `you've never missed a beat` (present perfect, matching the same tense as the sentence it sits beside). No test assertions referenced any of the old copy strings directly (confirmed via grep before editing), so no test changes were needed here. Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (206/206, unaffected).

**The present-tense copy sweep reverted, per direct correction (2026-08-12): "I don't really like all the present tense, I just meant I didn't want terms like 'that day' instead of 'this day' or 'today'."** The prior entry's broad grammatical-tense conversion (every `describe`/`triggerSuffix`/`triggerStandalone`, plus several `flavorText` lines, rewritten from past tense to present tense across all kinds) was a real over-read of the original ask -- reverted in full, back to the exact original past-tense phrasing (`"${taskName}" hit a 5-day streak`, `reached a ${v}-day streak`, `logged its Nth completion`, `Nailed it.`, `you just outdid yourself!`, etc., all restored verbatim). What was actually wanted was much narrower: `perfect-day`'s own `triggerStandalone` was the *only* place "that day" appeared in any user-facing string (confirmed via a repo-wide grep before touching anything this time) -- `All N tasks due that day were completed.` / `Every task due that day was completed.` became `...due today were completed.` / `...due today was completed.`, keeping the original past tense, just swapping the one phrase. Noted directly in that field's own comment: this is technically imprecise for a backfilled past-date completion (not literally "today" in wall-clock terms), an accepted tradeoff for more natural phrasing in the overwhelmingly common same-day case, not an oversight. Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (203/203, unaffected -- no test asserted on any of the reverted or changed copy strings, confirmed both times via grep before editing).

**Beast Mode removed entirely (2026-08-12), per explicit direction ("let's just get rid of Beast Mode") after the design conversation above surfaced two un-fixable gaps** (fires trivially with a single due task, since one completion's own timestamp span is always 0 minutes; its flat 10-minute window doesn't scale with due-task count, so it's implicitly easier on light days and harder on busy ones) and four alternative concepts were offered with none chosen -- rather than leave a known-broken achievement live pending a future replacement, it's gone. Catalog shrinks from 22 kinds to 21. Removed: the `'beast-mode'` member of `AchievementKind`; `BEAST_MODE_DURATION_MINUTES`; its `ACHIEVEMENT_META` entry (icon, title, describe, flavorText, ribbon, triggerStandalone, the Voltage color theme); its own bespoke detection block in `detectCompletionAchievements` (the perfect-day-plus-speed-constraint logic); and its entry in `ACHIEVEMENT_ORDER_INDEX` (the rest renumbered to close the gap). `detectRetroactiveAchievements` needed no changes -- Beast Mode was already excluded from that replay (documented at the time: it needs a specific day's own clustered timestamps, which a snapshot-independent scan was never going to reconstruct anyway), so removing it left no dangling reference there. Three stale comments elsewhere in the file (the six-kinds-added header comment, the completedAt-vs-date replay-ordering comment, the cross-task stats-freshness comment) were updated to stop naming it, since it's not a "removed, historical" fact worth preserving in-line the way e.g. Topaz→Gilded's rename history is -- it never shipped to a real user, only existed within this same session. The whole `describe('beast-mode', ...)` block in `achievements.test.ts` was deleted outright (three tests). Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (203/203, down from 206 -- the three deleted beast-mode tests, with no other count changes since this session's other work landed after the last full-suite check).

**The unlock-history count bubble's contrast fixed (2026-08-12), per direct report ("basically black on black").** `AchievementCelebration.tsx`'s `unlockCountBadge` copied `TrophiesScreen`'s own `countBadge` styling (a flat `#1C1C1E` fill, no border) without accounting for context -- on `TrophiesScreen` that badge sits on a themed card surface with real separation from it, but here it sits directly on this screen's own near-black backdrop (`tint: rgba(5, 8, 16, 0.92)`), with nothing distinguishing the two. Rather than tint the fill with the achievement's own accent color (rejected as a fix -- accent colors span the whole catalog from near-white to near-black, so there's no single text color that would stay legible against all of them), added a `borderWidth: 1.5, borderColor: 'rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.4)'` -- a plain semi-transparent white outline gives the badge definition regardless of backdrop shade, without needing to reason about any specific kind's own color at all.

**`detectRetroactiveAchievements` sped up (2026-08-12), per a direct report that the Settings "Check achievements" scan "took awhile" for 8 tasks and a few months of data.** Root cause: two genuinely quadratic-ish hot spots inside the replay loop, both independently rediscovered and fixed:
1.
