Early Bird/Night Owl's own check (inside `detectCompletionAchievements`) re-flattened and re-sorted *every* active task's *entire* accumulated completions from scratch on every single replay event, to derive a 14-completion trailing window -- for a growing pool approaching ~1,000 completions by the end of a real scan, that's a real `O(n log n)` cost repeated once per event.
2. Perfect Day/Perfect Week's own day-completion checks called `isTaskCompleted` (a linear `.find()` scan over a task's own completions array) repeatedly per event, including inside Perfect Week's own 7-day-times-two backward walk -- redundant with the fact that the exact same underlying data barely changes from one event to the next.

Directly asked whether fixing this risked duplicating logic already living in `calculateTaskStats`/`detectCompletionAchievements` -- a fair concern given this codebase's own repeated, explicit bias against exactly that (see e.g. the `getQuotaPeriodInfo` extraction, done specifically to kill three independent reimplementations of one idea). Answer, worked through directly: these two hot spots could be fixed with **zero** logic duplication, since both are purely about *how data gets sourced* into the same unchanged comparisons, not new rules -- but genuinely fixing `calculateTaskStats`'s own `O(n)`-per-call cost (it always re-walks a task's *entire* history from scratch, by design, which is exactly what makes it trustworthy) would require either a second, incremental reimplementation of the day-walking logic already in `calculateDueDayStats`/`calculateQuotaStats` (a real duplication risk) or a bigger, more invasive refactor of that same authoritative implementation to support incremental extension. Per explicit direction, only the two duplication-free fixes were made; `calculateTaskStats` itself was deliberately left untouched.

The actual changes: `detectCompletionAchievements` gained a new, fully optional trailing `options` parameter (`{ completionCountsByTaskId?, recentCompletionsOverride? }`) -- omitted entirely by the live completion path (`achievementsStore.ts` still calls the original 5-arg form, confirmed unchanged and byte-for-byte identical in behavior), used only by `detectRetroactiveAchievements`' own replay loop. `completionCountsByTaskId` (a `Map<taskId, Map<dateString, count>>`, maintained incrementally -- one `O(1)` insert per event, not rebuilt from scratch) backs a new `isCompletedOnDate` helper that reproduces `isTaskCompleted`'s own exact formula (`count >= timesPerDay`) but reads the count from the map instead of rescanning, mirroring the same `buildCompletionCountsByDate` pattern (from `streaks.ts`) this app's calendar grids already use for the identical problem -- reused, not reinvented. `recentCompletionsOverride` is computed via a plain `O(window)` index-slice into `events` (the replay's own already-`completedAt`-sorted event list) rather than re-deriving and re-sorting the pool from the current task snapshots on every call.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (205/205, +2 new tests asserting the `options`-supplied and `options`-omitted code paths produce identical results for both perfect-day and early-bird, specifically to catch any future drift between the two). A throwaway timing script (8 tasks, ~150 days each, ~90% completion rate, ~1,078 total completion events -- a realistic stand-in for "8 tasks, a few months of data") completed the full retroactive scan in ~540ms, comfortably fast for a manual, occasional action. Not yet reconfirmed on a real device against the actual reported-slow dataset, but the fix directly targets the two mechanisms most likely to have caused it.

**Per-kind "mute this celebration" added (2026-08-12), addressing a real risk flagged during a design discussion: repeatable achievements (the low streak tiers especially) could realistically fire often enough on an unstable task to feel naggy, made worse by the celebration's own heavyweight full-screen, ~8-second, manually-dismissed nature.** Rather than touch detection frequency itself (which would mean weakening what these kinds actually celebrate), the fix is presentation-only: any repeatable kind's own celebration now offers a small, quiet "Show a quick alert next time instead" link at the bottom -- tapping it mutes that *kind* (not just this one task or this one instance) going forward, and every future unlock of it surfaces as a plain toast instead of the full takeover. One-time kinds never show the link at all (nothing to suppress -- they can only ever fire once), and neither does a locked card's own preview replay (muting something you haven't earned yet reads as confusing, not useful).

`achievementsStore.ts` gained a new persisted `mutedKinds: AchievementKind[]` array plus two actions: `muteKind(kind)` (idempotent -- muting an already-muted kind is a no-op, not a duplicate entry) and `unmuteAllKinds()`. Persistence needed no migration handling -- `partialize` just grew to include the new field, and since a pre-existing installed device's persisted blob simply won't have a `mutedKinds` key at all, Zustand's own default shallow-merge behavior (`{...currentState, ...persistedState}`) leaves the store's own initial `mutedKinds: []` default untouched rather than overwriting it with `undefined`. Muting is deliberately scoped to the *kind*, not the task or the specific instance -- the whole point is "I get this one often enough that the ceremony's stopped feeling special," which is a property of the achievement itself, not of any one earn of it. There's also deliberately no per-kind *unmute* UI anywhere -- undoing is a single all-or-nothing reset from Settings, not a list of individually-togglable kinds to manage, matching how narrowly-scoped this feature needs to be to solve the actual problem.

`AchievementCelebration.tsx`'s top-level component (`AchievementCelebration`, the one that decides whether/how to render a pending celebration) gained a second `useEffect` alongside its existing "celebrations disabled -> drain silently" one: when the pending achievement's kind is in `mutedKinds`, it shows a plain toast (`${meta.title} ${meta.describe(achievement)}`, e.g. "Streak Started! "Morning Run" started a 2-day streak") via `useToast()` and drains the queue the same way, rather than ever mounting the full `CelebrationContent`. Muting doesn't change what gets *recorded* at all -- `recordCompletionAchievements` still queues every earned achievement unconditionally regardless of mute state (matching how the `achievementCelebrationsEnabled` setting already works) -- it only changes how `AchievementCelebration` chooses to *present* a pending entry once it's already there.

The mute link itself (`CelebrationContent`'s own `handleMute`) lives inside the existing `metaBlock` RevealSlot, right after the unlock-date/history-list content, gated on `meta.repeatable && achievement.dedupScope !== 'preview'` (`showMuteLink`). Tapping it calls `muteKind`, shows its own distinct confirmation toast ("Got it — you'll get a quick alert for {title} from now on"), and calls the same `handleDismiss` the explicit close button uses -- acknowledging "stop celebrating this" is itself a form of dismissing the current one. Styled deliberately quiet (`muteLink`: small, `rgba(255,255,255,0.45)`, underlined) so it reads as a minor, easy-to-ignore option rather than competing with the celebration itself -- `metaBlock`'s own `RevealSlot` `minHeight` grew by a new `MUTE_LINK_HEIGHT` (32, an estimate in the same spirit as `TaskCard`'s own `ESTIMATED_BADGE_SIZE`) only when the link is actually shown, so layout stability holds regardless of which achievements do or don't get it.

**Settings' own restore path**: a new "Restore Full Celebrations" row in the existing Achievements section (`SettingsScreen.tsx`), directly below Trophy Case, showing a trailing count of currently-muted kinds -- and, per the same "nothing to reset, nothing to show" principle the free-tier task cap and other conditional UI in this app already follow, only rendered at all once `mutedAchievementCount > 0`. Tapping it calls `unmuteAllKinds()` and confirms via a plain toast, matching this screen's own established toast-confirmation pattern for settings actions.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (205/205, unaffected -- no test coverage exists or is expected for this component-level work per this project's stated testing scope). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth prioritizing: earning a repeatable achievement, muting it via the new link, confirming a *second* earn of that same kind shows the toast instead of the full screen, and confirming Settings' restore row appears/disappears correctly and actually brings the full celebration back.

**The celebration screen's bottom safe-area margin fixed (2026-08-12), per direct report ("doesn't have safe margin at the bottom to account for Android buttons").** `CelebrationContent`'s own `content` was always a plain, non-scrolling `View` -- it already carried `paddingBottom: insets.bottom + 24`, but that padding only helps if the content's own total height actually fits within the screen; a plain `View` doesn't clip or scroll when its content is taller than that, it just overflows past the screen's own edge. That's exactly what surfaced here: between a multi-row unlock-history list and the newly-added "show a quick alert instead" mute link (see this section's own entry just above), the total content height can now genuinely exceed the visible area on a shorter device, pushing whatever's at the bottom (potentially the new mute link) to render underneath Android's own nav bar/gesture area instead of respecting the inset.

**First attempt (same day): wrapped `content` in a `ScrollView`.** Functionally reasonable, but explicitly rejected per direct follow-up ("Eh, that's not really what I wanted. I want to just make sure that the entire layout fits within the view") -- the ask was for the screen to genuinely fit, not to become scrollable. Reverted in full: `content` is a plain `View` again (`flex: 1`, no `contentContainerStyle` split), the now-unneeded `contentScroll` style was deleted, and the `nestedScrollEnabled` prop/comment added to `UnlockHistoryList`'s own inner `ScrollView` (to fix the nested-same-direction-scroll conflict the outer `ScrollView` introduced) was removed along with it, since that conflict no longer exists once the outer wrapper is gone.

**Real fix: compact the layout's own fixed sizing instead.** Every constant contributing to the screen's worst-case total height (every reveal fully played out, a 3-row-capped unlock-history list, and the mute link all showing at once) was tightened: `content`'s own top/bottom padding beyond the safe-area insets (`+16`/`+12` -> `+8`/`+8`), its inter-section `gap` (18 -> 12), `titleMinHeight` (140/64 -> 116/48, with `bigNumber`/`eyebrow`/`unitCaption`/`plainTitle` font sizes trimmed slightly to match), the description `RevealSlot`'s `minHeight` (100 -> 80), `metaBlock`'s base `minHeight` (64 -> 48), `HISTORY_ROW_HEIGHT` (40 -> 32) and `HISTORY_MAX_VISIBLE_ROWS` (4 -> 3, so the history list's own worst-case height dropped from 160 to 96), and `MUTE_LINK_HEIGHT` (32 -> 24). This is a "much less likely to overflow" fix, not a mathematically guaranteed one -- React Native has no built-in way to force arbitrary content to shrink to fit an exact available height without either scrolling (rejected) or measuring-and-scaling at runtime (not attempted here). The single largest fixed dimension on this screen, the emblem's own `TROPHY_BADGE_STACK_SIZE` (260, shared with `TrophyBadge.tsx`'s own internal halo/ring/pulse sizing), was deliberately left untouched -- shrinking `emblemSlot`'s own reserved layout box below that value wouldn't actually shrink the emblem itself (it renders at its own fixed internal size regardless of what box wraps it), it would just make the badge visually overlap the title text below it. If overflow still shows up on a real device, that's the next lever to reach for -- either shrinking `TROPHY_BADGE_STACK_SIZE` itself (a bigger change, cascading into the halo/pulse-ring math documented at length elsewhere in this file) or genuinely measuring/scaling the layout at runtime.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (205/205, unaffected -- pure layout/sizing change, no logic touched). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth prioritizing given the prior ScrollView attempt was itself rejected sight-unseen from a stated preference, not a confirmed on-device measurement: confirm the celebration screen (ideally for an achievement with a full multi-row unlock history and the mute link both showing) now genuinely fits within the visible area above Android's own nav bar/gesture region without any content clipping or rendering underneath it, on the shortest real device available.

**Follow-up (2026-08-12), on-device: the unlock-history list bumped back up, and the mute link became a bell-icon toggle button under the close button, per direct user feedback ("the unlock history could be a bit bigger... instead of putting the link about quick alert, add an icon button toggle under the close button (smaller) that looks something like turn off notifications").**

`HISTORY_ROW_HEIGHT`/`HISTORY_MAX_VISIBLE_ROWS` (32/3, tightened in the immediately-prior compaction pass) went back up to 36/4 -- the room to do this without reopening that same overflow risk came directly from the second change: pulling the mute control entirely out of the in-flow `metaBlock` stack (which no longer needs to reserve `MUTE_LINK_HEIGHT`, deleted outright) and into a fixed top-right corner button instead.

**The mute control became a real on/off toggle, not a one-shot "mute and dismiss" action.** `achievementsStore.ts` gained `unmuteKind(kind)`, a direct counterpart to the existing `muteKind` (same idempotent-via-`.filter()` shape) -- previously the only way back was Settings' own all-or-nothing `unmuteAllKinds`, since the mute link's own one-way "mute, toast, close" design never needed a reverse single-kind path. `CelebrationContent` now reads `isMuted` (`mutedKinds.includes(achievement.kind)`) and its `handleToggleMute` branches on it -- unmute-and-toast-"celebrations are back" or mute-and-toast-"you'll get a quick alert instead" -- and, critically, no longer calls `handleDismiss()` either way: a persistent setting reads more naturally as something flippable in place than as an action bundled into closing the screen, which the icon-toggle framing (state reflected, not just an action taken) reinforces.

Rendered as `muteToggleButton` -- a smaller (32px vs. `closeButton`'s 40px) circular icon button, same translucent-white styling, positioned directly beneath the close button (`right: 20` vs. `closeButton`'s `right: 16`, centering the narrower button under the wider one; `top: insets.top + 60` clears the close button's own height plus a small gap) -- `bell-outline` when celebrations are on, `bell-off-outline` once muted (both confirmed present in the installed MaterialCommunityIcons glyph map before use). `accessibilityRole="switch"` + `accessibilityState={{ checked: isMuted }}`, matching this app's established convention for a real two-state toggle (see `TaskProgressIcon`'s pill toggles elsewhere in this doc) rather than `"button"`. Only rendered for repeatable kinds, never on a locked card's own preview replay (`achievement.dedupScope !== 'preview'`) -- unchanged eligibility logic from the old link, just renamed (`showMuteLink` -> `showMuteToggle`).

The old in-flow text link (`muteLink` style, its `Pressable`/`Text` JSX inside `metaBlock`) was deleted outright, not just relocated -- `metaBlock`'s `RevealSlot` `minHeight` simplified back to `48 + historyListHeight` with no mute-link term.

`SettingsScreen.tsx`'s own "Restore Full Celebrations" row and its surrounding comments (both the handler's and the JSX's) were updated to stop asserting "there's no per-kind unmute anywhere" -- now accurately describing itself as the bulk equivalent to the per-kind bell toggle each celebration screen offers, rather than the only way back.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (205/205, unaffected -- pure UI/store-action change, no logic in a tested `utils/` module touched). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth checking: the toggle button's icon/state flips correctly and immediately on tap, the two toast messages read clearly for their respective directions, the button doesn't visually collide with the close button above it or the badge/rings sweeping through that same top-right region at various points in the reveal sequence, and the now-larger unlock-history list (up to 4 rows at 36px each) still fits comfortably within the screen's own tightened vertical budget.

**Follow-up (2026-08-12), a real correction on the toggle button's own wording, plus two genuinely new behaviors, per direct user clarification ("It should not close the congratulations when you press the notification icon. Instead, it should toggle, and the toast should say that future unlocks of this achievement will not show congratulations... in EITHER case the trophy case lets you open any congratulations").** The "doesn't dismiss" half of the ask was already true of the toggle shipped in the entry just above (`handleToggleMute` never called `handleDismiss`) -- what actually needed fixing was the toast *wording* (didn't yet say "future unlocks" / "congratulations" in those terms) and two behaviors that had never been built: the muted-unlock toast showing an icon, and Trophy Case replays bypassing mute.

**1. Toggle toast wording**, `handleToggleMute` in `AchievementCelebration.tsx`: `You'll get the full celebration for X again.` / `Got it — you'll get a quick alert for X instead of the full celebration.` → `Future unlocks of "X" will show the full congratulations again.` / `Future unlocks of "X" won't show the full congratulations — just a quick alert.` -- matches the user's own literal phrasing and the app's own name for the feature ("congratulations," not "celebration," in every direct user quote throughout this whole feature's build).

**2. The muted-unlock toast (top-level `AchievementCelebration`, fires when a *live* unlock's kind is muted) now carries the achievement's own icon.** `ToastOptions` (`ToastContext.tsx`) gained optional `icon?: MaterialCommunityIconName` / `iconColor?: string` fields -- every other call site in the app omits both, so this stays a plain text banner everywhere else; `ToastBanner.tsx`'s `ToastContent` renders the icon (20px, `iconColor ?? '#fff'`) ahead of the message text when present. The muted-unlock effect now passes `icon: meta.icon, iconColor: meta.color.base`, so a quietly-muted unlock still reads as a recognizable, kind-colored moment rather than a generic notice -- this half of the ask ("we should still get a toast, with icon and message, on unlocks") was already true for the *message*, just missing the icon.

**3. "In either case the trophy case lets you open any congratulations"** needed a real mechanism, since the muted-kind gate (`mutedKinds.includes(pending.kind)`) previously applied unconditionally to whatever sat at the front of the queue -- including a deliberate Trophy Case replay (`queueCelebration`, tapping an unlocked card's own Play), which would have silently downgraded to the same quiet toast instead of showing the celebration the user explicitly asked to see. Fixed with a new not-persisted `forcedCelebrationIds: string[]` on `achievementsStore.ts`, populated only by `queueCelebration` (never by `recordCompletionAchievements`, so a real live unlock still respects mute exactly as before) and cleaned up by `dismissCurrentCelebration` (removing whichever id it just shifted off the front of the queue, so the set can't grow unboundedly across a session). `AchievementCelebration`'s top-level `isMuted` check now reads `mutedKinds.includes(pending.kind) && !isForced` -- a forced replay always wins over mute, satisfying "either case" (an unmuted or muted kind) the same way. No changes were needed at either `queueCelebration` call site (`TrophiesScreen.tsx`'s unlocked-card replay, and its locked-card preview via `buildPreviewAchievement`) -- both already funnel through the one store action this now covers generically.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (205/205, unaffected -- the one pre-existing `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` warning on `ToastBanner.tsx`'s own entrance-animation effect is untouched by this change, confirmed via `git diff` before treating it as a non-issue). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth checking: the muted-unlock toast's icon renders at a sensible size/position alongside the message text, the two toggle toasts read clearly for their respective directions, and -- the real behavioral fix here -- that tapping Play on an unlocked Trophy Case card for a kind you've muted still opens the full congratulations screen rather than downgrading to a toast.

**Follow-up (2026-08-12), a real z-index bug found by direct report ("When toggling the bell the toasts don't show above the congratulations message").** Root cause traced to `_layout.tsx`'s mounting order, not the toggle logic itself (which already worked correctly -- `showToast` fires, the toast state updates, `ToastBanner` genuinely re-renders with the new message): `<ToastBanner />` mounts before `<AchievementCelebration />` as siblings, and both hosts carried the *identical* `zIndex: 1000` / `elevation: 1000` -- at equal values, two absolutely-positioned siblings resolve stacking by paint order, so the later-mounted celebration screen's own opaque backdrop always painted over the toast host regardless of which one the toast was actually triggered by or when. This happened to look correct for the scenario this app's own docs originally describe ("the celebration screen naturally supersedes the bottom completion toast while it's showing") -- a plain completion toast fired moments before an achievement's own full-screen takeover reasonably reads as "the more important thing took over" -- but that was really just this same structural tie being resolved in a way that happened to match the desired outcome for *that one* case, not a deliberate design that also correctly covered a toast fired *from inside* the celebration screen itself (the mute toggle's own confirmation), which is exactly backwards: a toast triggered by interacting with the topmost screen should always be able to show on top of it.

Fixed by giving `ToastBanner`'s own `host` style a genuinely higher `zIndex`/`elevation` (2000, both platforms) than `AchievementCelebration`'s (1000) -- a toast now always wins the stacking order regardless of mount order or which screen triggered it, settling both cases the same principled way ("a toast is a transient acknowledgment layered on top of whatever's currently showing") rather than relying on an incidental tie-break. Documented directly in `host`'s own style comment, including the one acknowledged tradeoff: a plain completion toast that happens to still be showing at the exact moment an achievement's celebration takes over will now remain visible (peeking at the bottom) rather than being fully hidden behind the celebration's backdrop as before -- judged a reasonable, arguably better outcome (the toast's own Undo action stays reachable) rather than a regression worth guarding against separately.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (205/205, unaffected -- pure z-index/elevation value change, no logic touched). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth prioritizing given this is a real reported bug, not a preemptive fix: confirm the mute-toggle toast now genuinely renders above the congratulations screen's own tint/content, and take a look at the completion-toast-vs-celebration overlap case too (a task completion that immediately earns an achievement) to confirm the now-visible-toast-on-top result reads fine rather than cluttered.

**A muted kind's live-unlock notice moved off the shared toast system entirely, into a new dedicated top-anchored `AchievementAlert.tsx` (2026-08-12), per explicit user direction ("The achievement toast should be more of an alert that appears at the top").** The muted-unlock notice (added two entries above) had reused the app's plain, bottom-anchored `ToastBanner` -- fine for blending in with routine app toasts (task completed, settings changed), but the whole point of this notice is the opposite: it's standing in for a real achievement moment that's being deliberately downgraded, so it should read as its own distinct, achievement-flavored thing, not just another line in the same gray banner everything else uses. It should also live at the *top* of the screen -- matching this feature's own original pre-full-screen design intent (the very first plan for this feature, before it became a full-screen takeover, called for exactly this: "a top-anchored card... so both can coexist without fighting for space" against the bottom completion toast that so often fires at the very same moment a task is completed).

Rather than teach the shared `ToastContext`/`ToastBanner` a `position`/rich-content option for one caller (which would've meant threading an icon prop through a system every *other* toast in the app would never use -- exactly the icon/iconColor addition from two entries above, which is reverted here as a result), built a small standalone component instead: `AchievementAlert.tsx` mirrors `ToastBanner`'s own `ToastContent` shape closely (the same horizontal swipe-to-dismiss thresholds/gesture, a similar Reanimated entrance) but is entirely self-contained -- its own `useEffect`-driven auto-dismiss timer (4.5s, shorter than `ToastBanner`'s 8s default, since there's no action to read or tap here, just a moment to notice), its own top-anchored host (`top: insets.top + 12`, entrance `translateY` from *negative* -> 0, the mirror of `ToastBanner`'s rises-from-below), and its own card shape: an icon in a small circular badge tinted the achievement's own kind color, next to a bold title + a smaller muted description line -- reading as a real notification card rather than a plain text banner. `AchievementCelebration.tsx`'s top-level component now renders `<AchievementAlert>` in place of `<CelebrationContent>` whenever the pending achievement's kind is muted (both branches keyed by `pending.id`, so switching between achievements always gets a fresh mount/fresh timer rather than reusing a stale instance) -- the muted-branch `useEffect` that used to call the shared `showToast` directly was removed entirely, since the decision of *what* to render is now made once, in the render body, rather than as a side effect that then had to manually call `dismissCurrentCelebration()` itself.

`ToastOptions.icon`/`iconColor` (added specifically to back the now-replaced call) were reverted from `ToastContext.tsx`/`ToastBanner.tsx` outright, confirmed via a repo-wide grep that nothing else in the app had picked up the option in the meantime -- genuinely dead code once this moved, not something to leave "just in case" per this project's own stated bias against exactly that. `ToastBanner`'s own `host` zIndex fix from the entry above (2000, above `AchievementCelebration`'s 1000) is untouched and still relevant -- it's what keeps the mute-*toggle* toast (a separate, ordinary `showToast` call fired from the bell button, still routed through the plain shared toast system, not this new alert) visible above the congratulations screen; `AchievementAlert`'s own host sits at 1500, between the two, on the reasoning that it should sit above ordinary app content and the (mutually-exclusive, for a given pending item) full celebration takeover, but doesn't strictly need to out-rank the shared toast since the two never actually occupy the same screen region (one top-anchored, one bottom-anchored).

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (205/205, unaffected -- pure new-component + call-site change, no logic in a tested `utils/` module touched). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth prioritizing given this is a materially new UI element: the alert card's entrance/auto-dismiss/swipe-to-dismiss all work as expected, it reads clearly as "an achievement, downgraded" rather than a generic notice, it doesn't collide with anything else that might be near the top of the screen (status bar, a header back button on whatever screen is underneath), and that it and a simultaneously-showing bottom completion toast genuinely coexist without either one feeling cramped.

**A Trophy Case nav button added to `DashboardScreen`'s header (2026-08-12), per direct user request after asking about an unexplained empty circle there.** `DashboardHeader`'s `headerTop` row (back button / centered title / a same-sized element on the right, `justifyContent: 'space-between'`) had always used a plain, iconless `<View style={styles.headerButton} />` on the right purely to balance the back button so the title stayed visually centered -- a real layout element, not a bug, but with nothing in it. Replaced with a real `TouchableOpacity` (still the same `headerButton` style/size, so it fills the identical balancing role) navigating to `/trophies`, `trophy-outline` icon (matching `SettingsScreen`'s own Trophy Case row icon), `accessibilityLabel="Trophy Case"` / `accessibilityHint="Opens your trophy history"` (identical hint wording to that same Settings row, for consistency). This is now the Dashboard's own quick path to the Trophy Case, alongside the existing Settings row -- Settings' own row (with its trailing achievement count) is unchanged.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (205/205, unaffected -- pure additive nav button, no logic touched). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth a quick look that the trophy icon reads clearly at this size/position and that tapping it navigates correctly from Dashboard.

**Four smaller follow-ups landed together (2026-08-12), all per direct user direction during the same session.**

**1. The unlock-history list's generic "Overall" label for a global (taskless) instance replaced with that instance's own count stat, where one exists, or hidden entirely otherwise.** `AchievementCelebration.tsx`'s `UnlockHistoryList` used to fall back to a plain literal `'Overall'` whenever `a.taskName` was undefined -- said nothing the achievement's own icon/title didn't already convey. Now checks `ACHIEVEMENT_META[a.kind].ribbon.kind`: a `'count'`-ribbon kind (perfect-week, the three century-club-N tiers, habit-collector) shows that instance's own formatted stat via `getRibbonText(a)` (e.g. "7 DAYS", "1,000 DONE") -- the exact same formatting `TrophyBadge`'s own ribbon banner already uses, so this line and that banner never disagree; a `'fixed'`-ribbon kind (first-completion, perfect-day, early-bird, night-owl), whose ribbon text is a fixed word rather than a real count, renders nothing at all for that line, leaving just the date centered in the row on its own.

**2. `AchievementAlert.tsx`'s (the muted-kind top notification) description now colors and icons a task name inline, per direct request ("I want task names to be colored and with their task icon").** Previously used `meta.describe(achievement)`, a plain string with the task name embedded in quotes as flat text. Now mirrors `CelebrationContent`'s own `DescriptionText` technique exactly (confirmed to work since `MaterialCommunityIcons` is itself `Text`-based, so nesting it inside a parent `Text` works like nesting a styled `Text` span): when the achievement has a `taskName` and its kind has a `triggerSuffix`, renders the task's own icon + a bold, task-colored name span, followed by the plain suffix sentence; falls back to `triggerStandalone`/`describe` otherwise (taskless kinds, or a kind with no `triggerSuffix`). New `descriptionTaskName` style (`fontWeight: '800'`, color applied inline) added to this file, matching `AchievementCelebration.tsx`'s own identically-named style.

**3. A subtle "snoozed" (muted) indicator on Trophy Case cards, per direct request ("not interactive, just an indicator").** `TrophiesScreen.tsx`'s `AchievementBadgeCard` gained an `isMuted` prop (from a new `mutedKinds` selector at the screen level) and, for an unlocked+muted card, a small `bell-off-outline` icon rendered inline in the caption row right beside the unlock date (`captionRow`, `flexDirection: 'row'`) -- moved here (2026-08-12, same day) from an initial absolute corner-badge placement per direct follow-up ("Put the snooze badge next to the unlock date"), so the now-removed `snoozedBadge` style/position never shipped in its original spot. Deliberately not interactive (no `TouchableOpacity` of its own, `importantForAccessibility="no"` so it's not independently focusable inside the card's own tappable region -- the whole card's existing tap still replays/previews normally) and deliberately quiet (a plain small icon in `colors.textSecondary`, not a bold colored pill) so it reads as a footnote next to the date it's annotating, not a second competing badge.

**Follow-up (2026-08-13): the Settings row for bulk-restoring muted achievements renamed to match this same "snoozed" vocabulary, per direct user direction ("come up with a clearer more descriptive label for 'restore full congratulations' and use a different icon").** `SettingsScreen.tsx`'s row -- previously "Restore Full Celebrations" with a `party-popper` icon (the exact same icon already used one row above it, for the unrelated "Celebrate Achievements" toggle, which this rename also stops colliding with visually) -- is now **"Snoozed Achievements"** with a `bell-off-outline` icon, tying it directly to the Trophy Case's own "snoozed" indicator above and the bell iconography the celebration screen's own mute toggle already uses (`bell-outline`/`bell-off-outline`). The row's own framing flipped to match: it now reads as "here's what's currently snoozed" (with the trailing count already showing how many) rather than a bare action label, so `handleRestoreFullCelebrations`'s own toast text and code comments were reworded to match ("Every achievement is unsnoozed -- full congratulations are back."), though the handler's own name and behavior (`unmuteAllKinds()`) are unchanged. Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (210/210, unaffected -- pure copy/icon change).

**Corrected the same day, per direct user follow-up ("'Snoozed Achievements' makes me think tapping it will open a view which shows snoozed achievements, not that it will 'unsnooze' them all").** A fair catch -- a bare noun-phrase label reads as a navigation link to a filtered list, not a bulk action, especially next to a trailing count that could just as easily be describing "how many are in this view" as "how many this button affects." Relabeled to **"Unsnooze All Achievements"** -- a verb-led phrase that can't be mistaken for navigation, still keeps the same "snooze" vocabulary tying it to the Trophy Case indicator and celebration-screen toggle. Icon (`bell-off-outline`) and toast copy left unchanged -- only the ambiguous label itself was the problem. Verified via `tsc`/`eslint` (pure copy change, no jest impact).

**Naming resolved for good, 2026-08-13, after a real design discussion rather than another guess-and-correct cycle.** Prompted by a direct question about whether "Celebrate Achievements" (the global toggle) was even worth keeping, given it now overlaps in vocabulary with the separate per-kind snooze mechanic. Two things were established through that discussion, both worth recording since they resolve genuine ambiguity that had been building across several renames:

1. **What the global toggle actually controls, confirmed directly against the code rather than assumed.** `achievementCelebrationsEnabled` is read in exactly three places -- the celebration popup itself, the two Stats-screen showcases, and the Settings row -- and nowhere in `taskStore.ts`'s `completeTask` or `achievementsStore.ts`'s `recordCompletionAchievements`. Detection and recording to persisted history are **always unconditional**, regardless of this setting; turning it off only ever suppresses the popup and the Stats showcase, never tracking. The user had originally intended it to gate tracking entirely (partly hoping for a performance win) but agreed not to pursue that once the tradeoffs were laid out: the actual per-completion detection cost is negligible (simple arithmetic against already-computed `task.stats`, nothing like the real chart-rendering cost this app already found and fixed elsewhere), while gating tracking would create *permanent, unrecoverable* gaps for moment-dependent kinds (`comeback`, `perfect-day`, `perfect-week`, the time-of-day pair) that the retroactive scan can never backfill, undermining the Trophy Case's own standing "your history is always complete" guarantee. Given that, "Celebrate Achievements" turned out to be an accurate name after all -- it really is just controlling celebrations -- so it was kept as-is.

2. **"Unsnooze All Achievements" renamed to "Unsnooze All Celebrations."** The remaining confusion wasn't the global toggle's name, it was this row's: "achievement" as the object of "unsnooze" implies the *achievements themselves* were suppressed, when what's actually snoozed is the *celebration* treatment for that kind (the achievement is still tracked and shown in the Trophy Case regardless). Renaming to "Celebrations" makes the object match what's truly being toggled, and now mirrors "Celebrate Achievements" directly -- both centered on "celebration(s)" as the actual control surface, with "achievements" only ever naming the category they apply to.

**A third, related inconsistency was caught and fixed in the same pass**: the achievement-screen bell toggle's own live copy said "congratulations" (`AchievementCelebration.tsx`'s `handleToggleMute` toasts: "...will show the full congratulations again" / "...won't show the full congratulations"), a *third* word for the same underlying concept alongside "celebrate" and "snooze." Per explicit user confirmation ("yes, lets make it consistently 'celebration' not 'congratulations'"), every live-copy and paraphrased-comment occurrence of "congratulations" was swapped to "celebration": both `handleToggleMute` toast strings, `SettingsScreen.tsx`'s own `handleRestoreFullCelebrations` toast (now "Every celebration is unsnoozed -- the full celebration is back.") and its row's `accessibilityHint` (now "Turns the full celebration back on for everything you've snoozed" -- reworded from a direct find-replace to avoid saying "celebration" twice in one sentence), and one explanatory comment in `AchievementCard.tsx`. **Three occurrences were deliberately left untouched** -- one each in `achievementsStore.ts`, `AchievementCard.tsx`, and `AchievementCelebration.tsx` -- because each is a direct, verbatim quote from an earlier user request (e.g. "the trophy case lets you open any congratulations", "the congratulation screen"), and this codebase's own established convention (see this whole document's own "Follow-up" entries throughout) is to preserve historical quotes exactly as spoken rather than retroactively rewrite them to match current terminology.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (236/236, unaffected -- pure copy changes across four files, no logic touched).

**4. Century Club split from one 1,000-target kind into three literal tiers -- 100/500/1,000 -- per explicit user direction ("Add a 100 and 500 version of century club, appropriately named and styled").** The single kind id `century-club` was renamed `century-club-1000` (freeing "Century Club" to become the literal 100 tier's own name, rather than continuing to describe 1,000 idiomatically) and two new sibling kinds added: `century-club-100` ("Century Club!" -- now genuinely literal) and `century-club-500` ("500 Club!"), with the former top kind retitled "Millennium Club!" (also now literal). `AchievementKind`'s union, `ACHIEVEMENT_META`, and `ACHIEVEMENT_ORDER_INDEX` all updated accordingly; every kind-id rename follows this feature's own established precedent for renaming when it improves accuracy pre-launch (Topaz→Gilded, first-streak→first-completion, both documented earlier in this same section).

Styled as a **stone/monument** theme -- Marble (100) → Granite (500) → Obsidian (1,000, the original kind's own color set, unchanged) -- deliberately distinct from milestone-N's own metal-medal theme (a *per-task* progression) and from the gemstone/fire themes used elsewhere in the catalog, since a cross-task lifetime total reads more like an engraved monument than a medal. Marble's first draft (`base: '#ECEFF1', accent: '#37474F'`) turned out to be the exact same two colors as milestone-1000's own Platinum theme, just with the base/accent roles swapped -- caught via this codebase's own established practice of grepping the full palette for duplicate hex literals before finalizing a new theme, and replaced with a genuinely distinct warmer palette (`base: '#FAFAFA', glow: '#E0E0E0', accent: '#4E342E'`) before it shipped.

Mechanically, this is the same "add a tier of an existing pattern for free" property every other multi-tier family in this file already has: a new `TOTAL_COMPLETIONS_SUM_ENTRIES` derived list (`ACHIEVEMENT_KIND_ORDER` filtered to `progressStrategy.type === 'total-completions-sum'`, mirroring `FIXED_THRESHOLD_ENTRIES`'s own exact shape) replaced the single hardcoded `if (isFirstEarn('century-club', 'global'))` block with a generic loop over all three tiers -- `nextSum`/`sumDelta`/`prevSum` (the cross-task lifetime sum and this completion's own isolated contribution to it) are computed once outside the loop, since none of the three tiers' own crossing checks need to recompute them. `getAchievementCardStatus`'s existing `'total-completions-sum'` case needed no changes at all -- it was already generic over `strategy.target`, keyed off whichever kind's own status is being asked for.

6 tests updated/added in `__tests__/utils/achievements.test.ts`: the `ONE_TIME_KINDS` assertion now lists all three tiers; the original single-kind detection describe block became a 6-test describe covering each tier firing independently, none firing below the lowest threshold, and a single large jump correctly firing all three at once (two of these needed `toContain`/`not.toContain` rather than an exact-array `toEqual`, since a single task's own big jump also legitimately crosses that same task's own `milestone-100`/`milestone-1000` per-task tiers -- a real, harmless coincidence of the two families sharing several identical round-number targets, not a bug); the `getAchievementCardStatus` progress test now checks all three tiers' own independent caps from the same underlying task sum.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (210/210, up from 205 -- net +5 tests after accounting for the 3 new detection tests, 1 new progress-cap assertion folded into the existing test, and 2 pre-existing tests updated in place rather than added). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth a look at: the unlock-history list's new count-stat-or-hidden line for a few different global kinds, `AchievementAlert`'s inline task icon/color rendering cleanly at that small size, the snoozed indicator sitting legibly beside the date without crowding it, and the three Century Club tiers' new stone-themed colors (especially Marble's very light base, this catalog's only light-based theme besides comeback's own lighter accent) reading clearly on the Trophy Case grid and the full celebration screen alike.

**Follow-up (2026-08-13), a real correction on both fronts, per direct user feedback: "come up with a clever name for 500 Club, that name sucks" and "the color scheme of 1000 club is great, but 100 and 500 have serious contrast problems -- try something more consistent with 1000 club."**

**Naming**: `century-club-500`'s title changed from a flat "500 Club!" to "Fortune 500!" -- a deliberate pun on the real-world "Fortune 500" company list. Century (100) and Millennium (1,000) both work as titles because they're genuine English number-words; there's no equivalent clean word for "500," so rather than force one (quincentennial, half-millennium, etc. -- all considered and rejected as either obscure or clunky), borrowing an already-famous "500" and reframing it as *your own* fortune (of habit completions) reads as the more genuinely clever option. `numberBlock.eyebrow` and `flavorText` ("You just made your own Fortune 500.") updated to match; `describe()`/`triggerStandalone` (the factual copy) didn't need to change, since neither ever referenced the old name.

**Color, a real root-cause fix, not just a retouch**: the prior Marble (100)/Granite (500) themes were the *only* light-or-mid-toned bases in this 22-kind catalog, paired with `useAccentText` left unset -- which broke two things at once for exactly the reason "serious contrast problems" describes. First, every kind's ribbon banner (`TrophyBadge.tsx`/`TrophyEmblem`) fills with `base` and draws fixed **white** text on top, unconditionally -- Marble's `#FAFAFA` face made that white text nearly invisible, the single worst offender. Second, Granite's mid-grey `#757575` base sat right at the edge of comfortable contrast against both its own accent and the celebration screen's near-black backdrop. Obsidian (1,000) never had either problem, because it was already built the "right" way: dark base, bright accent, `useAccentText: true`.

Rather than re-tune the same light/mid bases again, both were rebuilt on Obsidian's own formula instead -- **Slate** (100, deep blue-grey `#263238` base, sky-blue `#40C4FF` accent) and **Basalt** (500, dark volcanic brown-black `#3E2723` base, molten-ember-orange `#FF6E40` accent), both now `useAccentText: true` like Obsidian. This isn't just a palette swap -- it also gives the trio a coherent geological story it didn't have before (common sedimentary/volcanic stone -> hardened volcanic rock -> volcanic glass, an "increasingly extreme/rare" progression), rather than three arbitrary shades of stone-grey. Verified numerically (the same WCAG relative-luminance contrast formula this codebase has used before for exactly this class of check, run directly rather than eyeballed): Slate's base-vs-accent is 6.62:1 and base-vs-white-ribbon-text is 13.16:1; Basalt's are 4.97:1 and 13.82:1; both comfortably clear WCAG AA's 4.5:1 threshold and land in the same range as Obsidian's own pairing (11.41:1 / 16.10:1), confirmed with no hex collisions against the rest of the palette.

No test changes needed -- none of `__tests__/utils/achievements.test.ts`'s coverage asserts on color values or title strings, only kind ids and numeric behavior, both unchanged here. Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (210/210, unaffected). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth a specifically careful look this time, given the prior color pass on this same trio was the one the user flagged as having "serious contrast problems": confirm the ribbon text is now clearly legible on both new themes, and that "Fortune 500!" reads clearly as a title at actual card/celebration-screen size.

**Follow-up (2026-08-13), two of the three theme names renamed again the same day, per direct user feedback: "Lets call Slate color theme Cobolt -- otherwise it's perfect. Basalt is meh, try something else."** `century-club-100`'s theme renamed `Slate` -> `Cobalt` with its own colors left completely untouched (`base: '#263238', glow: '#546E7A', accent: '#40C4FF'`) -- the user was explicit the color itself was already right, only the mineral-name label needed correcting.

`century-club-500`'s theme replaced outright, not just renamed -- `Basalt` (`#3E2723`/`#6D4C41`/`#FF6E40`, a dark volcanic brown-black) swapped for **Garnet** (`base: '#4A0E0E', glow: '#8B2635', accent: '#FFCA28'`, `useAccentText: true` retained), a deep-red gemstone base with a warm amber-gold accent (garnet is classically set in gold jewelry, the real-world pairing this borrows). Deliberately a genuinely different hue family, not just a re-tuned brown -- Cobalt already owns the cool blue-grey end of this trio, so Garnet's warm red gives the middle tier its own clearly distinct identity rather than reading as a slightly-different shade of the same idea. The original accent candidate (`#FFAB40`) collided with streak-5's own `glow`, and the next one tried (`#FFC107`) collided with streak-10's own `glow` -- both caught via this codebase's own established practice of grepping the full palette for duplicate hex literals before finalizing, landing on `#FFCA28` instead. Contrast verified the same numerical way as the entry above: base-vs-accent 10.09:1, base-vs-white-ribbon-text 15.44:1 -- both comfortably ahead of even Obsidian's own reference pairing.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (210/210, unaffected -- pure theme-name/hex-value changes, no logic or test coverage touched). Not yet confirmed on-device.

**Follow-up (2026-08-13), same day: `century-club-500`'s `flavorText` replaced, per direct user feedback ("I like the name Fortune 500 but the flavor text is a bit lame, give me some other options").** The original line ("You just made your own Fortune 500.") just restated the title back at the user rather than adding any real wit -- the same flaw this catalog's own flavor-text guidance has flagged before elsewhere (see the achievement-copy history above: flavor text should be "purely reactive/celebratory," not a mechanical echo of the achievement's own name/condition). Presented four options spanning different angles (a proverb riff, a corporate-IPO pun, an executive-title pun, a vivid business-press image) via `AskUserQuestion` rather than guessing; picked: **"Fortune favors the consistent."** -- a play on both the "fortune" in Fortune 500 and the old adage "fortune favors the bold." `describe()`/`triggerStandalone`/`numberBlock`/`ribbon` all untouched, only `flavorText` changed. Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (210/210, unaffected -- pure copy change).

**A performance-focused review of the congratulations screen and the achievement system (2026-08-13), per explicit user request, found and fixed three real inefficiencies.** Read through the full pipeline (`AchievementCelebration.tsx`, `TrophyBadge.tsx`, `Confetti.tsx`, `ParticleSystem.tsx`, `achievements.ts`'s detection engine, `taskStore.ts`, `achievementsStore.ts`, `TrophiesScreen.tsx`) rather than guessing at likely hotspots -- two findings turned out to be real, one (a Trophy Case progress-bar linear scan, the same class of issue as the third fix below but only running when that screen is actually opened, not a hot path) was flagged as low-priority and left alone per explicit user direction ("fix 1,2,3").

**1. The count-up re-rendered the entire celebration tree on every animation frame.** `useCountUp`'s `setDisplay` state lived directly in `CelebrationContent` (`const count = useCountUp(...)`), so every `requestAnimationFrame` tick -- up to ~60/sec for 1-3s (`getCountUpDuration`) -- re-rendered the *whole* tree, including `TrophyBadge` (3 frame rings, a halo, a shockwave, 3 pulse rings, a ribbon, sparkle particles) and `Confetti` (60 falling pieces), neither of which was wrapped in `React.memo` -- so React re-executed their full component bodies and re-diffed their entire subtrees on every single tick, even though nothing in either actually reads `count`. This window (roughly 1.6s-4.5s into the celebration) directly overlaps the screen's most animation-dense period. Important nuance: the underlying Reanimated animations were never at risk of glitching or restarting either way, since they run on the UI thread via shared values with mount-only effects -- this was purely wasted JS-thread reconciliation work, not a visual bug.

Fixed by wrapping `TrophyBadge`, `Confetti`, `DescriptionText`, and `UnlockHistoryList` in `React.memo` (plus the `.displayName` assignment this codebase's `react/display-name` lint rule requires for an anonymous `React.memo`-wrapped component, matching `ParticleSystem`/`ParticleComponent`'s own existing precedent) -- every prop across all four is either a primitive (strings/numbers) or a reference that's already stable across `CelebrationContent`'s own re-renders (`achievement` and `allAchievements` are both store-sourced references that only change when the underlying data actually does, unrelated to the count-up ticking), so this alone stops the cascade without needing to restructure where the count-up's own state lives.

**2. `TrophyBadge`'s `sparkleConfig` broke `ParticleSystem`'s own existing memoization.** `const sparkleConfig = getSparkleConfig(accent);` built a fresh object every render -- and since `ParticleSystem` is already `React.memo`'d, a new object reference for its `particles` prop always read as "changed" to that shallow comparison, regardless of whether the actual field values differed. Low-impact once fix #1 lands (`TrophyBadge` itself stops spuriously re-rendering in the first place), but fixed directly at the allocation site anyway with `useMemo(() => getSparkleConfig(accent), [accent])` -- a one-line belt-and-suspenders fix, and the `useMemo` import was already free to add alongside the existing `useCallback`/`useEffect`/`useState` imports.

**3. The live completion path never used the fast-lookup option `detectCompletionAchievements` already supports.** `detectCompletionAchievements` has an optional `completionCountsByTaskId` map for O(1) date lookups -- built specifically for `detectRetroactiveAchievements`' own chronological replay (see that feature's own performance-fix history above) -- but the **live** path (`taskStore.completeTask` -> `achievementsStore.recordCompletionAchievements` -> `detectCompletionAchievements`) never passed it. That meant `perfect-day`'s check (unconditional on *every* completion) and `perfect-week`'s (up to 12 more day-checks, whenever perfect-day happens to pass) fell back to `isTaskCompleted`'s plain linear `.find()` over a task's *entire* completions array from the start -- the worst-case pattern for finding a *recent* date in an array appended in chronological order. This ran on literally the single most frequent interaction in the whole app, with a cost that grows linearly with a task's lifetime completion count. Not yet visibly slow at realistic personal-tracker data sizes (a linear scan of even a couple thousand records is sub-millisecond in JS), but a real, easily-avoidable inefficiency that only gets worse the longer someone uses the app, fixed with machinery that already existed in the codebase -- it just wasn't wired into this one call site.

Fixed in `taskStore.completeTask`: once `prevTask`/`nextTask` are known, builds a `Map<taskId, Map<date, count>>` (via `buildCompletionCountsByDate`, already `streaks.ts`'s own established fix for this exact class of problem elsewhere -- calendar grids use it the same way), scoped to non-archived tasks only (the same universe `detectCompletionAchievements`' own perfect-day/perfect-week checks already restrict themselves to), and passes it through `recordCompletionAchievements`'s new optional `completionCountsByTaskId` parameter into `detectCompletionAchievements`'s existing `options` object. Building the map costs the same `O(total completions)` as the linear scans it replaces would have cost for a single lookup anyway -- the actual win is that every *additional* lookup within the same call (perfect-day's own up to 6, plus perfect-week's up to 12 more when it runs) becomes an O(1) map read instead of another full rescan, rather than paying that O(n) cost repeatedly. `recordCompletionAchievements`'s new parameter is optional and only `taskStore.ts`'s real call site supplies it -- nothing else calling it (there is no other call site) needed to change.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (210/210, unaffected across all three fixes -- pure re-render/memoization and hot-path-optimization changes, no behavior or test coverage touched). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth a look at whether the celebration screen's frame pacing feels any smoother during the count-up specifically (the fix that's most likely to be perceptible, if the re-render churn was ever actually costly enough to show up as jank rather than just wasted headroom), though neither this nor the completion-path fix change what's rendered or detected, only how much redundant work it takes to get there.

**Early Bird's icon changed (2026-08-13), per direct user feedback ("I don't love the Early Bird achievement icon choice. Give me a few alternatives").** Presented four options (a literal `bird`, an `alarm`, a `coffee` cup, and a more precise `sun-clock-outline`) via `AskUserQuestion`; the user asked for a plain sun instead ("How about a sun?"), landing on `weather-sunny` -- replacing `weather-sunset-up`. Chosen over the visually-similar `white-balance-sunny` for the more directly on-theme name, and it keeps Early Bird in the same `weather-*` icon family as Night Owl's own `weather-night`. Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (210/210, unaffected -- a single icon-name literal change).
1. **`TaskCalendarScreen`, `TaskStatsScreen`, and `TaskDetailScreen` all throw synchronously if the task isn't found.** All three do `if (!task) throw new Error('Missing task')` on first render. `tasks` loads asynchronously from AsyncStorage in `TaskContext`, so a hard page reload / direct deep link on web (or a cold app launch landing straight on one of these routes) hits this before `loadTasks()` resolves, showing a crash screen instead of a loading state. Reproducible by navigating straight to `/task-calendar?taskId=...` via a fresh page load rather than in-app navigation. This is also the exact mechanism behind a real, now-fixed crash (2026-08-07): `AddTaskScreen`'s Archive/Delete confirm used to call `router.back()`, which — when this screen was reached via `task-detail`'s pencil icon (a modal pushed on top of another modal) — only popped back to `task-detail`, still holding the just-deleted `taskId`; `TaskDetailScreen`'s own `if (!task) throw` then fired on that re-render. Fixed at the call site by using `router.dismissTo('/')` instead (pops every intermediate modal at once, landing directly on Home), but the underlying fragility in all three screens remains for any other path that could land on one with a stale/deleted `taskId` (e.g. a saved deep link). Fix: show a loading/`null` state while `tasks` is empty-but-still-loading (or the task is genuinely gone), rather than throwing.

(`calculateAggregateStats`'s completion-rate averaging bug, formerly item 2 here, was fixed 2026-08-13 -- see the `DashboardStatsView` entry above.)

2. **Streak-count badge lands one day early on `specific_days_of_week` calendars, reported directly (2026-08-13), not yet investigated.** A task due Sun/Mon/Wed/Fri with completions on Tue/Wed/Thu shows the correct current-streak count of 3 in `TaskHeader`'s status badge, but `buildDayConnectionInfo`'s calendar badge (`TaskCalendarScreen`'s Month view, `DashboardCalendarView`'s Timeline grid) shows a badge of 2 on Wed instead of 3 on Thu (today). Given `buildDayConnectionInfo` places its badge at each chain's own `endDate` (see this doc's own extensive "the calendar badges disagreed with the Streaks screen" history above for why it reads `chain.length`/`endDate` from `getTaskStreakChains` rather than counting locally), the likely cause is somewhere in `getDueDayStreakChains`'/`isFirstDueDayAfter`'s handling of today as a live, not-yet-closed chain for a `specific_days_of_week` task -- worth checking whether today (Thu, a non-due day for this task) is being excluded from the chain's own `endDate` even though `isChainConnectedDay`'s own "today" special-case (`streakStatus === 'up_to_date'`) is what's supposed to let a live streak's connector/badge reach into it.

**Congratulations screen follow-up (2026-08-13): the snooze/bell mute toggle's visibility condition was too narrow, per direct user correction ("it should appear for all tasks that can have multiple completions. This includes repeatable tasks and per-task achievements").** Previously gated on `meta.repeatable` alone -- correct for kinds like the streak-N ladder (the *same* task can re-earn them after a reset), but wrong for one-time, **task-scoped** kinds like `milestone-N`/`anniversary`: `repeatable: false` only means one specific task can't earn its own copy twice, not that the *kind* itself can never fire again -- a different qualifying task earns its own independent copy freely, so the toggle's "will you get this again" premise still applies, it was just being hidden. Fixed to `(meta.repeatable || meta.scope === 'task') && dedupScope !== 'preview'` -- the only kinds now excluded are ones that are both non-repeatable *and* global (i.e. `first-completion`, a genuine once-ever-for-the-whole-app moment with truly nothing left to suppress) plus preview instances (nothing real to mute). The stale comment above `handleToggleMute` (which had asserted "a one-time kind... can never fire again for anything," true for `first-completion` but not for the task-scoped one-time kinds) was corrected to spell out the real distinction.

**Trophy Case follow-up (2026-08-13): the "Not yet earned" caption on locked cards with no progress data removed, per direct user direction.** `AchievementBadgeCard`'s caption ternary (unlocked date → progress bar → "Ready — revive a lapsed streak" → "Not yet earned") had its final fallback branch changed to render nothing (`null`) instead of that text -- the dimmed badge, lock-icon overlay, and muted title (`cardTitleLocked`) already make "not earned yet" obvious without a redundant caption restating it.

**Trophy Case: filter by task (2026-08-13), closing out a standing `TODO.md` item.** A radio-style row of chips ("All" plus one icon chip per active task, reusing `DashboardScreen`'s own task-filter-bubble visual language and single-select `accessibilityRole="radio"` pattern rather than that screen's checkbox multi-select — a per-kind card only ever shows one task's own progress at a time regardless, so filtering to more than one task at once wouldn't narrow anything further) sits below the header, shown only once there's more than one active task (nothing to filter between otherwise). Deliberately implemented entirely at the screen layer — `achievements.ts`'s own `getGroupedAchievementCardStatuses`/`getAchievementCardStatus` needed zero signature or logic changes, matching this feature's own established "the pure engine doesn't need to know about UI-only concerns" boundary: `TrophiesScreen` pre-filters its two existing inputs before calling in — `achievements` down to just the selected task's own records (`a.taskId === selectedTask.id`; a global achievement's record has no `taskId` at all, so it's excluded the same way a genuinely different task's record would be), and `activeTasks` down to `[selectedTask]`, which naturally reshapes every "closest task" progress calculation into "this task's own progress" for free.

**Global-scoped kinds (`first-completion`, `perfect-day`, `perfect-week`, the three `century-club-N` tiers, `habit-collector`, `early-bird`, `night-owl`) are dropped from the grid entirely once a task filter is active**, rather than shown scoped-but-misleading — traced through each one's own progress strategy before deciding this: Century Club's `total-completions-sum` is a genuine cross-task sum, so computing it against just one task's own count would read as "this task alone needs to hit 1,000" when the real condition is a total across every task; Perfect Day/Perfect Week's `today-progress`/`perfect-day-streak` both require `PERFECT_DAY_MIN_DUE_TASKS` (2) tasks due at once, so scoped to a single task they could never show any progress at all; Habit Collector's `active-task-count` would always read a nonsensical flat `1/6`. Implemented as a post-filter on `getGroupedAchievementCardStatuses`'s own returned groups (`statuses.filter(s => ACHIEVEMENT_META[s.kind].scope === 'task')`, empty groups dropped) rather than touching the achievements engine itself.

`AchievementBadgeCard` gained a `hideTaskAttribution` boolean (true whenever a task filter is active) suppressing both the earner-icon row and a progress caption's `· {taskName}` suffix — once every card on screen already belongs to the one task named in the filter chip above, repeating that same name/icon on every single card read as pure noise. An empty state (`trophy-outline` + "No task-specific trophies yet for {name}") covers a freshly-filtered task with nothing task-scoped earned or in progress yet, rather than an empty screen with no explanation.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint` (no `utils/` logic changed, so the existing 210-test suite needed no updates and still passes as-is). Not yet confirmed on-device — worth a look at: the filter row only appearing with 2+ active tasks, selecting a task correctly narrowing to just that task's own unlocked/in-progress kinds with global kinds cleanly dropped, the empty state for a task with nothing yet, and that re-selecting the same chip toggles back to "All" rather than getting stuck.

**Achievements integrated onto the Stats screens (2026-08-13), closing out the last standing `TODO.md` sub-item under the achievements feature ("I want some achievements shown on the stats screen with link to trophy case for all, and include task-specific handling accordingly").** A new shared `app/components/AchievementsPreviewCard.tsx` — a compact, tappable card (unlocked badges as small kind-colored circles, the single closest not-yet-earned kind's own progress bar underneath, an unlocked/total count in the corner) sitting between the tertiary stat strip and the "Activity" chart section on both `TaskStatsView` and `DashboardStatsView`, right where `DashboardStatsView`'s own "Total Completions" per-task bar list already established the precedent of a plain-`View` (non-`react-native-chart-kit`) card in that slot. Reuses `achievements.ts`'s existing pure engine (`getGroupedAchievementCardStatuses`) directly rather than any new detection/progress logic — this is presentation only, over data the Trophy Case already computes the identical way. A new `isTaskScopedKind(kind)` export in `achievements.ts` (`ACHIEVEMENT_META[kind].scope === 'task'`) factors out the "drop global-scoped kinds like Century Club/Perfect Day, which aren't attributable to a single task" predicate `TrophiesScreen`'s own task filter had just introduced inline — both now call the one shared function instead of duplicating the same one-liner, closing off a drift risk before it could start.

**Task-specific handling, per the explicit ask**: `TaskStatsView` filters `achievements` down to just this task's own records (`a.taskId === task.id`) and passes a stable single-element `[task]` as `activeTasks`, with `taskScoped={true}` — so the card shows exactly this task's own earned badges and its own closest-next progress, nothing cross-task. Its "View Trophy Case" tap deep-links `router.push({pathname:'/trophies', params:{taskId: task.id}})`, reusing the very `taskId` param `TrophiesScreen` gained in the entry directly above — landing on the Trophy Case already filtered to this same task, not the full unfiltered grid. `TrophiesScreen`'s own `selectedTaskId` state now reads `useLocalSearchParams<{taskId?:string}>().taskId` as its initializer (a plain `useState` default, sufficient since a fresh `router.push` always mounts the screen fresh) instead of always starting at `null`.

**"For all," per the explicit ask, on the aggregate screen**: `DashboardStatsView` deliberately reads the *full* active task list via its own fresh `useTaskStore` selector (not the `tasks` prop this screen otherwise threads through everywhere else for its charts) and every recorded achievement, both completely unfiltered by Dashboard's own header task-selection bubbles — trophy status is a persistent, cross-session fact, not something that should visibly shrink just because a couple of filter chips happen to be unchecked for charting purposes right now. Its own "View Trophy Case" tap is a bare `router.push('/trophies')`, landing on the full unfiltered grid.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (219/219, unaffected — pure additive component + prop-threading, no `utils/` logic changed beyond the trivial `isTaskScopedKind` extraction, which follows this file's own established precedent of not adding dedicated test coverage for a one-line pass-through getter like `dedupKey`). Not yet confirmed on-device — worth a look at: the card's badge row/progress bar reading clearly at this compact size, the per-task card genuinely showing only that task's own achievements with no cross-task leakage, the aggregate card's counts staying stable regardless of Dashboard's task filter, and both "View Trophy Case" links landing on the right (filtered vs. unfiltered) grid.

**Follow-up, same day: redesigned to reuse Trophy Case's own dressed-up card, not a cheaper summary, per explicit user direction ("I want the achievement emblems in stats to be a little more dressed up like their counterparts in trophy case, including banner and title. Reformat the layout to accommodate 3 achievements spanning the width of the row").** The original preview (small flat kind-colored circles + one progress bar below) was its own separate, lighter visual language from the actual Trophy Case grid — reasonable as a first pass, but not what was asked for once directly compared against the real thing.

**Extraction, so the two surfaces can't visually drift apart.** `TrophyEmblem` (the halo/ring/shadow/ribbon emblem), `EarnerIconRow`, `buildPreviewAchievement`, and `AchievementBadgeCard` itself all moved out of `TrophiesScreen.tsx` into a new shared `app/components/AchievementCard.tsx` — genuinely self-contained now (its own `useThemeColors`/`useMemo`-computed `StyleSheet`, previously threaded down as a `styles` prop from the parent screen's own `createStyles`) so it can drop into either context unmodified. `AchievementBadgeCard`'s `cardWidth` prop went from required to optional: when a caller passes it (Trophy Case's own fixed-column grid, unchanged), the card gets an explicit pixel width exactly as before; when omitted, the card wraps in a new `flex: 1` container instead — the mechanism that lets `AchievementsPreviewCard` drop a small, variable-count row of these into a row and have them evenly divide whatever width is actually available, with no upfront measurement needed. `TrophiesScreen.tsx` itself shrank accordingly (its own `createStyles` lost every style key that moved with the card: `card`, `badgeWrap`, `badgeCircle`, `lockBadge`, `countBadge`/`countBadgeText`, `cardTitle`/`cardTitleLocked`, `cardCaption`/`captionRow`/`cardCaptionReady`, `progressBlock`/`progressTrack`/`progressFill`, `earnerRow`/`earnerIcon`/`earnerOverflow`/`earnerOverflowText`) and now just imports `AchievementBadgeCard` from the shared file, passing `cardWidth` as it always did.

**Layout, per the explicit "3 achievements spanning the width of the row" ask.** `AchievementsPreviewCard` now renders up to `PREVIEW_SLOT_COUNT` (3) full `AchievementBadgeCard`s in a `flexDirection: 'row'` with each one `flex: 1` (via the `cardWidth`-omitted path above), replacing the old badge-circle row + separate progress block entirely. Selection: one slot is always reserved for the closest not-yet-earned kind (`nextUp`, the `locked` group's own first entry — already sorted in-progress-first by closeness, so no new sorting needed) whenever anything is left to work toward, with the remaining slots filled by the most-recently-unlocked kinds (`unlocked`, already newest-first). A task/screen with fewer than 3 things to show (e.g. one earned trophy and nothing yet in progress) still renders correctly — however many cards actually exist just divide the row's width evenly among themselves, rather than the layout expecting exactly 3.

**The "Next" label**, per direct same-turn follow-up ("Also I think we need a 'Next' label above the progress"): `AchievementBadgeCard` gained an optional `topLabel?: string` prop -- a small uppercase eyebrow line rendered above the card, matching this app's own established small-caps label language (e.g. `TrophiesScreen`'s section headers). Trophy Case's own grid never passes it (every card there is an equal peer, no distinction needed); `AchievementsPreviewCard` passes `topLabel="Next"` only on the one reserved `nextUp` slot, so it's clear at a glance which of the (up to) 3 cards is "what to work toward" versus "what you've already earned."

Since each mini-card now individually reuses `AchievementBadgeCard`'s own tap behavior (replay an unlocked kind's celebration, or preview a locked one in dev builds), the row itself is no longer the tap target for "View Trophy Case" -- that moved to its own dedicated header row (title + unlocked/total count + chevron, still a `TouchableOpacity` calling `onViewAll`), so tapping an individual emblem and tapping "view everything" are now two distinct, non-conflicting gestures rather than one tap target trying to do both.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (219/219, unaffected -- pure component extraction + a UI-layer redesign, no `utils/` logic touched). Not yet confirmed on-device -- this is the more novel of the two passes and worth prioritizing: a 3-per-row card is meaningfully narrower than Trophy Case's own minimum 2-column grid, and `TrophyEmblem`'s halo/ribbon were tuned at that wider width -- confirm the emblem, ribbon banner, and title text all still read clearly (not clipped or overlapping a neighbor) at this narrower size, that the "Next" label doesn't feel disconnected from the card it labels, and that tapping an individual card vs. the header's "view all" row both do the right thing.

**Follow-up, same day: the per-achievement card chrome removed entirely, per explicit user direction ("The achievements don't need a card/border around them").** `AchievementCard.tsx`'s own `card` style dropped `backgroundColor`/`borderRadius`/`borderWidth`/`borderColor` (and `padding` shrank from 12 to a plain `paddingVertical: 8`) -- since `AchievementBadgeCard` is the single shared component both Trophy Case's own grid and the Stats screens' `AchievementsPreviewCard` render, this is a genuine, deliberate change to *both* surfaces, not scoped to just one. Matches this app's own established "no card chrome" bias elsewhere (`TaskStatsView`'s stat blocks are the original precedent for exactly this call) -- the dressed-up emblem already reads as a self-contained object via its own halo/ring/shadow/ribbon, so a bordered rectangle around the whole card on top of that was redundant weight; grid/row spacing (Trophy Case's own `row`/`listContent` gap, `AchievementsPreviewCard`'s `cardRow` gap) now carries all the separation between cards instead of an individual border per card. On the Stats screens specifically this also resolves a "card nested inside a card" look, since `AchievementsPreviewCard` itself is still its own bordered box -- each of the (up to 3) achievement cards inside it no longer double up on that same border treatment.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (219/219, unaffected -- pure styling removal, no logic touched).

**Follow-up, same day: the "Next" slot demoted from a full dressed-up card to a compact bar underneath the card row, per explicit user direction ("I want the stats achievement showcase to show up to 3 achievements, and the Next be a simplified row/bar below them").** Previously `AchievementsPreviewCard` reserved one of its 3 card slots for the closest not-yet-earned kind, rendered via the same full `AchievementBadgeCard` (emblem/ribbon/title) as an unlocked entry, distinguished only by a small `topLabel="Next"` eyebrow above it. Reworked so the card row now shows *only* unlocked kinds (up to `MAX_VISIBLE_CARDS`, still 3, still most-recently-earned first) -- `nextUp` no longer takes a card slot at all, or competes with unlocked kinds for one of the three.

In its place, a new `nextBlock` -- a plain icon + "Next: {title}" label + current/target count row, with a thin progress bar underneath (or the existing "Ready — revive a lapsed streak" text for `comeback`'s own readiness case, no numeric progress) -- renders below the card row, separated by a hairline `borderTopWidth` divider (`nextBlockSpaced`, only applied when the card row actually rendered above it -- a task/screen with nothing unlocked yet just shows the Next bar alone, no orphaned divider). This is essentially the *first* design this whole preview card went through, before the "dress it up like Trophy Case" follow-up -- now applied specifically to the one thing (an in-progress, not-yet-earned kind) that never needed the full emblem treatment to communicate "how close," where a compact bar reads more clearly than another full card competing for the same visual weight as the genuinely-earned ones beside it.

`AchievementBadgeCard`'s own `topLabel` prop (added specifically for the "Next" card's eyebrow label) is now unused by every caller -- removed from `AchievementCard.tsx` entirely (prop, destructure, render, and its own now-orphaned `topLabel` style) rather than left as dead API surface.

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (219/219, unaffected -- pure layout/logic change scoped to this one component plus a clean prop removal from its shared dependency, no `utils/` logic touched). Not yet confirmed on-device -- worth a look at: the card row now genuinely capping at 3 *unlocked* cards (not counting Next), the compact Next bar reading clearly against the plain unlocked cards above it, and the hairline divider only appearing when there's actually a card row above it to separate from.

**Follow-up, same day: the header's "view all" indicator changed from an `unlocked/total` fraction to an overflow-style `+N`, per explicit user direction ("The link to see all achievements currently shows x/y but it should be more like +N").** `unlocked.length}/{totalCount}` read as a progress fraction, which is what the Trophy Case's own header subtitle ("N of M unlocked") already communicates -- redundant with, and easy to conflate with, what this smaller header row was actually for (a link out, not a second progress readout). Replaced with `remainingCount = totalCount - visibleUnlocked.length` -- everything the Trophy Case has beyond what's already visible in this preview, whether that's further unlocked kinds past the top 3 or still-locked ones, since either is equally "more to see" once you actually tap through. Rendered as `+{remainingCount}`, the same "+N overflow" language `AchievementCard.tsx`'s own `earnerOverflow` chip and `TrophiesScreen`'s `EarnerIconRow` already use elsewhere in this feature for exactly the same "there's more than fits here" idea -- and omitted entirely once there's nothing left to reveal (a small task/fresh install where every achievable kind already fits in the 3-card preview), rather than showing a meaningless "+0".

Verified via `tsc`/`eslint`/`jest` (219/219, unaffected -- pure display-value change, no logic touched).
